<result># Review: entireio/cli#2131 (DRAFT) — `perf(checkpoint): batch persistent session writes with CAS`

## Verdict for a draft

**Continue, but rebase onto #1926 — do not land #2131's CAS layer independently.** The direction is right and the code quality is high; the problem is that ~40% of this PR is a second, independent implementation of the fix #1926 already ships, and #2131's version is missing two protections #1926 has.

Review worktree: `/private/tmp/claude-501/.../scratchpad/rev-2131` (PR head `4da261a76` + `origin/main` merged).

## Build / lint / test actuals

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | **pass** (exit 0) |
| `mise run lint` | **pass** — golangci-lint 2.11.3, **0 issues**; gofmt/gomod/shellcheck clean |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/... ./cmd/entire/cli/strategy/... ./cmd/entire/cli/ ./api/...` | **pass**, exit 0 (checkpoint 27s, strategy 133s, cli 167s) |

Fork CI hasn't run; locally this draft is green. Nothing below is a build/lint/test failure — all findings are from reading and from one repro test I wrote and then deleted.

---

## 1. Collision with #1926 — CONFIRMED, near-total

They are two implementations of the same fix. Every hunk conflicts.

| Function | #1926 | #2131 |
|---|---|---|
| ref-write primitive | **adds** `updatePersistentRef` + `casUpdateRef` (`persistent_ref_update.go`, new) | **adds** `RunRefTransaction` + `CompareAndSwapRef` (`ref_transaction.go:66,127`, new) |
| `GitStore.setPrimaryRef` | rewrites callers | **deletes** (`store.go`, -9) |
| `gitRefsStore.setRef` / `refBase` / `refBaseForBackfill` / `refTip` | rewrites | **deletes all four** (`refs_store.go`, -163) |
| `backfillAttribution` / `backfillSummary` / `backfillTranscript` (both stores) | rewrites each | rewrites each |
| `MigrateBranchToRefs` | rewrites | rewrites (`migrate.go`) |
| `casUpdateShadowBranchRef` | -43 (extracts to `casUpdateRef`) | -48 (extracts to `CompareAndSwapRef`) |

**#2131's primitive is better in one respect, worse in two.**

Better: `RefMutation` takes `current` as a parameter (`ref_transaction.go:78` reads the tip, hands it to the mutation), so a mutation structurally *cannot* forget to re-read. #1926's `persistentRefBuilder func() (new, expected, err)` relies on the closure remembering to re-read.

Worse — two things #1926 has that #2131 dropped:

- **No cross-process flock.** #1926 adds `withPersistentRefFlock` (`entire-persistent-ref-locks/`), mirroring merged prior art `withShadowBranchFlock` (`checkpoint/shadow_ref.go:140`). #2131 is pure optimistic CAS. On the shadow path the flock makes CAS conflict the *rare* case; here it's the common case, and each conflict costs a full checkpoint-tree rebuild. On a 500-session checkpoint (the size #2131's own `TestBatchSessions_CheckpointContentVisitationIsLinear` uses) with several hooks contending, the 16-attempt budget (`ref_transaction.go:22`) can be exhausted and the write hard-fails.
- **No loose-object cleanup after a losing attempt.** `ref_transaction.go:103–110`: on `ErrRefConflict` the just-created commit + trees stay in the object store forever. `tryDeleteLooseObject` already exists (`shadow_ref.go:116`) and is used by the merged ephemeral path (`ephemeral.go:163,342`); #1926 calls it. **CONFIRMED**, easy fix.

**Recommended order: #1926 first, then rebase #2131 on top.** #1926 is not a draft, is scoped to the bug, closes a filed data-loss issue, and carries the cross-process regression test (`integration_test/concurrent_checkpoint_writers_test.go`, +194) that #2131 has no equivalent of. On rebase, #2131 should keep #1926's flock + cleanup and contribute only (a) the `mutate(current)` signature and (b) the batch builder.

## 2. Does #2131 preserve the #1917 fix? — Yes for the ref race, but it opens a *different* data-loss hole

**Ref CAS: correct.** Every persistent ref update goes through `CompareAndSwapRef` with a real expected-old; `git.PlainOpen`-free; `.Storer.SetReference` / `.Storer.CheckAndSetReference` are gone from `checkpoint/` and `strategy/` (verified by grep, and by the PR's own `persistent_ref_inventory_test.go`).

**Retry rebuilds, does not re-point.** `writeBatchSessions` (`persistent_batch.go:255,296`) calls `prepareBatchSessions` *once* (session subtrees and task subtrees are content-addressed and parent-independent, so reuse is sound), then rebuilds the **checkpoint root tree and commit from the fresh parent on every attempt** via `applyPreparedBatch` + `createCommitAt`. This is the correct shape, not the re-point antipattern `shadow_ref.go` warns about. Same for all four backfills.

### 🔴 HIGH — CONFIRMED: a merged production guard is deleted, and the shape it guarded now silently loses a session

`persistent.go:454 writeStandardCheckpointEntries` contains a hard refusal (`"refusing to overwrite session 0 of checkpoint %s"`) plus a WARN tripwire, added for *an unreproduced production report of session 0 being silently replaced by a different session's data*. #2131 deletes `applySessionWrite`, which was its only production caller. It is now reachable **only from `persistent_tripwire_test.go:80`** — the test stays green while guarding nothing.

The live path, `applyPreparedBatch`, does not carry the guard. `persistent_batch.go:365–384`:

```go
if existingSummary != nil {
    for index := range existingSummary.Sessions { ... }   // drives indexBySession
}
nextSessionIndex := len(sessionPaths)                     // 0 when Sessions is empty
```

If the root summary's `Sessions` is empty/truncated while the tree still holds `0/`, the loop never runs, the incoming session is assigned index 0, and `rootEntries["0"]` is overwritten wholesale.

I confirmed this with a throwaway test against `applyPreparedBatch` (since deleted from the worktree):

```
resulting session dirs: map[0:session-new]
DATA LOSS: session-old was silently dropped
```

On `main` the same input **refuses**: `findSessionIndex` (`persistent.go:781`) returns `len(existingSummary.Sessions)` = 0, `sessionIndex == 0`, session-0 metadata holds a different ID → error. So this is a straight regression of a guard that exists precisely because this shape was seen in production.

Fix: port the session-0 refusal into `applyPreparedBatch`, or (better) derive `indexBySession` from the actual numbered subtrees present in `rootEntries` rather than from `existingSummary.Sessions`, and refuse when the two disagree.

### 🟠 MEDIUM — CONFIRMED: task-subtree rewrite drops previously-stored files

`mergePreparedTasks` (`persistent_batch.go:469`) replaces the whole `tasks/&lt;tool-use-id&gt;` subtree with the prepared one. `prepareTaskSubtree` builds only `task.json` plus `agent-&lt;AgentID&gt;.jsonl` *when a transcript is present* (`persistent.go:398`). On `main`, `flattenExisting` + `writeTaskRecordEntry` merged at the *file* level, so an existing `agent-*.jsonl` survived a later transcript-less write. Now it is dropped. Also drops the old blob if `AgentID` changes between writes. Narrow (needs a repeat write of the same tool-use ID) but real.

## 3. `CheckAndSetReference`? — No. It **removes** the two remaining uses. 👍

This is the best thing in the PR. `manual_commit_opf_refs.go:169` and `manual_commit_opf_rewrite.go` were both using go-git's `Storer.CheckAndSetReference` — exactly what `checkpoint/shadow_ref.go:65` says does not interoperate with native git's `.lock` files. #2131 converts both to `checkpoint.CompareAndSwapRef` and keeps OPF's deliberate single-attempt fail-closed semantics. `manual_commit_rewind.go:512` also moves off unconditional `SetReference`. Verified: zero `CheckAndSetReference`/`Storer.SetReference` remain in `checkpoint/` or `strategy/` production code.

Nit: `persistent_ref_inventory_test.go` enforces this by scanning two hardcoded directories for byte strings. The repo already has the idiomatic mechanism — a `forbidigo` rule in `.golangci.yaml` (used for `worktree.Status()`). Use that instead; it covers the whole module and can't drift.

## 4. Batching + crash safety — atomic per backend; the risk moves to the (unwritten) lifecycle layer

Per Git backend a batch is **one tree, one commit, one CAS ref update** → genuinely atomic. A kill mid-batch leaves only unreferenced loose objects; no partial checkpoint is ever visible. Correct.

Two things to flag anyway:

- **Batching converts "partial progress preserved" into "all or nothing."** Today, if a session-end hook is killed after session 3 of 8, sessions 1–3 are already durable. Post-batch, nothing is. Given issue #2035 (killed session-end hooks), that's *more* data lost per kill, not less. The PR body's "Left for the next PR" item 3 ("persist enough intent to recover after a process crash") is the mitigation — it should be designed **before** the lifecycle switch, not after.
- **No transaction across physical backends.** `fanoutStore.Write` (primary then best-effort mirrors) and `kindRoutingStore.Write` (dual-write on a migrated copy) can leave backend A with the batch and backend B without. The PR body states this honestly; it needs to become a comment on the caller contract, not just PR prose.

## 5. Observable-semantics changes

Batching is **not wired to any caller** — no production code constructs `BatchSessions` (only `fsstore.go:138`'s single-session adapter). So there is no ordering change to `TurnCheckpointIDs` or session linkage today. Other deltas:

- 🟡 **`writeSingleSessionViaBatch` (`persistent_batch.go:246`) splits a single write into two commits + two CAS updates** when `opts.CombinedAttribution != nil`, where `main` folded it into one commit (`persistent.go:674`). Latent only — nothing assigns `WriteOptions.CombinedAttribution` in production today (attribution arrives as its own `CheckpointAttribution` request). Still: it's a 2× ref-write on a perf PR's own hot path, and a crash window between the two. Fold it back into the batch commit.
- 🟡 **`strictProbe` asymmetry** (`routing_store.go:217,273`): a transient read error probing the read-preferred backend is *tolerated* for `ReservedSession` but *fatal* for `BatchSessions`. Same data, different failure mode based on how the caller happened to bundle it. Justify or unify.
- 🟡 **Corrupt root `metadata.json` becomes permanently unwritable.** `applyPreparedBatch` hard-errors on unparseable root summary; `main` logged Debug and continued. Fail-closed is the right instinct here, but there is no repair path.
- ⚪ `summary.Strategy` / `Branch` now resolve last-wins over sorted session IDs instead of taking the incoming write's value; `FilesTouched` ordering changes (set + `sort.Strings` vs `mergeFilesTouched`). Cosmetic, but a stored-format diff.
- ⚪ Batch commits emit **multiple `Entire-Session:` trailers** and omit `Entire-Strategy` when sessions disagree. Verified safe: nothing reads trailers off `entire/checkpoints/v1` commits (`sessionTrailerRegex` only parses shadow/user commits).

## Perf claim

**Not substantiated for this PR, by construction.** The 73–103 ms/session figure is quoted from issue #2091 (prior measurement of the existing code), not a result here. The PR states plainly that PostCommit still writes one session at a time and "the full `process_sessions` latency reduction is not active." The only measurement added is `TestBatchSessions_CheckpointContentVisitationIsLinear`, which counts tree-entry visits (4N+1) — a complexity assertion, not a latency one. That's honest, but it means the PR title's `perf(checkpoint)` currently describes an intention. Ask for a before/after on the real `process_sessions` path in the follow-up PR that wires it up.

## Split recommendation

2530/903 across 36 files is 3–4 PRs. Suggested cut, in landing order:

1. **Drop entirely** — the CAS conversion (`ref_transaction.go`, `persistent.go`, `refs_store.go`, `migrate.go`, `shadow_ref.go`, `store.go`, most of `strategy/`). This is #1926's territory. ≈ **-1100 lines**.
2. **Land separately and immediately** — `CheckAndSetReference` → native CAS for OPF (`manual_commit_opf_refs.go`, `manual_commit_opf_rewrite.go`) + `manual_commit_rewind.go`. Small, self-contained, fixes a real interop bug, no dependency on batching. Add the `forbidigo` rule here instead of the lexical inventory test.
3. **Land separately** — the `dispatchPersistentWrite` / `persistentWriteBackend` de-duplication and `classifyWriteRequest` exhaustive routing (`persistent_write.go`, `routing_store.go`). Pure refactor, reviewable on its own, independent of both #1926 and batching.
4. **Keep as the real PR** — `persistent_batch.go` + tests + `api/checkpoint/interfaces.go` + `fanout.go` + `fsstore`. ≈ 900 lines and reviewable, **with the session-0 guard restored**.

Then the lifecycle wiring as PR 5, with measurements.

## What's genuinely good

Fair credit — this is well above the usual drive-by contribution:

- **The retry-rebuild boundary is right.** Preparing content-addressed session/task subtrees once outside the loop and rebuilding only the parent-dependent root tree + commit inside it is exactly the correct decomposition, and it's the thing that's easiest to get wrong.
- **Removing the last two `CheckAndSetReference` calls** is a real bug fix nobody asked for, and it correctly preserves OPF's fail-closed-on-conflict semantics rather than blindly retrying a stale privacy rewrite.
- **`RefMutation(current)`** is a better API shape than #1926's zero-arg builder — it makes "retry must re-read" structurally enforced rather than conventional. Worth carrying forward into the rebase.
- Deterministic-by-construction batch output: `CanonicalizeBatchSessions` sorts by SessionID, shares one truncated-to-second UTC instant, and is called *once* at the routing/fanout boundary so mirrors produce identical commit hashes. The reverse-order-produces-same-tree test is the right test to have written.
- `classifyWriteRequest` closing the sealed union so an unknown request errors instead of silently inheriting create routing — that's tightening exactly the failure mode the old `backfillTarget` comment warned about.
- Comment density and quality match the house style; lint is clean at 0 issues; `t.Parallel()` used throughout the new tests; the PR body is unusually candid about what is and isn't done.</result>

# Review: entireio/cli#2052 — `fix(codex): use repository hook root in linked worktrees`

## Verdict: **needs-restructuring** (do not merge as-is)

One-line reason: the PR replaces `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` with a strict in-process Git-layout classifier that **hard-errors on four real-world Git layouts**, silently disabling all session capture for every agent in those repos — including the exact `.bare` layout issue #2020 asks to support.

## Build / lint / test (all run locally on PR head `ef7d44727`, already merged with current `origin/main` `6561a64b8`)

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | **PASS** (exit 0) |
| `mise run lint` | **PASS** — golangci-lint `0 issues`, gofmt/shellcheck/gomod clean (26.6s) |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/agent/codex/... ./cmd/entire/cli/gitrepo/... ./cmd/entire/cli/paths/... ./cmd/entire/cli/session/...` | **PASS** (4/4 packages) |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/...` | **PASS** — exit 0, **62 packages ok**, 0 FAIL, 0 panics |
| Integration (`//go:build integration`) | **not run** — requires `npm i -g @openai/codex@0.149.0` |

The test suite is green. Every finding below was reproduced by building the PR binary and a `origin/main` baseline binary and running them side by side against real `git`-created layouts.

---

## Findings (most severe first)

### 1. CONFIRMED — BLOCKER. Session state resolution hard-fails in 4 real Git layouts; failure is silent and affects all agents

`cmd/entire/cli/session/state.go:1121-1127` swaps `getGitCommonDir` from a `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` subprocess to `gitrepo.ResolveGitLayout(ctx)`, and propagates the classifier's error. `gitrepo.ResolveGitLayout` is far stricter than `git` and returns an error — not a fallback — for layouts `git` resolves fine.

`session.GetGitCommonDir` is the root of `.git/entire-sessions/`. Its 17 non-test callers include `strategy/session_state.go:69,821`, `strategy/checkpoint_sync_capture.go:38,46`, `strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go:852`, `strategy/cleanup.go:460`, `settings/settings.go:779`, `trail_context_cache.go:167,419`, `investigate/*`, `review/marker_fallback.go`. When it errors, none of them work.

Reproduced with real `git`, PR binary vs `origin/main` binary. Baseline works in every case:

| Layout (created by real `git`) | `origin/main` | PR |
|---|---|---|
| normal checkout | ok | ok |
| conventional linked worktree | ok | ok |
| `.bare` container + worktree (**issue #2020's own repro**) | ok | **state store fails** |
| `git clone --bare foo.git` + `git --git-dir=foo.git worktree add` | ok | **state store fails** |
| `git init --separate-git-dir=…` + `git worktree add` | ok | **state store fails** |
| `.git` is a symlink | ok | **state store fails** |

Error sites in `cmd/entire/cli/gitrepo/layout.go`:
- **:184-190** — `.bare` branch computes `MainWorktreeRoot = filepath.Dir(commonDir)` then requires `rootOwnsGitDir(container, .bare)`, which reads `&lt;container&gt;/.git`. Real `git` never creates that file in a `.bare` layout, so this **always** errors: `bare-worktree container "…/layout" does not own common Git directory "…/layout/.bare"`. The `GitLayoutBareWorktree` branch is effectively unreachable for the canonical layout.
- **:163-165** — `filepath.Base(commonDir) != ".git"` → error. Breaks every bare clone not literally named `.git` (i.e. the conventional `foo.git`).
- **:146-152** — `GitDirLayoutUnrecognized` + a `commondir` file → `"separate Git directory has an unexpected commondir file"`. Breaks separate-git-dir + worktree.
- **:98-100** — `.git` symlink → unconditional error.

**Failure scenario (end-to-end, verified):** user in a `.bare` layout runs `entire enable --agent claude-code`. Output ends with `Ready.` `entire status` prints `● Enabled · branch main` with **no warning**. The SessionStart hook prints its normal `Entire CLI will link this conversation to your next commit.` promise and exits 0. Meanwhile `.entire/logs/entire.log` has a lone `WARN failed to update session state on start … failed to create state store`. **No session state, no checkpoints, ever — and nothing tells the user.** The only surface is `entire doctor`, which emits an unformatted trailing line: `failed to list session states: failed to create state store: …`.

This is strictly worse than the bug being fixed: #2020 loses Codex sessions in one layout; this loses **all agents'** sessions in four layouts, silently.

**Fix direction:** `session.getGitCommonDir` must not be gated on the strict classifier. Either keep the `rev-parse` resolution for the common dir and use `ResolveGitLayout` only for the Codex discovery question, or have the classifier return `GitLayoutUnresolved` **with a populated `CommonDir` and no error** so callers that only need the common dir keep working while hook discovery degrades to "unresolved".

### 2. CONFIRMED — the layout test fixture is non-representative, which is why CI is green

`cmd/entire/cli/gitrepo/layout_test.go:56` hand-writes a `.git` pointer file real `git` does not create:

```go
require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(containerRoot, ".git"), []byte("gitdir: ./.bare\n"), 0o600))
```

That single line is what makes `rootOwnsGitDir` succeed and the `.bare` subtest pass. Remove it — reproducing the issue's own repro script verbatim — and the test fails. Similarly, the "bare repository named `.git`" subtest (`:65-82`) picks the one bare-directory name that survives the `:163` base-name check, missing the conventional `foo.git`.

There is also **no test anywhere** asserting that `session.GetGitCommonDir` still resolves in a non-normal layout, which is why a change to that function's error behavior passed 62 packages.

### 3. CONFIRMED — the PR does not fix the reported bug; it only diagnoses it

Verified on a fresh repo with hooks enabled **only** in the linked worktree: `entire enable --agent codex` writes `linked/.codex/hooks.json`, creates nothing in the primary checkout, prints `Installed 7 hooks for Codex` and `Ready.` — no warning at the moment the user makes the mistake. Codex still discovers nothing. The user must separately run `entire status` or `entire doctor` to learn it, then hand-fix by cherry-picking or re-running `enable` from the primary.

This is a deliberate, well-argued design choice in the PR body (don't take ownership of another checkout), but it directly contradicts issue #2020's "Suggested fix" and "Migration behavior" sections, which ask for install-to-authoritative-location plus migration of legacy worktree-local entries. **A maintainer needs to sign off on this scope reversal before any code review matters.** At minimum, `entire enable` should emit the warning inline rather than printing `Ready.`

Note also that the SessionStart warning path (`lifecycle.go:188-191`) is structurally unreachable for the primary failure mode: if only the linked worktree has hooks, Codex never loads them, so the SessionStart hook never runs to deliver the warning.

### 4. CONFIRMED — dead abstraction: `HookInstallationSkipError` has zero implementors

`cmd/entire/cli/agent/agent.go:164-169` defines the interface. `grep` across `cmd/` and `e2e/` finds exactly one other reference — the `errors.As` consumer at `setup.go:1674`. No agent returns an error satisfying it. Consequently `hookSetupResult.skipped` is always nil, `writeHookSetupSkipped` (`setup.go:1711`) always returns false, and the `hookSetup.skipped != nil` guard in `setupAgentHooksNonInteractive` is dead. No test exercises the path. ~60 lines of unwired machinery.

One reachable side effect survives: `setup.go:1257-1259` now returns `errors.New("cannot enable Entire: no agent hooks were installed")` whenever `agents` is empty in `runEnableInteractive` — previously that succeeded. That is a real behavior change hidden inside dead scaffolding.

Also `setup.go:429` — `hookAgentOptions(_ context.Context, …)` adds an ignored parameter threaded through three call sites for no purpose.

### 5. CONFIRMED — orphaned `.codex/hooks.json.lock` pollutes the working tree forever

`hooks.go:82,128` create `&lt;checkout&gt;/.codex/hooks.json.lock` via `flock`. `origin/main` uses no lock file at all. Nothing ever removes it:
- After `entire agent remove codex`, `.codex/` contains **only** `hooks.json.lock` — the hooks file is gone, the lock remains.
- `entire clean -f` does not touch it.
- Once the user follows the PR's own doctor remedy (`Commit .codex/hooks.json`), `git status` shows `?? .codex/hooks.json.lock` permanently.

Either place the lock outside the working tree, unlink it on release, or add it to the repo's ignore guidance.

### 6. CONFIRMED — contradictory `doctor` output

`doctor.go` writes the `Codex hooks: CURRENT-WORKTREE FILE NOT DISCOVERED` block and then falls through (no `return`) into the `✓ Codex hooks: INSTALLED` branch. Observed output puts a red-flag header two lines above a green checkmark:

```
Codex hooks: CURRENT-WORKTREE FILE NOT DISCOVERED
  … changes to this worktree's file do not affect it.
  Commit .codex/hooks.json and apply that commit to the primary checkout,
✓ Codex hooks: INSTALLED
```

Defensible internally (the *discovered* file is installed), but it reads as "broken / fine".

### 7. SUSPECTED — `OutdatedHookAgents` scope widened, unrelated to this PR

`config.go:168` changes iteration from `GetAgentsWithHooksInstalled(ctx)` to `agent.List()`. Every registered agent's `CheckHookConfig` now runs on every `entire status`. `HooksAbsent` filtering contains the blast radius, but this adds per-agent filesystem work to a hot path and widens drift warnings to agents the user never enabled. The comment rewrite acknowledges it; it belongs in its own PR.

### 8. SUSPECTED — CI now hard-depends on a pinned preview npm package

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` adds `npm install --global @openai/codex@0.149.0` plus `ENTIRE_TEST_REQUIRE_CODEX_APP_SERVER: "1"` to **every** integration shard. When OpenAI yanks or reshapes 0.149.0, all integration shards hard-fail (by design — `requirePinnedCodexAppServer` calls `t.Fatal`). Consider a single dedicated job rather than every shard, and decide whether version drift should block merges repo-wide.

### 9. Minor / stale

- `trail_checkout_worktree.go:57` comment still says "session.GetGitCommonDir is not reused here because it returns relative…" — the new implementation returns a canonical absolute path (an unremarked behavior change in itself; `EvalSymlinks` is now applied, so `/tmp` → `/private/tmp`).
- `gitrepo/layout.go:43-46` — the layout cache is process-global with no invalidation except `ClearGitLayoutCache`. `TestResolveGitLayout_MemoizesByWorktreeRoot` (`:138-150`) explicitly asserts stale-tolerance by renaming `.git` away. Harmless for short-lived CLI runs; a `git worktree repair` during a long-lived `entire mcp` server would be served a stale layout.
- **CLAUDE.md / AGENTS.md not updated.** Only `cmd/entire/cli/agent/codex/AGENT.md` changed. CLAUDE.md documents both the `gitrepo` open/status invariants and `.git/entire-sessions/` semantics; this PR changes how the common dir is resolved for every caller and adds a whole new shared classifier. That needs a CLAUDE.md entry.

## Invariant compliance (checked, all clean)

- ✅ No `git.PlainOpen` / `git.Open` outside `gitrepo` introduced.
- ✅ No direct `worktree.Status()` calls.
- ✅ No `os.Getwd()` misuse for git-relative paths. The one new `os.Getwd()` (`hook_root.go:134`) is a `//nolint:forbidigo` fallback for non-repo/bootstrap dirs — legitimate.
- ✅ Test isolation is good: `testutil.InitRepo`, `testutil.GitIsolatedEnv()`, `CODEX_HOME=t.TempDir()`. Nothing touches the real `~/.config/entire`, `~/.cache/entire`, or keyring.
- ✅ `t.Parallel()` used consistently across all new test files.
- ✅ Logging uses `logging.*`; user-facing output uses `cmd.OutOrStdout()`/`ErrOrStderr()`. No user content logged.
- ✅ `agent/architecture_test.go` allowlist correctly extended for the new `gitrepo` and `testutil` imports.

## Split recommendation

4,700 lines is not justified — this is five independent changes. Suggested landing order:

1. **`paths.ParseWorktreeGitDir` + `readStableGitFile` hardening** (`paths/worktree.go`, `gitrepo/repository.go`) — ~110 lines, pure refactor, no behavior change. Lands today.
2. **`gitrepo.ResolveGitLayout` classifier** (`gitrepo/layout.go`, `layout_test.go`) — new, unconsumed. Land with fixture bugs from Finding 2 fixed and `.bare`/`foo.git`/symlink/separate-gitdir cases classified rather than errored.
3. **`session.GetGitCommonDir` migration onto the classifier** — ~96 lines. **Only after #2 provides a non-erroring `CommonDir` for every layout `git rev-parse` handles.** This is the piece carrying the blocker; it should land alone with its own regression tests so a break is attributable.
4. **Codex discovery + diagnostics** (`codex/*`, `codex_hook_diagnostics.go`, `doctor.go`, `status.go`, `lifecycle.go`) — the actual PR subject, ~2,700 lines. Land after the product decision in Finding 3.
5. **`HookInstallationSkipError` / `removableAgentHookNames` / `OutdatedHookAgents` refactor** (`agent.go`, `setup.go`, `config.go`) — drop entirely (Finding 4) or land separately with a real implementor and tests.

The pinned integration test + CI change can ride with #4 or go standalone.

## What's genuinely good

This is unusually high-quality work for an external contribution, and the diagnosis is better than the fix.

- **The investigation is excellent.** Issue #2020 is one of the best bug reports in this repo — it isolates the Codex-side cause (openai/codex#21969), supplies two runnable repros, distinguishes "not discovered" from "not trusted", and explicitly rules out three adjacent prior fixes.
- **The integration test is the highest-value artifact here.** `TestCodexAppServerHooksList_LinkedWorktreeUsesPrimaryCheckout` pins the real Codex contract over stdio JSONL with no model call, no credentials, isolated `CODEX_HOME`, and a negative control. It documents a mechanism nobody had written down: **the linked worktree needs its own `.codex/` directory to establish the project layer before Codex will read the primary's `hooks.json`.** That belongs in the codebase regardless of the rest of this PR.
- **The path-role separation (`WorktreeHooksPath` vs `DiscoveredHooksPath`) is the right abstraction.** Making the write target a distinct type from the read target, with `HookDiscovery` deliberately carrying no write/lock/migration path, is exactly how you prevent a future refactor from quietly turning a diagnostic into a mutation.
- **Refusing to guess is correct.** Returning "unresolved" for layouts whose Codex behavior isn't pinned, rather than fabricating a writable fallback, is the right instinct — the bug in Finding 1 is that "unresolved" was wired to a hard error on a shared code path, not that the concept is wrong.
- **Write hardening is thorough and well-motivated**: symlink refusal, destination containment, 1 MiB cap with an overflow byte, `os.SameFile` identity checks before and after read, permission-bit preservation, preservation of unknown events and unrelated top-level fields, uninstall touching only Entire-managed entries.
- **Comment quality is high** — the `core` flag rationale in `managedHooks`, the PascalCase-vs-snake_case Codex quirk, and the `AreHooksInstalled` explanation all say *why*, matching this repo's house style.
- Lint is clean on first submission and every new test file uses `t.Parallel()` and the canonical `testutil` fixtures — the contributor clearly read CLAUDE.md.

The blocker is one wiring decision (routing `session.GetGitCommonDir` through a classifier that errors), not a flaw in the approach. Findings 1 and 2 are mechanical to fix; Finding 3 needs a maintainer product call before more review time goes in.</result>

<result># Review: entireio/cli#2099 — "fix(claude-code): capture Stop transcripts as validated snapshots"

## Verdict: **needs-restructuring**

The core idea (an Entire-owned, fingerprint-validated Stop snapshot) is sound and the race it closes is real — but the PR converts Claude checkpoint capture from fail-open to hard fail-closed on a producer-format string equality check, and I reproduced **two scenarios that silently lose a turn's checkpoint on the PR and succeed on `main`**. Split and re-land the capture core with degraded-mode fallback.

## Build / lint / test (run locally on PR head `d602d8a61` merged with `origin/main`)

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | PASS (exit 0) |
| `mise run lint` | **`[lint:go] 0 issues.`** (first attempt aborted on a concurrent golangci-lint; re-run clean) |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/agent/... ./cmd/entire/cli/strategy/... ./cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/... ./cmd/entire/cli/session/...` | PASS (20 packages ok) |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/` | PASS (147s) |
| `mise run test:integration` | PASS — 531 tests, 3 skipped, 112s |

Worktree left at `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-soph--herdr-worktrees-cli-worktree-brave-harbor-f520/a1bf9ce6-b287-4738-8a50-d4eae7ca9da4/scratchpad/rev-2099` (PR + main merged, tree clean).

The suite is green because the tests were adapted to the new contract, not because the contract is safe. Note `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/hooks_test.go` had to gain `session.TranscriptBuilder.WriteToFile(...)` before the prompt hook and switch `SimulateUserPromptSubmit` → `...WithTranscriptPath` — that edit *is* the regression, encoded as a test fixture.

---

## Findings (most severe first)

### 1. CONFIRMED — First turn of a new session loses its checkpoint entirely
`cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/claude.go:288-290` + `cmd/entire/cli/state.go:145-153` + `cmd/entire/cli/lifecycle.go:748-756`

`GetTranscriptPosition` now returns `ErrTranscriptNotReady` for a missing file instead of `(0, nil)`. `CapturePrePromptState` swallows that as a warning and leaves `TranscriptPositionCaptured=false`. At Stop, a modern payload with `!TranscriptPositionCaptured` hard-errors before anything is captured, copied, or saved.

Claude creates the session `.jsonl` lazily, so on the **first** `UserPromptSubmit` of a fresh session the file typically does not exist yet — which is exactly why the deleted branch existed (`// Returns 0 if the file doesn't exist or is empty`).

Reproduced with a probe against the real hook binary (probe removed afterward):

```
Output: transcript not ready: final response supplied without a turn-start transcript position
--- FAIL: TestReviewProbe_FirstTurnNoTranscriptAtPromptTime (1.28s)
```
The identical scenario **passes on `origin/main`**. Blast radius: exit-1 Stop hook (user-visible error in Claude), no shadow checkpoint, session stuck in `ACTIVE`, no retry path — Claude fires Stop once. `TestHandleLifecycleTurnEnd_ModernCaptureRejectsUnmeasuredTurnStartPosition` locks this in as intended, so it's a design choice to reverse, not an oversight.

### 2. CONFIRMED — Exact-equality on `last_assistant_message` fails closed on ordinary producer-format variation
`cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/transcript_capture.go:158-162`, `:259-279`, `:295-321`

`finalAssistant != *request.FinalResponse` is byte equality between Claude's field and a reconstruction that (a) only looks at the **last** `type:"assistant"` record, (b) joins in-record text blocks with `"\n"`, (c) `TrimSpace`s the reconstruction but not the payload. Any mismatch → poll to `maxWait` → `timed out after 3s` → same total loss as #1, plus 3s added to every Stop.

Three probes, all fail closed:

```
multi-record same-id final message -&gt; err=transcript not ready: timed out after 200ms
trailing-newline final response    -&gt; err=transcript not ready: timed out after 200ms
blank-line-joined blocks           -&gt; err=transcript not ready: timed out after 200ms
```

The multi-record case is not hypothetical: this repo's own `transcript/compact/compact.go:255` exists precisely because Claude writes **"consecutive assistant entries with the same message ID"** as separate JSONL lines, which compaction merges. `validateTranscriptSnapshot` deliberately implements the in-record join and *not* the cross-record merge, so a final message split across lines can never match. A single trailing `\n` in the payload is equally fatal. SUSPECTED only in *how often* Claude emits these shapes; CONFIRMED in that any of them is unrecoverable.

**Fix direction for 1+2:** capture failure should degrade, not abort — fall back to the legacy quiet-window read + `recordCaptureDegraded(ctx, sessionID, true)` (the mechanism already exists and is already used for the status-budget skip). Losing the snapshot guarantee for one turn beats losing the turn. Also compare with `strings.TrimSpace` on both sides and accept a suffix/prefix match.

### 3. CONFIRMED — Stale-transcript threshold is now fail-closed
`transcript_capture.go:77-79`, `:110-114`

`time.Since(observed.modTime) &gt; 2m` used to *skip the wait and proceed*; it now aborts the whole Stop. Any clock skew, NFS/mtime weirdness, or crashed-then-resumed agent turns a degraded capture into no capture. Same one-line fix as above.

### 4. CONFIRMED — `TurnEndRefreshFailed` permanently pins a shadow branch
`cmd/entire/cli/lifecycle.go:2140-2145`, `strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go:2966-2970`, `strategy/cleanup.go:244`

`sealPendingTurnAtSessionEnd` deliberately keeps `TurnCheckpointIDs` when the last refresh failed, and `protectedShadowBranchForSession` requires `len(TurnCheckpointIDs)==0` to reclaim. After `SessionEnd` nothing can ever clear them (`InitializeSession` only runs on a new prompt for that session ID). The shadow branch — shared per base-commit+worktree, so it pins other sessions too — is protected from `entire clean` forever. Needs a release path (retry at session end, or a TTL/age-based override in cleanup).

### 5. SUSPECTED — Bundled, unrelated subagent-discovery rewrite
`cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/transcript.go:409-471`

`AnalyzeTranscriptTurn` replaces `ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs(fullParsed)` with an `os.ReadDir` glob of `agent-*.jsonl`. `paths.SubagentsDir` is session-scoped so this is probably equivalent-or-better and it does remove a full second parse — but it changes *which* subagents are attributed to a checkpoint (any file in the dir, referenced or not) and it silently orphans `ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs` in `claudecode` (now test-only; `factoryaidroid` keeps its own copy). This is a standalone perf change riding along with a correctness change.

### 6. CONFIRMED — Required docs not updated
`git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD | grep '\.md$'` → **none**. CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md mandate keeping `docs/architecture/sessions-and-checkpoints.md` current on strategy behavior change. Stale now: `docs/architecture/checkpoint-scenarios.md` (documents `TurnCheckpointIDs` being cleared at turn end — no longer true for Claude) and `docs/architecture/agent-guide.md` (documents `PrepareTranscript` as *the* Stop readiness mechanism; three new capabilities `TranscriptCapturer` / `TranscriptTurnAnalyzer` / `RepeatableTurnEnd` are undocumented).

### 7. Minor
- `agent/event.go:107-113` — `FinalResponsePresent` and `StopHookActive` are parsed and asserted in tests but **never read by production code**. Dead fields; drop or use.
- `lifecycle.go:830-834` + `manual_commit_hooks.go:2995-2998` — two defensive `bytes.Clone`s of the snapshot. On the PR's own 70 MiB benchmark case that's +140 MB peak in a hook process versus ~70 MB before. The lifecycle clone guards `SanitizeTranscriptForStorage`, which for Claude is identity.
- `transcript_capture_test.go:131` — `TestCaptureTranscript_LegacyStableUsesQuietWindow` calls the exported `CaptureTranscript` with defaults, so it sleeps the real 500 ms quiet window. Use `fastCaptureConfig`.
- Token scoping drift: in the captured path `tokenUsage` comes from `analyzedTokenUsage` computed at `transcriptOffset` (`lifecycle.go:906`), while `StepTranscriptStart` metadata stays `transcriptLinesAtStart` (`:1074`). Identical when `preState` is captured (the only path that reaches it today), but the two can diverge if the guard is ever relaxed.

### Verified clean against the review checklist
- **Ordering (sanitize → externalize → redact)**: untouched on all three paths. Stop/shadow write still sanitizes pre-write (`lifecycle.go:836`); finalize still sanitizes then delegates to `redactSessionTranscript` (`manual_commit_hooks.go:3131`).
- **`CheckpointTranscriptSize` / `TranscriptSizeBaseline`**: not referenced anywhere in the diff.
- **Coordinates**: `TranscriptSnapshot.Position` counts newline-terminated lines plus a trailing unterminated line — byte-identical semantics to `GetTranscriptPosition` and to `SliceFromLine`. No compact/raw confusion; `compact_transcript_start` untouched.
- **Dynamic path resolution**: no path stored in checkpoint metadata; non-capturers keep `resolveTranscriptPath`.
- **Agent transcript never modified**: capture is `os.Open`/read-only.
- **Privacy**: no user content logged; all logging via `logging.*`.
- **Concurrency**: no new shared-file or shared-state hazard. Capture state (`rejected`, `observed`) is per-call; `TurnEndPending` lives in the lock-guarded per-session state file; multi-session commits stay session-scoped in the checkpoint store.
- **Test hygiene**: `t.Parallel()` used consistently, `t.Chdir` cases correctly opted out with a comment, `testutil.InitRepo`, `t.TempDir()` throughout.

---

## Split recommendation

2,280 lines is at least six independent changes. Land in this order:

1. **TurnStart pre-prompt invalidation** (`lifecycle.go:565-568`) — small, standalone bugfix, no dependency on the rest.
2. **Sentinel removal + `PrepareTranscript` simplification** (`claudecode/lifecycle.go`) — pure deletion of dead sentinel logic; ~200 lines removed.
3. **`TranscriptTurnAnalyzer` single-parse refactor + subagent directory discovery** — perf-only, independently benchmarkable, independently revertable.
4. **`TranscriptPositionCaptured` + `GetTranscriptPosition` missing-file contract** — tiny but load-bearing; needs its own review of every consumer.
5. **Owned/validated Stop snapshot** — the core, *with* degraded-mode fallback (findings 1–3) rather than hard failure.
6. **Repeatable Stop / `TurnEndPending` / `TurnEndRefreshFailed` / `sealPendingTurnAtSessionEnd`** — an orthogonal session-state-machine change with its own leak to fix (finding 4).

Pieces 1–3 look mergeable close to as-is.

## What's genuinely good

This is careful, senior-level work and the diagnosis is correct: `TranscriptPreparer` returning only an `error` really did leave lifecycle and finalization free to read different bytes than the ones readiness blessed, and nobody had named that. Specific things worth crediting:

- The fingerprint → read → re-verify-both-handle-and-path sequence in `readObservedTranscript` (`:203-231`) is the right shape, and the PR is honest about its one blind spot (same-size rewrite within one mtime tick) instead of overclaiming.
- Threading `capturedTranscript` explicitly through `HandleTurnEnd`/`finalizeAllTurnCheckpoints` rather than letting finalize re-derive a path is exactly the ownership fix the problem calls for, and `readTranscriptForFinalize` preserves the non-capturer path (Cursor's flat→nested relocation) rather than breaking it.
- The immediate-first-check trick (seeding a buffered channel before switching to `ticker.C`) removes a guaranteed 50 ms from every healthy Stop, and `TestCaptureTranscript_ModernReadyUsesSemanticEvidenceWithoutPollingDelay` proves it by setting `pollInterval = time.Hour`.
- The rejected-fingerprint memo (`:125-127`, `:154-156`) avoids re-reading and re-scanning an unchanged 70 MiB file every 50 ms — a real problem that a naive poll loop would have.
- `optionalString` correctly distinguishes omitted from explicit `null`, which is the right primitive for a legacy/modern payload split.
- Test coverage of the *mechanism* is strong: truncation, replacement, same-size rewrite, cancellation, mid-write pause, no-trailing-newline, matching-response-before-boundary, and a genuinely clever integration test that mutates the live source mid-Stop and asserts the owned copy is unaffected. The gap is coverage of *environmental* failure (missing transcript at prompt time, producer-format drift), not of the happy path.
- Benchmarks with a documented methodology and an explicit "Kept out of this PR" section — better PR discipline than most internal changes get.</result>

<result># Review: entireio/cli#2053 — `refactor(jsonutil): centralize streaming atomic replacement`

**Verdict: merge-after-fixes** — behavior is preserved on the OpenCode path (I verified the subtle fd-passthrough by experiment), but the PR silently changes `WriteFileAtomic` semantics on Windows for 11 unrelated call sites, and it deletes the documentation + test for the one invariant the original OpenCode code existed to protect.

Note: #1877 (the stated dependency) merged 2026-08-24, so this is now reviewable directly against main.

## Verification actuals

Worktree: `/private/tmp/.../scratchpad/rev-2053` at `pr-2053-rev` merged with `origin/main` (clean merge, no conflicts today).

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | exit 0 |
| `GOOS=windows go build ./...` | exit 0 |
| `GOOS=windows go vet` (jsonutil, opencode) | exit 0 |
| `mise run lint` | **0 issues** (golangci-lint 2.11.3) |
| `go test -race -count=1` jsonutil, opencode, cli, checkpoint/…, strategy/…, settings, investigate/… | **all ok** (cli 209.7s, strategy 140.8s, checkpoint 30.6s) |
| `mise run test:integration` | **530 tests, 3 skipped, 0 failures** (112.8s) |
| `mise run test:e2e:canary` | Vogon **56/56**, Roger-Roger **4/4** |
| `mise run dup` on PR | **886** dupl findings |
| `mise run dup` on `origin/main` (6561a64b8) | **886** dupl findings |

**The centralization removes exactly zero measured duplication.** Production code is net **+84 lines** (284 ins / 200 del) to serve one call site; tests are +738.

## Findings (most severe first)

### 1. CONFIRMED — `WriteFileAtomic` silently gains Windows rename-retry for 11 unrelated callers
`cmd/entire/cli/jsonutil/write.go:95-98` (config), `:130-145` (`defaultAtomicWriteOps` unconditionally wires `isRenameContention` + `wait`), `:232` (`renameWithRetry` is unconditional).

Base `WriteFileAtomic` did one `os.Rename` and failed fast. Now every caller retries up to 5 times with 40 ms backoff whenever the error is `ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED` (5) or `ERROR_SHARING_VIOLATION` (32). Nobody asked for this; POSIX is unaffected (`isRenameContention` → false → single attempt, identical wrap), so it is a Windows-only, silent change.

Concrete failure scenario: `cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/redact_cache.go:197` and `:221` call `WriteFileAtomic` **once per tree path** during condensation, and swallow the error with `logging.Debug`. On a Windows box where an AV scanner or search indexer holds handles under `.entire/` — which surfaces as errno 5, exactly what this code now treats as transient — each write goes from instant-and-silent to 160 ms-and-silent. 200 cached entries ⇒ **+32 s on a post-commit hook** with no user-visible cause. Same shape applies to `strategy/checkpoint_sync_capture.go:89` on the push path.

Second-order: `ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED` is also the errno for a genuinely read-only or ACL-denied destination. A permanent failure now costs 160 ms and gets its message rewritten to `"(another process may be reading X)"` — a wrong diagnosis for a permission problem.

Fix is one line of intent: make retry opt-in via `atomicWriteConfig` the same way `retainStagedOnPublishFailure` already is, so only `WriteFileAtomicStream` gets it.

### 2. CONFIRMED (doc + test regression; runtime currently OK) — the "no pipe for stdout" invariant is now undocumented and untested
`cmd/entire/cli/agent/opencode/cli_commands.go:33-45`, `cmd/entire/cli/agent/opencode/cli_commands_test.go`.

The deleted doc said: *"This avoids pipe/stdout capture truncation bugs in some opencode versions."* The deleted test `TestRunOpenCodeExportToFile_WritesStdoutToPath` exported to a real path. Its replacement `TestRunOpenCodeExport_WritesStdout` writes into a `bytes.Buffer` — which is **precisely the pipe path the original code was written to avoid** (`os/exec` only passes the fd through when `cmd.Stdout`'s dynamic type is `*os.File`; anything else gets `os.Pipe` + a copy goroutine).

I confirmed empirically that production is still safe: `*os.File` → `syncWriteCloser` → `io.Writer` preserves the dynamic type, and a child process sees a `REGULAR_FILE` on fd 1. So no live bug. But the guarantee is now load-bearing, invisible, and unenforced: wrapping `output` in a `bufio.Writer`, a byte counter, or an `io.MultiWriter` for logging reintroduces the truncation bug with every test green. Ask: restore the rationale as a comment on the `produce` closure (`lifecycle.go:233-236`) and keep one test that exports to a real file.

### 3. CONFIRMED — cancellation cleanup is internally inconsistent
`write.go:222-230` (cancel ⇒ plain error, staging deleted) vs `write.go:247-249` inside `renameWithRetry` (cancel ⇒ `PublishError`, staging **retained**).

The same user action — hook deadline or Ctrl-C — produces two different outcomes depending on nanosecond timing (POSIX) or on whether a Windows retry was in flight. In the retain branch, `lifecycle.go:242` reports `"failed to install export file (export saved at …)"` for what was actually a cancellation, and leaves a `.tmp` in `.entire/tmp` that only `entire clean` reclaims. `TestWriteFileAtomicStream_CancellationDuringRenameRetryRetainsStaging` (write_test.go:177) pins this as intended, and the PR body defends it, but the asymmetry with the check three lines earlier isn't defensible. Pick one: always retain post-validation, or classify cancellation separately from publish failure.

### 4. Design — the abstraction is parameterized to reproduce two behaviors, not to unify one
`atomicWriteConfig` (`write.go:124-128`) has three fields, **two of which exist solely to keep the two callers different**: `tempPattern` (leading dot for stream, none for the byte version) and `retainStagedOnPublishFailure`. `atomicWriteOps` is an 8-field function-pointer struct with exactly one production implementation, freshly allocated on every `WriteFileAtomic` call, existing only for test fault injection. That is a lot of machinery for one new consumer, zero duplication removed, and net-positive production LOC. The PR body itself notes no other rename-based writer was migrated — so the "centralization" premise is currently unrealized.

### 5. Nit, CONFIRMED — one new Windows test will never run in CI
`write_windows_test.go:13` `TestIsRenameContention`. `.github/workflows/ci.yml:71` runs Windows tests as `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/... -run '(Windows|MSYS)'`. The other three (`TestRenameWithRetry_Windows*`) match; this one doesn't. Rename it. (Also: the two `TestRenameWithRetry_*` tests inject `ops.rename`/`ops.wait` and are platform-independent — they could run everywhere with a sentinel error instead of being gated to the one platform that never runs them locally.)

### 6. Nit — the staging-name invariant lost its guard
`lifecycle_test.go:435`: `assertNoStagedExports` weakened from `HasPrefix(exportStagePrefix)` to `HasSuffix(".tmp")`. The deleted `exportStagePrefix` comment called the leading `.` and the absence of a `.json` suffix "load-bearing" because `.entire/tmp` is scanned by name (`state.go:658` `pre-task-*.json`, `factoryaidroid/tool_use_fallback.go` `*.json`). The new name `.&lt;sessionID&gt;.json.&lt;rand&gt;.tmp` **does** satisfy both — I checked every scanner — but nothing documents or tests it any more.

## Behavior-preservation per risky call site

| Site | Verdict |
|---|---|
| `WriteFileAtomic` — perms (`CreateTemp` 0600 → `Chmod(perm)` → rename) | **preserved** |
| `WriteFileAtomic` — fsync ordering (Write→Sync→Close→Chmod→Rename→best-effort dir fsync) | **preserved** |
| `WriteFileAtomic` — temp naming `base+".*.tmp"`, cleanup on every pre-rename failure | **preserved** |
| `WriteFileAtomic` — POSIX rename failure path + error text | **preserved** (byte-identical wrap) |
| `WriteFileAtomic` — **Windows** rename failure path | **CHANGED** — see #1 |
| OpenCode — validate-then-publish; failed/partial/empty/invalid export never clobbers a good transcript | **preserved** (tests cover all four) |
| OpenCode — export fsync before close | **preserved** |
| OpenCode — stdout goes to a real fd, not a pipe | **preserved at runtime** (verified), **doc + test lost** — see #2 |
| OpenCode — retain validated staging on rename failure + "export saved at %s" message | **preserved** |
| OpenCode — staging filename vs `.entire/tmp` scanners and `clean --all` | **preserved** (name changed, invariants hold) |
| OpenCode — Windows sharing-violation retry (5 × 40 ms, errno 5/32 only) | **preserved**, now cancellation-aware (improvement) |
| Checkpoint / session-state / transcript durability | **not touched** except via #1's Windows retry |
| Concurrency (concurrent hook processes) | **preserved** — `os.CreateTemp` per process, no fixed temp names, last-validated-writer-wins, no new TOCTOU |
| Symlinked / missing destination | **preserved** (`TestWriteFileAtomic_ParentMissing` still passes; rename replaces the symlink, same as base) |

Repo conventions: clean. No `git.PlainOpen`, no `worktree.Status()`, no `fmt.Print*` for operational output, `logging.Debug` used correctly, `SilentError` untouched, tests use `t.TempDir()` and `t.Parallel()` everywhere legal (the two `t.Chdir` tests correctly omit it).

## Blast radius

`jsonutil/write.go` is cold (0 commits since 2026-06-01). The OpenCode half is hot — main has churned every one of those five files since 2026-08-10. Three open PRs collide:

- **#2108** (open) modifies `renameOverExisting` inside `stage_export.go` — **the file this PR deletes**. Guaranteed hard conflict, and semantically overlapping: #2108 also rewrites the `"failed to install export file"` wrapping that #2053 relocates into `PublishError`. Whichever lands second needs a hand rebase, not a mechanical one.
- **#2030** (open) touches `jsonutil/write.go`, `jsonutil/write_test.go`, `clean.go`, `opencode/lifecycle.go` — 4-file overlap including the core file.
- **#2027** (open) touches `opencode/lifecycle.go` + `lifecycle_test.go`.

For a change that removes no measured duplication and adds net production LOC, three conflicting open PRs is a poor trade at this moment. Sequencing after #2108 would cost the least.

## Smaller version that captures most of the value

Yes, and it's most of the way there already. The real value is *"jsonutil owns the rename-retry and the retained-staging ownership contract so OpenCode doesn't hand-roll them"*. That is ~60 production lines: add `WriteFileAtomicStream(produce, validate)`, move `isRenameContention` into `jsonutil`, keep `renameWithRetry` **inside the stream path only**, and leave `WriteFileAtomic` on its existing single-rename code path. That eliminates finding #1 outright, drops both divergence flags from `atomicWriteConfig`, lets most of the 8-field `atomicWriteOps` collapse to real temp dirs plus one injectable rename hook, and shrinks the conflict surface with #2030 to near zero.

## What's genuinely good

This is careful work and it deserves saying so. The ordering contract (produce → sync → close → validate → chmod → rename → dir fsync) is stated explicitly and honored. `PublishError` is a real improvement: it turns "we kept the file, good luck" into a typed ownership transfer the caller can act on, and OpenCode's actionable message survives intact. The Windows retry is correctly narrowed to errno 5/32 rather than blanket-retrying. The context checkpoints are new capability the old code didn't have. The fault-injection tests are thorough — sync, close, chmod, open-for-validation, reader-close, and best-effort dir sync are all covered, and `require.Same` is used deliberately to pin that producer errors come back unwrapped. It landed with 0 lint issues, a clean `-race` suite, and both canaries green, and the PR body is honest about what it deliberately did *not* migrate. The three fixes above are all small and local.</result>

# Review: entireio/cli #2027 and #2132 (both by @MuskanPaliwal)

---

## A) #2027 — `fix(opencode): preserve prompts after metadata fallback`

**Verdict: merge-after-fixes.** The design is right and the tests are unusually good, but it does not compile against current `main`, and it introduces one new prompt-drop path plus leaves the same bug unfixed in the sibling code branch.

### Build / lint / test actuals

**As submitted** (head `d71f316b`, **80 commits behind main**):

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | **PASS** |
| `mise run lint` | **PASS — "0 issues."** |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/agent/opencode/...` | **ok** 2.5s |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/` | **ok** 113s |
| `go test -tags=integration -run TestOpenCode ./cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/...` | **ok**; both new tests `--- PASS` |

**Merged with current `origin/main`** (textual merge is conflict-free):

```
# github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli
cmd/entire/cli/lifecycle_prompt.go:76:30: undefined: strategy.MutateSessionStateSaved
```

After I locally adapted that single call site to `MutateSessionStateOnSaved(..., onSaved)`: build OK, and `opencode` pkg / `TestOpenCode*` integration / `TestDispatchLifecyclePromptUpdate*` / dogfood-drift tests all pass. So the substance survives the rebase; only the wiring needs redoing. (Local patch reverted; nothing left modified.)

### Findings, most severe first

**1. CONFIRMED — does not compile on current `main`.** `/Users/soph/.herdr/worktrees/cli/worktree-brave-harbor-f520` → `cmd/entire/cli/lifecycle_prompt.go:76`.
`main` replaced `MutateSessionStateSaved(...) (saved bool, err error)` with `MutateSessionStateOnSaved(ctx, id, fn, onSaved func())`. This is **not a rename** — the doc comment in `strategy/session_state.go:571-598` says the bool was removed precisely because *"this frame is not necessarily the one that saves"*, and that gating irreversible effects on it double-emits. The PR's code is the exact removed pattern:

```go
stateSaved, err := strategy.MutateSessionStateSaved(...)
...
if stateSaved { strategy.EmitSkillInvocationTelemetry(ctx, appendedSkillEvents) }
```

`EmitSkillInvocationTelemetry` is the *motivating case* named in that comment (duplicate PostHog events). Fix: move the emit into the `onSaved` callback.

**2. CONFIRMED — new prompt-drop path: content-keyed dedupe.** `cmd/entire/cli/lifecycle_prompt.go:47`

```go
if skipDuplicate &amp;&amp; len(prompts) &gt; 0 &amp;&amp; prompts[len(prompts)-1] == prompt {
    return false, nil
}
```

`prompt.txt` is cleared **only** at post-commit condensation, and only when every touched file is committed (`strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go:1355` → `clearFilesystemPrompt`). Turn-end does not clear it.

Failure scenario: user sends "run the tests" (fallback path → `prompt-update`), agent changes nothing, no commit; user sends "run the tests" again → last entry matches → **append silently skipped**. The eventual checkpoint records the prompt once instead of twice. Repeated identical prompts ("continue", "again", "run the tests") are routine. The non-fallback path (`appendPromptToFile`, `skipDuplicate=false`) has no such collapse, so the behavior is asymmetric and OpenCode-only.

The dedupe *is* load-bearing — their own `TestDispatchLifecyclePromptUpdate_DoesNotDuplicateTurnEndBackfill` shows why: turn-end backfills `prompt.txt` from the transcript (`lifecycle.go:776-800`) and a late repair would duplicate it. But the key is wrong. Key on **identity, not content**: record the repaired turn (`state.TurnID`, or a `PromptRepairedTurnID` field) and skip a second repair of the same turn.

**3. CONFIRMED code path / SUSPECTED reachability — the sibling branch fires a promptless `turn-start` without setting the marker.** `cmd/entire/cli/agent/opencode/entire_plugin.ts`, `message.part.updated` case:

```ts
if (!seenUserMessages.has(msg.id)) {
  seenUserMessages.add(msg.id)
  if (sessionID) {
    fireTurnStart({ session_id: sessionID, prompt: part.text ?? "", model: currentModel ?? "" })
  }
} else if (promptlessTurnStarts.has(msg.id) &amp;&amp; part.text &amp;&amp; sessionID) { ... }
```

If the **first** text part for an unseen message carries empty/undefined text, this fires `turn-start("")` and never adds to `promptlessTurnStarts`. The next part with real text falls into the `else if`, the marker is false, and the prompt is lost — bug #2001 verbatim, in the other branch. The author already assumes empty text parts occur (the `&amp;&amp; part.text` guard, and the harness's `text: ""` event). One-line fix: `if (!part.text) promptlessTurnStarts.add(msg.id)` in the first branch.

Same block, lower probability: `seenUserMessages.add(msg.id)` runs *before* the `if (sessionID)` check in **both** handlers, so a message first seen with no resolvable session ID is permanently marked seen and can never start a turn. Pre-existing.

**4. CONFIRMED — PR description contradicts the code on session-switch cleanup.** The body states *"The set is cleared on session switches, session deletion, and server disposal."* The diff **removes** `seenUserMessages.clear()` and `messageStore.clear()` from `resetSessionTracking()`; `clearSessionMessages()` is called only from `session.deleted`. Net effect: in a long-lived TUI, `messageStore` (full message-info objects) and `seenUserMessages` now grow for the entire process lifetime across every session, where they were previously bounded to the current session. There is a real motivation (a mid-flight session switch used to destroy a pending repair — the harness covers it), but it's an unstated behavior change with a memory cost. Either bound it or say so in the body.

**5. SUSPECTED — first-non-empty-chunk wins; progressive text truncates.** Once the first non-empty `part.text` lands, `promptlessTurnStarts.delete(msg.id)` fires and all later updates to that part are ignored — the harness enshrines this (`["message-3","third prompt"]` then `["message-3","duplicate third prompt"]` → only the first). Correct for genuine duplicate events; if OpenCode ever emits a user text part progressively, or a user message carries two text parts (text + pasted attachment), only the first chunk is captured. Worth confirming against OpenCode's `message.part.updated` semantics. Unchanged from pre-PR behavior on the normal path.

**6. CONFIRMED (minor) — `ErrStateNotFound` not tolerated.** `lifecycle_prompt.go:93` wraps every mutate error, including `strategy.ErrStateNotFound`. Every other handler in `lifecycle.go` explicitly tolerates it (lines 264, 343, 512, 650, 852, 1012, 1064, 1886, 1925, 2007). A `prompt-update` whose `turn-start` never initialized state (policy rejection, forwarded-hook skip, Entire enabled mid-session) now returns a hook error. Harmless to OpenCode (plugin discards stdio + exit code) but noisy in `.entire/logs` and inconsistent with the codebase.

**7. CONFIRMED (minor) — read-error semantics changed.** Old TurnStart code swallowed *any* `os.ReadFile` error and overwrote `prompt.txt` with just the new prompt; new `appendPrompt` (`lifecycle_prompt.go:42-45`) returns on any non-`NotExist` error and writes nothing. Safer, but it converts "lose all prior prompts once" into "lose all future prompts until the file is fixed". Deliberate? Worth a sentence.

**8. CONFIRMED (minor) — the strongest test never runs in CI.** `cmd/entire/cli/agent/opencode/hooks_test.go:274` skips unless `bun` is on `PATH`. `bun` is **not** in `mise.toml [tools]` and not installed by any `.github/workflows/*`. So `TestGeneratedPlugin_PreservesPromptAfterMessageUpdatedFallback` — the only test that runs the actual plugin — is a permanent skip on the merge path. There's precedent (`hooks_test.go:726` does the same with `node`), so it's a repo-wide gap, not a new sin; adding `bun` to `mise.toml` would activate both.

**9. Nit.** `cmd/entire/cli/lifecycle.go:794` still hardcodes `"\n\n---\n\n"` where `checkpoint.PromptSeparator` is now in use next door. Pre-existing; cheap to unify.

### Privacy — clean

`handleLifecyclePromptUpdate` logs only `event` and `session_id`. `slog.String("prompt", ...)` appears nowhere in non-test Go. The plugin's `callHookSync` uses `stdio: ["pipe","ignore","ignore"]`, so the payload reaches the hook's stdin only. `state.LastPrompt` stays truncated via `session.TruncatePromptForStorage` (100 runes). No new prompt text reaches logs.

### Collision with #1877 — **No.**

#1877 (Legonaftik, on-demand transcripts for attach) is **already MERGED** into `main` as `1ee312662`. Both PRs touch `cmd/entire/cli/agent/opencode/lifecycle.go` and `lifecycle_test.go`, but in disjoint regions: after merging `origin/main` into #2027's branch there were **zero textual conflicts**, and both symbols coexist in the merged tree — `FetchTranscript` at `lifecycle.go:189` (from #1877) alongside `HookNamePromptUpdate` at lines 50/61/99 (from #2027). #2027 does not touch `attach.go` at all. The rebase blocker is entirely unrelated to #1877 (see finding 1).

Note on the review brief's premise: #2027 does **not** assume the transcript file pre-exists. It never reads the transcript; it writes `.entire/metadata/&lt;session&gt;/prompt.txt`. The `opencode export` path is only reached via the pre-existing turn-end backfill, which #2027 leaves intact and defers to.

### What's genuinely good

- **The design pivot is correct and well-argued.** The first attempt re-fired `turn-start`, which would have reset `TurnID`, the pre-prompt snapshot, prompt attribution, `LastCheckpointID`, and `TurnCheckpointIDs`. A narrow `PromptUpdate` event that does none of that is the right shape.
- The integration test asserts the **whole** `SessionState` struct is unchanged except `LastPrompt` (`expectedState := *stateBefore; expectedState.LastPrompt = ...`). Most contributors wouldn't think to write that invariant.
- Ordering the file write *inside* the `MutateSessionState` gate so it serializes against condensation's `clearFilesystemPrompt`, with `TestDispatchLifecyclePromptUpdate_WaitsForSessionLockAndPersistsPrompt` actually exercising lock contention.
- `TestGeneratedPlugin_...` runs the real generated plugin under bun with a fake `entire` on `PATH` and asserts an exact 14-call ordered hook trace across empty parts, duplicates, removal, session deletion, interleaving, and disposal. Better plugin coverage than the repo previously had.
- Extracting `appendPromptSkillEventToState` removes real duplication *and* makes slash-command skill detection work on the repair path (a promptless `turn-start` previously couldn't see `/command`).
- `agent-guide.md` event tables updated; generated template and dogfood `.opencode/plugins/entire.ts` kept byte-identical (drift test passes).

This is a genuine red/green guard, not a tautology.

### Findings

**1. Scope — 3 affected `MultiSelect` sites remain unfixed (not a blocker; the PR says so).** I measured huh v2 directly (8 options, 40-row terminal): `MultiSelect` with a title only **does** hide the last option; `Select` (single) shows all 8. So the PR author's decision to exclude `Select` is **correct, and #2126's "46 call sites" figure over-counts** — worth stating in the thread, it resolves the issue's open question.

Still affected and untouched:
- `cmd/entire/cli/sessions.go:819` — "Select sessions to stop" (title + description → loses 2)
- `cmd/entire/cli/setup_import.go:179` — "Import existing sessions" (title + description → loses 2)
- `cmd/entire/cli/dispatch_wizard.go:265` — "Repos" (uses `OptionsFunc`, so the count isn't known at build time; the helper doesn't apply directly)

Already correct: `investigate/picker.go:213` (`len+2`, capped 12), `investigate/multipicker.go:60` (`len+1`, title only), `review/picker.go:1287` (`len+1`, title only).

`session stop` is the one I'd fix now — it's a user-facing list where hiding the most recent entries matters, and it's a two-line change with the helper in place.

**2. Nit — three coexisting height conventions.** `+3` (new helper), `+2` (investigate/picker), `+1` (multipicker, review/picker). Each is correct for its own header shape (yoffset = title rows + description rows), but the helper's doc ("reserves three rows for rendered field headers") doesn't convey the rule. Consider `SingleLineMultiSelectHeight(optionCount, headerRows int)` or naming the formula.

**3. Nit — the helper is opt-in, so the bug can be reintroduced.** #2126 explicitly asks "sweep the remaining call sites, or add a wrapper so new pickers cannot reintroduce it." This adds a wrapper that must be *remembered*. A `uiform.NewMultiSelect[T]()` constructor that sets height by default, or a `forbidigo` rule on bare `huh.NewMultiSelect`, would actually close it. Fine as follow-up.

### Convention checks — clean

- **Agent-safe fallbacks:** no regression. The change alters *visibility* of an existing list, not its membership. The non-interactive branch still prints `Use: entire agent add &lt;name&gt;` (`setup.go:489-490`), and `entire agent list` is unaffected. If anything this improves compliance — the bug was an interactive-only surface silently hiding two options.
- **External agents (#1928):** untouched. Both pickers still call `external.DiscoverAndRegisterAlways(ctx)` before building options (`setup.go:403`, `setup.go:497`). No duplication, no regression of that discovery path.
- **ACCESSIBLE:** both call sites remain on `NewAccessibleForm` → `uiform.New` → `WithAccessible(true)`; verified the new test passes under `ACCESSIBLE=1`.
- **Terminal gating:** no `IsTerminalWriter` / `CanPromptInteractively` changes.
- `t.Parallel()` on the new test and both subtests. No `fmt.Print*`, git, or filesystem surface touched.

### What's genuinely good

- Correct root-cause diagnosis of a third-party library bug, backed by a **headless reproducing test** rather than a screenshot — and the test genuinely fails without the fix.
- Deletes the local `multiSelectHeight` helper and reuses the shared one, so this is net de-duplication rather than additive code.
- Honest, tightly-drawn contract in the doc comment ("multiline options and unusually tall headers intentionally remain outside its contract") plus an explicit statement that the real fix belongs upstream in huh. That's exactly the right posture for a workaround.</result>

<result>## Verdict

**merge-after-fixes** — the bypass is real and the test is a genuine RED→GREEN regression test, but the PR fails `mise run lint` on gofmt as submitted, and the neutralizing predicate is both over-broad (mangles benign subjects) and misapplied to trailer *values*. A reader-side fix would be strictly better; details below.

**Scope correction:** GitHub reports base = `fix/shadow-commit-trailer-forgery`, i.e. this stacks on **PR #2168** (suhaanthayyil, open, base=main), which contributes the `(?m)^[ \t]*` anchoring + `flattenSubject`. #2176's own contribution is 2 commits / +15 −1. The unanchored-regex vulnerability described in the brief is fixed by **#2168**, not this PR. #2176 fixes the residual hole #2168 leaves.

## Build / lint / test actuals

| check | result |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | **PASS** (exit 0) |
| `mise run lint` | **FAIL** — `[lint:gofmt] ERROR task failed`, both files: `cmd/entire/cli/trailers/trailers.go`, `cmd/entire/cli/trailers/trailer_forgery_test.go` |
| `mise run lint` after `gofmt -w` | **PASS** — `[lint:go] 0 issues.` (formatting is the *only* lint failure) |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/trailers/...` | **ok** 0.24s |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/strategy/...` | **ok** 89.2s |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/` | **ok** 123.0s |

**Red/green check (fix hunk reverted, test kept):**
```
--- FAIL: TestFormatShadowCommit_SubjectCannotForgeTrailers (0.00s)
    trailer_forgery_test.go:65: ParseSession (single-line bypass) = "attacker-session" (ok=true),
        want "2026-01-20-8f76b0e8-b8f1-4a87-9186-848bdd83d62e"
    trailer_forgery_test.go:68: message has 4 trailer-started lines, want 3
FAIL	github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/trailers
```
All 13 other tests in the package still passed. Restoring the hunk → green. **The test is a real regression test, not a tautology.**

## Is the vulnerability real? — CONFIRMED

**Yes, on both halves, and the maintainers already know it.** `cmd/entire/cli/rewind.go:614-633` carries a comment saying the metadata dir "originates from the `Entire-Metadata` commit trailer, which is attacker-influenceable" and enforces `paths.IsSubpath` containment — so this threat model is already conceded in-tree.

**Attacker-controlled input reaches the shadow-commit subject verbatim:**
- Session path: `cmd/entire/cli/lifecycle.go:879-899` → `generateCommitMessage(lastPrompt, …)` → `cmd/entire/cli/commit_message.go:30-75` `cleanPromptForCommit` — strips a fixed prefix list, trims `?`, truncates to 72 runes. **No newline removal, no trailer-key filtering.**
- Task path: `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_git.go:231-245` → `FormatIncrementalSubject` → `FormatIncrementalMessage(step.TodoContent, …)` (`strategy/messages.go:85-93`) — the model-authored **TodoWrite `todos[].content`**, rune-truncated only (`ExtractLastCompletedTodoFromToolInput`, `hooks_claudecode_posttodo.go:101`). Shortest path from a hostile file in the repo under work to a shadow-commit subject.

**Exploitable parsers and impact** (all take the FIRST match; the subject precedes the trailer block):
- `ParseSession` → `checkpoint/ephemeral.go:536` filters which checkpoints belong to a session; `strategy/manual_commit_rewind.go:316` uses it to `loadSessionState` → `state.UntrackedFilesAtStart`, which decides **which untracked files rewind preserves vs deletes**; `:470` `resetShadowBranchToCheckpoint` hard-sets a shadow ref.
- `ParseMetadata`/`ParseTaskMetadata` → `ephemeral.go:206, 558, 564` → `RewindPoint.MetadataDir`, used as a **path** in git-tree lookups (`strategy/common.go:1412, 1457`, `explain.go:1695-1746`, `ephemeral.go:652-672`) and on the **filesystem** at `rewind.go:285, 470`. Only the rewind.go site is containment-checked; the tree lookups are not.

**On `origin/main` today** the regexes are unanchored, so a forgery doesn't even need a newline — a prose mention anywhere in the message wins. **#2168** closes that. **#2176** closes what #2168 leaves: after anchoring, a *single-line* subject that itself starts with `Entire-Session: ` is at column 0 of line 1 and still wins first-match. Verified.

Git's own semantics confirm Entire's parser is the outlier: `printf 'Entire-Session: attacker\n' | git interpret-trailers --parse` outputs **nothing** — git never treats the subject as a trailer.

## Is this the right fix? — No; anchoring is already done, and the *reader* should skip the subject

The anchoring question is moot (#2168 did it, correctly, with `[ \t]*` to survive `git merge --squash` indentation — that boundary is well-tested). The open question is how to close the line-1 case.

**#2176's writer-side approach mutates stored commit content and only covers half the surface.** `FormatCheckpoint` has **zero non-test callers**; the live user-commit path is `AppendCheckpointTrailer` (`attach.go:885`, `strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go:2160`), which is **not** flattened and must not be — you cannot rewrite a user's commit subject. I confirmed that half is still open:

```
message:
  Entire-Checkpoint: REDACTED    &lt;- contributor-authored subject
  (blank)
  Entire-Checkpoint: 01JAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA    &lt;- the real trailer
ParseCheckpoint =&gt; "REDACTED"    (the forgery wins)
```
That path is reached from commits Entire did not author: `attribution.go:402` (commits from `git blame`, including merged PR branches), `resume.go:627` (`origin/` branch tips, i.e. fetched commits), `checkpoint_resume.go:194`, `review_context.go:108` (builds the prompt fed to the review model), `explain.go:625, 1821, 1839, 2979`.

**Recommended instead: drop the subject line before matching, in the parsers.** ~4 lines, one place, no content mutation, matches git exactly, and closes both halves. I prototyped it against the PR tree:

```
skip-subject alternative resolves "real" correctly, message unmutated
skip-subject keeps squash parsing: "squashed-real"       (the #2168 boundary holds)
skip-subject on user commit resolves "01JAAAA…" (real)   (the half #2176 can't reach)
```
Known cost: a commit whose *entire* message is one trailer line stops parsing — which is precisely what `git interpret-trailers` does, so it is arguably correct. `AppendCheckpointTrailer("")` still works (it emits a blank subject line).

Flattening newlines out of the subject (#2168) is still worth keeping as defense-in-depth; it's the `(subject) ` prefix specifically that I'd replace.

## Findings

**F1 — BLOCKER: gofmt. `trailers.go:186-190`, `trailer_forgery_test.go:61-69`.** Added lines are indented with `space + tab` instead of `tab`, and `trailer_forgery_test.go:61` is a whitespace-only line. `mise run lint` fails: *"These .go files need to be formatted"*. Fork CI has not run; this is a guaranteed CI red. Fix: `mise run fmt`.

**F2 — over-broad predicate. `trailers.go:188.`** `strings.HasPrefix(s, "Entire-")` fires on any subject beginning with the literal `Entire-`, including non-trailers. Verified:
```
subject stored as: "(subject) Entire-Checkpoint trailer must be a ULID"
```
No forgery there — the regexes require `Entire-Checkpoint:` with a colon. The correct predicate is already written in the contributor's own test helper (`trailerStartedLines`): `strings.HasPrefix(line, "Entire-") &amp;&amp; IsTrailerLine(line)`. Use that in the fix too. (`git log --format=%s origin/main | grep -c '^Entire-'` = **0**, so no history regression here — but shadow subjects are prompts/todos, not commit subjects, so that count is only weakly reassuring, and this repo is exactly where an agent writes todos naming `Entire-*` keys.)

**F3 — the neutralizing prefix is applied to trailer VALUES. `trailers.go:204, 205, 216, 217.`** `flattenSubject` is called on `metadataDir` and `sessionID`. Verified round-trip:
```
sessionID "Entire-weird-session-id"  -&gt;  ParseSession returns "(subject) Entire-weird-session-id"
```
That silently corrupts the linkage key rather than rejecting it. Values need newline-collapsing only; the neutralizing prefix belongs at the subject call site. Split into `flattenSubject(message)` and a plain `stringutil.CollapseWhitespace` (or a `flattenValue`) for the two values. The name/comment already say "subject"; #2176 makes applying it to values actively wrong rather than merely misnamed.

**F4 — user-commit surface untouched (pre-existing, not a regression from this PR).** See the probe above. Worth a follow-up issue if the reader-side fix isn't adopted.

**F5 — minor, test discoverability.** The new assertions are appended into `TestFormatShadowCommit_SubjectCannotForgeTrailers` rather than a `t.Run("single-line bypass", …)` or its own function, so the behaviour the PR title names never appears in `go test -v` output. A named subtest would make the guarantee greppable.

**F6 — minor, `(subject) ` is user- and machine-visible.** Shadow-commit subjects are rendered by `checkpoint_list.go:136/139/142` (`entire checkpoint list`) and emitted as `Message` in the `--json` output at `checkpoint_list.go:59`. A bare `(subject) ` marker is unexplained to anyone reading that or `git log entire/&lt;sha&gt;`, and it's permanent in the object store.

**Non-findings (checked, clean):** no `fmt.Print*` added, no user content logged, `t.Parallel()` present on all new tests, tests are pure — no git repo, no config/keyring, no `t.Chdir`. Leading-whitespace bypass of the `HasPrefix` check is closed because `stringutil.CollapseWhitespace` ends in `strings.TrimSpace` (`stringutil.go:17-19`). Unicode line-separator bypass isn't possible: Go's `(?m)^` only breaks on `\n`. `Ephemeral-branch`, `Base-Commit`, `Entire-Source-Ref`, `Entire-Agent`, `Entire-Strategy` have **no parsers anywhere in the repo**, so restricting the check to `Entire-` keys leaves no gap today.

## What's genuinely good

- The bug is real, correctly diagnosed, and the reasoning in the PR body is accurate: the anchor in #2168 does not help when the hostile subject *is* line 1. That's a sharp catch on someone else's fix.
- The regression test genuinely fails without the change and passes with it, and it asserts a structural invariant (`trailerStartedLines(msg) == 3`), not just the one value — that's the right shape.
- The test helper already contains the precise predicate the fix should have used, which suggests the contributor understood the boundary; it just didn't make it into `flattenSubject`.
- Change is small, additive, and doesn't disturb the squash-merge boundary that #2168 was careful about — the full `strategy` and `cli` suites (212s combined) pass unchanged.

**No GitHub comment, review, reply, or push was made. All analysis was local in a detached worktree; the PR tree was restored to its exact submitted state after every experiment (`git status --porcelain` clean).**</result>

**merge-after-fixes** — the installer core is genuinely well-built and *does* verify checksums correctly against the real release layout, but the PR's headline "PowerShell 7 compatibility fix" is dead code resting on a false premise, and the user-PATH write has a known-destructive Windows failure mode.
