## Findings

### F1 — CONFIRMED (correctness/complexity): the entire "PowerShell 7 fix" is unreachable dead code
`install.ps1:129-134`, `:179-184`; `README.md:70,76,145,146`; `install.sh:64-72`

Microsoft's PS 7.5 docs for `Invoke-RestMethod`, verbatim: *"This parameter has been deprecated… This parameter is included for backwards compatibility only. When used, it has no effect on the operation of the cmdlet."* The parameter **still exists** in PS 6/7 — it appears in every 7.x syntax block. Therefore `(Get-Command Invoke-RestMethod).Parameters.ContainsKey("UseBasicParsing")` returns `$true` on PowerShell 7 exactly as on 5.1, the `else` branches are never taken, and both arms are behaviourally identical.

The PR body's rationale — *"preventing runtime errors in newer PowerShell versions where the flag is deprecated"* — is wrong. Deprecated ≠ removed. The cost of guarding against this non-error is a 6-line copy-paste blob in the README where a one-liner belongs, two extra `Get-Command` lookups per HTTP call, and the same dead conditional pasted into four more user-visible places. The repo's own `.github/workflows/e2e-windows.yml:30` already runs `irm https://claude.ai/install.ps1 | iex` — the plain shape, which is what this should be.

**Ask:** revert to unconditional `-UseBasicParsing` (harmless everywhere) or drop the flag entirely. This deletes ~40% of the PR's user-facing surface.

### F2 — CONFIRMED-by-known-behaviour (destructive): `Add-ToUserPath` flattens `REG_EXPAND_SZ` PATH entries
`install.ps1:257-276`

```powershell
$userPath = [Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable("Path", "User")
...
[Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable("Path", $newUserPath, "User")
```

`GetEnvironmentVariable` returns the **expanded** value; `SetEnvironmentVariable` writes back a `REG_SZ`. Any `%USERPROFILE%\…`-style entry in the user's PATH — written by scoop, the Python installer, npm, VS Code, and many others — is permanently converted to a hardcoded literal. Irreversible, silent, and hits a machine that never even asked for a direct install. This is precisely why rustup, scoop, and pnpm all write PATH via `Microsoft.Win32.Registry` with `RegistryValueKind.ExpandString`.

Secondary: no `WM_SETTINGCHANGE` broadcast, so already-running Explorer-launched shells won't pick up the change.

Everything else about the PATH handling is right: **User** scope (never Machine), and `Test-PathContains` (`:238-255`) makes re-runs properly idempotent.

### F3 — CONFIRMED (PS 5.1): the advertised one-liner has no TLS 1.2 bootstrap
`README.md:69-77,145-146`; `install.sh:66-72`

`install.ps1:297-298` sets TLS 1.2 correctly — but that line runs *after* the script has already been downloaded. On stock Windows PowerShell 5.1 without `SchUseStrongCrypto`, `ServicePointManager.SecurityProtocol` defaults to SSL3|TLS1.0; `raw.githubusercontent.com` requires TLS 1.2, so the bootstrap `irm` fails with *"Could not create SSL/TLS secure channel"* before the script exists. Chocolatey, scoop, rustup and pnpm all prefix their one-liners with `[Net.ServicePointManager]::SecurityProtocol = 3072` for exactly this reason. This one doesn't. The README claims "Windows PowerShell 5.1 or later" — that claim is untested on the machines where it matters.

### F4 — CONFIRMED-by-reading (logic): `-Channel nightly` on any Scoop machine always ends in a hard error
`install.ps1:291-293` → `:359-372`

Nightly + scoop present → warn, proceed, install to `~/.local\bin`. Then `Get-Command "entire" -CommandType Application` finds scoop's shim (`~\scoop\shims\entire.exe`), `Test-SamePath` is false, and the script **throws** "Installation completed, but PATH needs adjustment" — *after* copying both binaries. So the README's documented nightly command (`README.md:146`) reliably fails on the machine most likely to be running it: one that already has stable via scoop. The user is left half-configured staring at a red error.

### F5 — CONFIRMED (UX/contract): the Scoop branch silently discards `-InstallDir` and `-NoPathUpdate`
`install.ps1:286-290`

Stable + scoop present → `Install-EntireWithScoop` returns immediately. A user who passed `-InstallDir D:\tools` gets an install somewhere else with no message. The branch also adds a third-party bucket as a side effect (`:75`) without asking. At minimum: warn when `-InstallDir` is explicitly set and the Scoop path is taken, or let an explicit `-InstallDir` force the direct path.

### F6 — CONFIRMED (test gap): CI does not exercise the one form users are told to run
`.github/workflows/ci.yml:80-93`

Both new steps run `.\scripts\install.ps1 -Help` — the *file-invocation* path. The README advertises `irm … | iex`, which parses the script as a scriptblock and binds no parameters. That path, the `[scriptblock]::Create` nightly path, and the entire download/checksum/extract/PATH flow are all untested. `-Help` returns at `:279-282` before anything happens. Placement in `test-windows` (windows-latest) is correct and the 5.x/7.x version assertions are a nice touch — but this is a syntax smoke test, not coverage. A `-WhatIf`-style dry run, or at minimum piping the file through `iex` in CI, would catch real regressions.

### F7 — CONFIRMED (lint gap): whitespace defects that nothing in the repo will catch
- `install.sh:64` — the `error "…"` line gained a stray leading space (13 vs the 12 used by every sibling `case` arm).
- `install.sh:68` — a **trailing-whitespace-only line** (`    `) inside the heredoc-style string.
- `install.ps1:179-184` — `Save-RemoteFile`'s `if`/`else` bodies are dedented to column 4, breaking the file's own indentation.
- `install.ps1:129` — stray extra leading space on the `if`.

`mise-tasks/lint/shellcheck` runs `find mise-tasks -type f …` — `scripts/install.sh` is outside its scope, and there is no PowerShell linter. `mise run lint` reports 0 issues on all of the above. If the team wants installers held to the same bar as Go, this PR is the moment to widen the shellcheck glob and add PSScriptAnalyzer.

### F8 — CONFIRMED (docs): README block loses its code fence and the nightly instructions are commented out
`README.md:68-77`

The new block uses 5-space indentation (a bare indented code block) while the ```powershell fence sits 12 lines below at `:87` and every other install block in the file is fenced. Worse, the nightly instructions at `:75-77` are `#`-commented *inside* that block — copy-pasting them does nothing, and they depend on `$uri` from `:69`. `:145` and `:146` also spell the same dead check two different ways (`Get-Command Invoke-RestMethod` vs `Get-Command irm`).

### F9 — SUSPECTED (StrictMode): `$LASTEXITCODE` may be unset on the Scoop path
`install.ps1:66`

`Set-StrictMode -Version 2.0` is active. If `Get-Command "scoop"` (`:286`) resolves to `scoop.ps1` rather than `scoop.cmd`, invoking it does **not** set `$LASTEXITCODE`, and in a fresh session reading it throws *"The variable '$LASTEXITCODE' cannot be retrieved because it has not been set."* Scoop's standard shim layout makes `scoop.cmd` win on PATHEXT, so this is likely dormant — but I could not test it. Cheap fix: `$LASTEXITCODE = 0` before the first native call.

Related, low: `&amp; "scoop" update entire/entire` (`:85`) — `scoop update` conventionally takes a bare app name; `bucket/app` acceptance is version-dependent. Unverified.

### F10 — Low
- `install.ps1:129,179` — `Get-Command` without `-CommandType Cmdlet`; a profile-defined function or alias shadowing `Invoke-RestMethod` is picked up by both the probe and the call. Free to fix.
- `install.ps1:210-214` — `[IO.Path]::GetFullPath` resolves relative paths against the **.NET process** working directory, not PowerShell's `Get-Location`; `-InstallDir bin` can land somewhere surprising. Also `.TrimEnd(DirectorySeparatorChar)` turns `C:\` into the drive-relative `C:`.
- Corporate proxy: `Invoke-WebRequest` in Windows PowerShell 5.1 uses the WinINET/IE proxy, and neither 5.1 nor 7 handles an authenticated (407) proxy without `-ProxyUseDefaultCredentials`. `install.sh` gets curl's `http_proxy`/`https_proxy` handling for free; this doesn't.
- Post-install parity: `install.sh:344` runs `entire curl-bash-post-install`; `install.ps1` never does. I checked `cmd/entire/cli/setup.go:2150-2201` — it only wires zsh/bash/fish completion off `$SHELL`, and the release archive ships no `completions/entire.ps1`. So omitting it is **correct**, not a bug. Noting it only so it isn't "fixed" later by someone who didn't check.

---

## Security answers to the brief

- **Download integrity — YES, and better than the incumbent.** `Assert-Checksum` (`:187-208`) downloads `checksums.txt` from the pinned release, matches with an anchored `^[0-9a-f]{64}\s+\*?&lt;escaped-name&gt;\s*$`, and compares `Get-FileHash -Algorithm SHA256` ordinal-insensitively. `install.sh:229` uses `grep -iE "${archive_name}\$"` — anchored only at the end, so a hypothetical `foo_entire_windows_amd64.zip` line would match. The PowerShell version is the stricter of the two.
- **TLS/URL — clean.** No `-SkipCertificateCheck`, no `http://`, no credential handling. Host is hardcoded to `github.com/entireio/cli`. The only interpolated URL components are `$version` (semver-regex-validated at `:166` before use — `install.sh` does *not* do this) and `$architecture` (closed `switch`, `:111-115`). No injection vector found.
- **Execution policy / privilege — clean.** No `Set-ExecutionPolicy` anywhere, no elevation, no `LocalMachine` scope, no writes outside `%USERPROFILE%` and the system temp dir.
- **Injection — clean inside the script.** No `iex`/`Invoke-Expression` on fetched content. `-LiteralPath` used consistently, so `C:\Users\First Last` and usernames containing `[` are handled. The `irm | iex` bootstrap is the standard trust-the-vendor model and matches how `install.sh` is already distributed.
- **PATH — user scope, deduplicated, idempotent** (good) **but destructive to expandable entries** (F2).
- **Trust root context (pre-existing, not this PR's fault):** `https://entire.io/install.sh` is a 307 to `raw.githubusercontent.com/entireio/cli/refs/heads/main/scripts/install.sh` — so both installers already serve from branch tip. The checksum chain is the only integrity control: Windows binaries carry no Authenticode signature (`.goreleaser.yaml` notarizes macOS only) and `checksums.txt` has no cosign/SLSA attestation, so a GitHub-account compromise defeats it. Worth a separate issue, not a blocker here.
- **Hosting coordination:** `https://entire.io/install.ps1` **does not exist** (returns `307 → /login`). The PR bakes the raw GitHub URL into four user-visible places. Adding the vanity redirect before merge would keep the two installers symmetric and give the team a rollback point; `raw.githubusercontent.com` is also blocked on far more corporate networks than a vendor domain.

## Duplication / maintenance

Release-format knowledge now lives in three hand-maintained places (`install.sh`, `install.ps1`, plus the GoReleaser-generated scoop manifest and homebrew casks): archive naming, `checksums.txt` retrieval, the two GitHub Releases API shapes, version normalization, PATH-conflict messaging, and `git-remote-entire` handling are all reimplemented. `install.ps1` additionally wraps Scoop, so a stable Windows install has two code paths with different semantics. `docs/install-provenance-plan.md` states *"Make each installer write the provenance file"* — this makes that a fourth writer. Acceptable for a platform that genuinely needs a native installer, but it should be a conscious decision and the provenance plan should be updated to name `install.ps1`.

## What's genuinely good

Credit where due — this is well above the median drive-by installer PR:

- **Checksum verification is present and stricter than the existing bash installer** (anchored both ends, 64-hex guard, handles the `*` binary marker).
- **The release version is semver-validated before URL interpolation** — `install.sh` doesn't do this at all.
- **Artifact names and archive layout are exactly right.** I confirmed against live v0.10.3: `entire_windows_amd64.zip` / `entire_windows_arm64.zip` exist, the zip is flat with `entire.exe` and `git-remote-entire.exe` at the root, so `Join-Path $extractDir "entire.exe"` (`:333-334`) resolves. No 404s, no path typos.
- `PROCESSOR_ARCHITEW6432` handling (`:100-105`) for 32-bit PowerShell on ARM64/x64 — a detail most installers get wrong.
- The `foreach` over `Invoke-RestMethod` array output (`:145-154`) with an accurate comment is a real, non-obvious Windows PowerShell 5.1 gotcha, correctly handled.
- `try`/`finally` temp cleanup in a GUID-named directory; explicit TLS 1.2 via the numeric literal *with* a comment explaining why the enum name is avoided; `-LiteralPath` discipline throughout; the child-scriptblock scoping (`:15-17`) so `Set-StrictMode`/`$ErrorActionPreference` don't leak into the caller's shell when piped to `iex`.
- Adding CI on both 5.1 and 7 with explicit version assertions, placed in the correct `windows-latest` job — even limited, it's more than the repo had.

The contributor clearly read `install.sh` and improved on it in several places. The fixes needed are localized: revert F1, fix the PATH write in F2, resolve F4/F5's Scoop interaction, and re-fence the README.

# Review: PRs #2179 and #2182 (Beehtrix, external)

## Cross-cutting finding (both PRs) — CONFIRMED

**Neither PR targets `main`.** `#2179` targets `tokens-report`; `#2182` targets `paulo-goroot`. Both are other people's unmerged WIP branches. For #2179 this matters materially: **`ReadTaskRecords` does not exist on `main` at all** (`git grep ReadTaskRecords` on main → zero hits). The contributor is patching un-landed code owned by an in-flight branch. Whoever owns `tokens-report` should decide, not a general reviewer.

---

## A) #2179 — "surface load errors for missing checkpoints"

### Verdict: **REJECT** (as written) — breaks two existing tests, violates the documented interface contract, and fails gofmt.

### Actuals (worktree `…/scratchpad/rev-2179`, PR head `663785414` merged with `origin/main` `cbcda52db`)
| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | **exit 0**, clean |
| `golangci-lint run ./cmd/entire/cli/...` | **0 issues** |
| `mise run lint` | **FAIL** — `lint:gofmt`: `cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint_api_reader.go` needs formatting |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/... ./cmd/entire/cli/` | **exit 1** — `FAIL cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint` (12.19s); `ok cmd/entire/cli` (139.6s), `ok` fsstore/id/remote |

Failing tests:
- `TestReadTaskRecords/git-branch/unknown_checkpoint_is_ErrCheckpointNotFound` (`checkpoint/task_records_test.go:191`)
- `TestKindRoutingStore_ReadTaskRecords/absent_everywhere_is_ErrCheckpointNotFound` (`task_records_test.go:264`)

Attribution CONFIRMED: reverting the single `persistent.go` line back to `return nil, ErrCheckpointNotFound` makes both pass (`ok … 0.803s`).

### Fail-open vs fail-closed: **still FAIL-OPEN. No hook-path regression.**

Full caller trace of `ReadTaskRecords` (non-test):
- `cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint_tokens_load.go:89` — **the only production caller**, reached from `entire checkpoint tokens`.
- `checkpoint/fanout.go:67` and `checkpoint/routing_store.go:160` — pure delegation, no error handling.

`loadCheckpointTokenInputs` (`checkpoint_tokens_load.go:89-103`) explicitly degrades:
```go
records, err := store.ReadTaskRecords(ctx, cpID)
if err != nil {
    if ctxErr := ctx.Err(); ctxErr != nil { return checkpointTokenInputs{}, ctxErr }
    logging.Warn(ctx, "checkpoint tokens: task records unreadable", …)
    inputs.recordsErr = err          // non-fatal note on the report
}
```
Only context cancellation propagates. The struct field is even documented at `:47` as *"a non-fatal ReadTaskRecords failure, reported as a note."*

**No hook path is involved at all** — `ReadTaskRecords` is not called from post-commit, Stop, pre-push, or anything in `strategy/`. So the CLAUDE.md "capture degrades rather than aborts" invariant is not at risk here. This one is clean.

### Findings, most severe first

**1. CONFIRMED — `persistent.go:1576` breaks the documented `ErrCheckpointNotFound` contract for the common "no v1 branch yet" case.**
`getCheckpointFetchingTree` returns `ErrCheckpointNotFound` only on a *tree-walk* miss (`persistent.go:2298`). One level up, `getSessionsBranchTrees` returns `fmt.Errorf("sessions ref %s not found: %w", …)` / `fmt.Errorf("sessions branch not found: %w", …)` (`persistent.go:2351,2353`) when the metadata branch doesn't exist. That is *not* an `ErrCheckpointNotFound` chain — which is exactly what the test failure prints:
```
expected: "checkpoint not found"
in chain: "sessions branch not found: reference not found"
```
This breaks the contract stated in **two** places, neither updated by the PR:
- `cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/persistent.go:1568` — *"Returns ErrCheckpointNotFound if the checkpoint doesn't exist"*
- `api/checkpoint/interfaces.go:24-31` — *"a missing checkpoint yields an error wrapping ErrCheckpointNotFound"* (the exported interface contract)

So the contributor's premise — *"getCheckpointFetchingTree already returns ErrCheckpointNotFound on a true miss"* — is **only half true**: true for a missing checkpoint path, false for a missing branch. A repo with no condensed/pushed checkpoints is the everyday case.

**2. CONFIRMED — inconsistent with its sibling; the masking is deliberate convention, not an oversight.** The identical mask sits at `persistent.go:1466` (`getSessionTree`) and is *documented* at `:1457-1458` as intentional; `GitStore.Read` (`:1424`) is even more lenient (`return nil, nil //nolint:nilnil,nilerr`). The PR changes one of three and leaves the family split. If the masking really is wrong, all three plus the interface doc need to move together.

**3. CONFIRMED — `checkpoint_api_reader.go:292-295` is gofmt-invalid.** All four added lines are indented `space+tab` instead of `tab`. `mise run lint` → `lint:gofmt ERROR task failed`. CI blocks on this.

**4. CONFIRMED — the `apiCheckpointReader` half is a runtime no-op today, and adds a network round-trip for it.** `apiCheckpointReader` is constructed only at `explain_repo.go:48` (the `--repo` path). `explainExportOptions` (`explain_export.go:43-55`) has no repo field, and `checkpoint tokens` passes only `target` (`checkpoint_tokens_load.go:55`). So the sole `ReadTaskRecords` caller can never hold an `apiCheckpointReader` — the method exists purely to satisfy `SessionReader`. The change buys nothing and costs one `GET /api/v1/repos/{id}/checkpoints/{id}` per call the moment a caller does appear. (`loadDetail` is memoized at `checkpoint_api_reader.go:339-341`, so it'd be free *if* another read already ran on the same instance — not guaranteed.)

**5. CONFIRMED — the api-reader change doesn't achieve the stated consistency either.** `loadDetail` returns bespoke prose errors for 404/403 (`checkpoint_api_reader.go:357,360`) that do **not** wrap `ErrCheckpointNotFound`. So after the change `apiCheckpointReader.ReadTaskRecords` still violates the `api/checkpoint/interfaces.go` contract it was trying to honour — it just violates it differently. It also leaves the doc comment at `:288-290` ("reports no task records: the API exposes no per-subagent task record endpoint") describing behaviour the function no longer has.

**6. CONFIRMED — no tests added.** Both commits are code-only (`5 insertions` + `1 insertion`). Repo convention requires tests for behaviour changes, and the api-reader path has none at all (`checkpoint_api_reader_test.go` has zero `ReadTaskRecords` references).

### Contributor claims — factual scoring
| Claim | Verdict |
|---|---|
| `persistent.go` masks all `getCheckpointFetchingTree` errors as `ErrCheckpointNotFound`, hiding real failures | **Correct** |
| `getCheckpointFetchingTree` already returns `ErrCheckpointNotFound` on a true miss | **Half correct** — not for the missing-branch case, which is what breaks the tests |
| `apiCheckpointReader.ReadTaskRecords` "swallows errors" | **Incorrect** — nothing is attempted, so nothing is swallowed. It's a deliberate stub, documented as such |
| …"inconsistent with the rest of `apiCheckpointReader`" and can't distinguish no-tasks from no-checkpoint | **Correct as a contract observation** (`interfaces.go:26-28` does demand an error), but the fix doesn't deliver a conforming error |

**Q3 — does "checkpoint legitimately has no task records" now error?** **No.** That path is `readTaskRecordsFromCheckpointTree`, untouched; `TestReadTaskRecords/*/checkpoint without tasks yields no records and no error` still passes. Confirmed.

**Q4 — cross-repo network hiccup?** Fail-open preserved (caller degrades to a note), but the change *creates* a new way for a transient 5xx to put a spurious warning on an otherwise-fine report, on a path that previously could not fail. Net negative given #4.

### What's genuinely good
The `persistent.go` observation is a real smell that a careful reader found: blanket `err → sentinel` masking does destroy diagnostics, and the commit messages are specific and honest about the reasoning. The right shape is `if errors.Is(err, ErrCheckpointNotFound) { return nil, err }; return nil, fmt.Errorf("resolve checkpoint tree: %w", err)` — or make `getSessionsBranchTrees` wrap `ErrCheckpointNotFound` — applied to `getSessionTree` and `Read` too, with the two doc comments updated. Worth telling the contributor that.

## B) #2182 — "Update status.go"

### Verdict: **REJECT** — trips the repo's own `wrapcheck` linter; CI-blocking, with no behavioural benefit to justify a `//nolint`.

### Actuals (worktree `…/scratchpad/rev-2182`, PR head `820979456` merged with `origin/main`)
| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | **exit 0**, clean |
| `gofmt -l cmd/entire/cli/status.go` | clean |
| `mise run lint` | **FAIL — 1 issue**, caused by this change:&lt;br&gt;`cmd/entire/cli/status.go:80:10: error returned from external package is unwrapped: sig: func …settings.FilesPresent(ctx context.Context) (project bool, local bool, err error) (wrapcheck)` |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/` | exit 1 — **1 failure, PRE-EXISTING, not this PR** |

Test attribution CONFIRMED: `TestHandleLifecycleTurnEnd_EmptyRepository` (`lifecycle_test.go:854`, *"expected nil for empty repository (graceful no-op), got: failed to get worktree root"*) reproduces identically with the one-liner reverted. It belongs to the `paulo-goroot` base branch's `os.Root`/worktree-root work ("Stop opening the current directory when git will not say which repo this is"), not to #2182.

### Findings

**1. CONFIRMED, blocking — the wrap exists to satisfy `wrapcheck`.** `.golangci.yaml:75` enables `wrapcheck`; its `ignore-package-globs` (`:90-104`) lists only `internal/coreapi` and `cmd/entire/cli/checkpointpolicy`. `cmd/entire/cli/settings` is not exempt and `status.go` is not in the exclusion rules (`:130-172`). The same bare-`%w` idiom appears at `settings/settings.go:1034` and `:1069` for the same reason. Removing it is exactly the pattern the linter is configured to catch.

**2. CONFIRMED — the stated risk does not exist in this codebase.** Nothing compares this error at all. `runStatus` has exactly one production caller (`status.go:45`, the cobra `RunE`), and the error flows to `main.go`, which only prints it. `FilesPresent` has two callers: `status.go:78` and `status.go:853` (the latter takes `presenceErr.Error()` for JSON). No `==`, no `errors.Is`, no type assertion anywhere on this value. Nothing to fix.

**3. Correction to the claim's mechanics.** `fmt.Errorf("%w", err)` **preserves** `errors.Is` and `errors.As` — the chain is intact. Only top-level `==` identity and a bare type assertion on the outermost value would break. So "can break any direct equality checks" is technically true but describes a failure mode nothing in the repo exhibits, and the wording overstates it by implying `errors.Is` is at risk.

### Contributor claims — factual scoring
| Claim | Verdict |
|---|---|
| `settings.FilesPresent` errors are already contextual | **Correct** — all three return paths wrap: `"cannot access %s: %w"`, `"cannot access project settings file: %w"`, `"cannot access local settings file: %w"` (`settings/settings.go:1649,1653,1657`) |
| `fmt.Errorf("%w", err)` is a no-op for the message | **Correct** — identical string, new `*fmt.wrapError` |
| …but changes the concrete error value, which can break direct equality checks | **Technically correct, practically vacuous** — nothing does that here; `errors.Is`/`As` unaffected |
| Therefore prefer returning the original | **Incorrect conclusion** — the wrap is load-bearing for `wrapcheck`, which the PR did not check |

### What's genuinely good
The reading of `FilesPresent` is accurate and the reasoning about `fmt.Errorf("%w", err)` being message-identical-but-type-changing is correct Go. This is someone who actually understands error wrapping. The miss is repo-specific: they didn't know a linter was the reason the line looked redundant. Worth saying so, and pointing at `.golangci.yaml:75` + `settings.go:1034` as the precedent — that's the kind of context an external contributor can't be expected to infer.

# Review: PRs #2175, #2177, #2181 (Beehtrix, external)

## Finding that applies to all three — they do not target `main`

| PR | Base branch | That branch is | Author |
|---|---|---|---|
| #2175 | `audit/shadow-branch-hash-vector` | open PR **#2174** | suhaanthayyil |

| #2181 | `cli-dispatch-jurisdiction` | open PR **#2153** | alishakawaguchi |

These are drive-by patches onto *other contributors' in-flight branches*, not contributions to `main`. Each PR head is a direct child of its base tip. Merging any of them mutates someone else's unmerged PR without that author's involvement. CI genuinely has not run (`Entire Gates: skipping`; `mergeStateStatus: UNSTABLE` on all three). Recommend routing all three to the respective branch owners as review comments rather than merging.

## Blocker common to all three: whitespace corruption → `mise run lint` fails

Every added or modified line carries a stray leading space (` func`, ` \tt.Parallel()`), and #2175 additionally converts tabs to 4-space indent. `mise run lint` has a dedicated `lint:gofmt` subtask, and it fails on all three:

```
[lint:gofmt] These .go files need to be formatted:
[lint:gofmt] cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/ephemeral_test.go            (#2175)
[lint:gofmt] cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/ephemeral_transcript_chunk_test.go  (#2177)

[lint:gofmt] ERROR task failed
```

Signature is identical across all three — consistent with pasting diff-formatted text into the GitHub web editor. `go build ./...` and `go vet` pass regardless (Go ignores indentation); only gofmt catches it. Deterministic CI failure, trivially fixed with `mise run fmt`.

## A) #2175 — `ephemeral_test.go` t.Parallel — **REJECT (as-is)**

One-line reason: introduces two *new* `tparallel` lint errors that were not there before, on top of the gofmt breakage.

### Verification
| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | pass |
| `go vet ./cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/...` | pass |
| `mise run lint` | **FAIL** — `lint:gofmt` |
| `mise run lint` after local `gofmt -s -w` | **FAIL** — `lint:go`, 2 × `tparallel` |
| Same, with the two t.Parallel lines reverted (baseline) | **0 issues** — proves the PR introduces them |
| `go test -race -count=1 ./cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/...` | all 4 packages `ok` (41.5s) |
| `go test -race -count=2 ./cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/...` | **FAIL** — but **pre-existing**, reproduced identically on clean `origin/main` |
| `go test -race -count=500 -run 'TestHashWorktreeID\|TestShadowBranchNameForCommit\|TestParseShadowBranchName'` | `ok` (11.3s), no races |

The `-count=2` failure is `TestValidatePrimaryBackend_NonGitBackedRejected` panicking with `checkpoint: backend type "test-mirror-only-primary-check" already registered` (`cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/registry_test.go:63` → `registry.go:85`). The registry is a package global and `Register` is not idempotent, so `-count=N&gt;1` can never work for this package. Reproduced on unmodified `origin/main` — **not attributable to this PR**, but worth its own issue.

### CONFIRMED findings
1. **`cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/ephemeral_test.go:30` and `:87` — new `tparallel` violations.** golangci-lint (`tparallel` is enabled at `.golangci.yaml:67`):
   ```
   ephemeral_test.go:30:6: TestHashWorktreeID's subtests should call t.Parallel (tparallel)
   ephemeral_test.go:87:6: TestShadowBranchNameForCommit's subtests should call t.Parallel (tparallel)
   ```
   Both are table-driven; `t.Parallel()` was added to the parent only, not to the `t.Run` closures. Verified fix: adding `t.Parallel()` inside both subtest closures → `lint:go: 0 issues` and `-race -count=50` passes.

2. **`ephemeral_test.go:26-30` — the contributor's own cited exemplar contradicts the patch.** `objectsigner_test.go:13` (`TestCustomSignProgram`) calls `t.Parallel()` at the top *and again at line 107 inside `t.Run`*. The cited model shows the correct pattern; the PR implements half of it.

3. **Claim only half-delivered.** Four of the six tests in the file are still serial: `TestHashWorktreeID_Deterministic`, `TestHashWorktreeID_DifferentInputs`, `TestParseShadowBranchName`, `TestParseShadowBranchName_RoundTrip`. All four are equally safe to parallelize. "Consistency" is not achieved.

### Per-test parallel-safety determination
| Test | `t.Chdir`/`Setenv` in test or helpers | Shared mutable state | Deliberately serial? | Safe to parallelize |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TestHashWorktreeID` (:30) | none — calls only `HashWorktreeID` (`ephemeral.go:47`, pure sha256+hex) | none: no temp dir, no repo, no globals | No | **Yes** |
| `TestShadowBranchNameForCommit` (:87) | none — calls only `ShadowBranchNameForCommit` (`ephemeral.go:709`, pure string ops over consts) | none | No | **Yes** |

Neither test touches `t.TempDir`, git, or the package's mutable globals (`createBlobFromContent`, the backend registry). `TestMain` (`global_test.go:14`) only registers a go-git config plugin once before `m.Run()` and is unaffected. Both parents run their subtests via `t.Run` with Go 1.26 per-iteration loop vars, so no capture bug; there is no parent-cleanup-before-subtest hazard because neither uses `t.TempDir`.

**Deliberately serial? No.** `git blame` shows both were written by Alex Ong on 2026-02-02 (`619f25fd28`, `4b7c71d21a`), in `temporary_test.go` before its rename, in a file where *no* test has `t.Parallel`. This is an original omission from a file predating the convention, not a documented exemption. No trap here — the change is directionally right.

### Contributor claims
- "These tests don't call `t.Parallel()`" — **correct** (0 of 6 in the file).
- "Most other tests in this package do" — **correct** (~170 of ~250 top-level test funcs across the package).
- Citations `objectsigner_test.go:13` and `persistent_reader_test.go:16` — **both exactly accurate**, line numbers included.

### Good
Correct diagnosis, correct target, precise and verifiable citations, and both tests really are safe to parallelize. This is the rare consistency PR that picked genuinely safe candidates. The fix is small: `mise run fmt`, add `t.Parallel()` to the two subtest closures, and ideally cover the other four tests.

## B) #2177 — `ephemeral_transcript_chunk_test.go` — **MERGE AFTER FIXES**

One-line reason: the test-strengthening logic is correct and lands the scenario it claims, but it fails gofmt and costs ~480 MB RSS / an 18× slowdown for one unit test.

Note: **this PR is not a `t.Parallel` change** despite the same-shape framing. Its body is a review critique of #2167's test, and the diff implements that critique. The file exists only on #2167's branch, not on `main`.

### Verification
| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | pass |
| `mise run lint` | **FAIL** — `lint:gofmt` |
| `mise run lint` after local `gofmt -s -w` | **0 issues** — no substantive lint problems |
| `go test -race -count=1 ./cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/...` | all 4 packages `ok` (44.2s) |
| `go test -race -count=3 -run TestAddTaskMetadataToTree -v` | all PASS ×3 (12.2s) |
| `-race -count=2` full package | not run — pre-existing registry panic (see #2175) makes it meaningless |

### CONFIRMED findings
1. **The change is correct and does what it claims.** Verified the chunk arithmetic against `agent/chunking.go`: content is `"a"×(MaxChunkSize-1) + "\n" + "b\n"` = `MaxChunkSize+2` bytes, so `ChunkTranscript` (`chunking.go:25`) falls through to `ChunkJSONL`, which emits exactly **2 chunks** (`["a"×(M-1)", "b\n"]`). `createBlobFromContent` has exactly one call site — `ephemeral.go:442`, inside the chunk loop — so `calls == 2` is unambiguously the second *transcript chunk*, with the first already written for real. The old test wrote 15 bytes (1 chunk) and failed every write, so it could not distinguish "returns on first failure" from "returns on a mid-set hole". The critique in the PR body is factually correct and the fix genuinely closes it.

2. **`ephemeral_transcript_chunk_test.go:37` — heavy resource cost.** `MaxChunkSize` is 50 MB (`agent/chunking.go:16`). Measured:
   - runtime **0.15s → 2.7s** (3 runs at 0.15s baseline vs 2.67/2.73/4.71s after; 4.71s under `-race`)
   - **peak RSS 479 MB** for this single test (`/usr/bin/time -l`)

   The 50 MB string is copied several times (`strings.Repeat` → `[]byte` → `WriteFile` → `ReadFile` → `string()` → `strings.Builder`), and chunk 0 is written to the object store for real. The `checkpoint` package is already the slowest in the suite; under `-race` with cross-package parallelism this is a meaningful CI cost.

   Suggested cheaper shape: `agent.ChunkTranscript` hardcodes the constant, so 50 MB is currently the *only* way to force chunking through `addTaskMetadataToTree`. Making `MaxChunkSize` a `var` (or threading a chunk-size seam next to the existing `createBlobFromContent` seam) would let this test run at kilobyte scale. Worth raising with the #2167 author since the seam is theirs.

### SUSPECTED (latent, not introduced by this PR)
3. **The file's two tests have a fragile serial/parallel split.** `TestAddTaskMetadataToTree_PartialTranscriptChunkSetFails` mutates the package global `createBlobFromContent` (`:43-52`) and is *serial*; its sibling `TestAddTaskMetadataToTree_WritesEveryTranscriptChunk` (`:77`) is `t.Parallel()` and reads that same global through the production path. This is safe today only because Go holds all parallel top-level tests until every serial one has finished. If anyone applied #2175's "add `t.Parallel()` for consistency" reasoning to this file, it becomes an unsynchronized write/read of a package var across goroutines. I tried to reproduce it (`-race -count=8` with `t.Parallel()` added) and the detector did **not** fire — the windows didn't overlap in my runs — so this is SUSPECTED by mechanism, not demonstrated. Worth a `// deliberately serial: mutates createBlobFromContent` comment to make the constraint explicit, precisely because a consistency-sweep PR is in flight against the neighbouring file.

### Contributor claims
Every factual assertion in the PR body checks out: the old transcript was far below `MaxChunkSize` (15 bytes vs 50 MB), it did produce only one chunk, the override did fail every write, and the test could therefore pass while the real multi-chunk truncation case regressed.

### Good
This is the strongest of the three. It is a real gap in someone else's test, correctly diagnosed, with the chunker's line-splitting constraint understood (the comment about "no single line may exceed MaxChunkSize" is exactly right and is why the input is shaped as it is). Fail-the-second-call is the right instrument. Only gofmt and the resource cost stand between this and a merge.

## C) #2181 — `dispatch_wizard.go` accessible-mode validator — **MERGE AFTER FIXES**

One-line reason: correctly identifies and fixes a real accessible-mode defect — in fact more severe than the contributor claims — but fails gofmt and ships no test.

### Verification
| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | pass |
| `go vet ./cmd/entire/cli/` | pass |
| `mise run lint` | **FAIL** — `lint:gofmt` |
| `mise run lint` after local `gofmt -s -w` | **0 issues** |
| `go test -race -count=1 ./cmd/entire/cli/` | `ok` (138.8s) |
| `go test -race -count=2 -run Dispatch ./cmd/entire/cli/` | `ok` (4.5s) |

### CONFIRMED findings
1. **The claim is correct, and the bug is worse than stated — it is an unescapable prompt loop, not just "awkward".** Traced through huh v2.0.3:
   - `Form.runAccessible` (`form.go:721`) does `f.selector.Range(...)` over **every** group and never consults `group.hide`/`hideFunc`. So the existing code comment at `dispatch_wizard.go:296-301` is accurate, and the repo picker group — appended without a `WithHideFunc`, unlike its non-accessible twin — is always presented.
   - `MultiSelect.RunAccessible` (`field_multiselect.go:739`) is an unbounded `for {}`. The only exit is entering `0`, which is gated on `m.validate(...)`; a validation error prints and `continue`s. With `validateRepos` requiring ≥1 repo, an accessible-mode user who picked **Local** can never leave the prompt except by Ctrl-C.
   - Worst case: a local-only user with no cloud repos gets `len(options) == 0`, so `PromptInt` accepts only `0`, which always fails validation — a guaranteed infinite loop with no valid input at all. That is the exact user most likely to choose Local mode.

2. **The fix is semantically safe.** `dispatchWizardState.resolveCloudRepos()` (`dispatch_wizard.go:255`) already returns `nil` when `isLocal()`, so any repos selected in local mode are genuinely discarded — returning `nil` early from the validator cannot leak a selection into `opts.RepoPaths`. Non-accessible mode is unaffected (the group is hidden there, so `Validate` never runs, and if it did it now returns `nil` on a path that was previously unreachable). Cloud mode behaviour is byte-identical. Ordering is safe: Mode is group 1, repos group 2, and `runAccessible` is single-pass forward-only, so `state.modeChoice` is always set before the validator reads it.

3. **The added comment is factually accurate.** `--repos cannot be used with --local` is enforced at `cmd/entire/cli/dispatch/options.go:32`.

4. **No test.** `cmd/entire/cli/dispatch_wizard_test.go` has 25 tests, including `REDACTED` — testing exactly this class of behaviour is the file's established convention. `validateRepos` is a closure local to `runDispatchWizard`, so it is not reachable from a test as written; lifting it to a package-level `func validateDispatchWizardRepos(state *dispatchWizardState, value []string) error` would make it directly testable and is a small, contained refactor.

### Repo-convention checks
- **Agent-Safe CLI Fallbacks: no regression.** The change only affects an interactive wizard path and strictly *removes* a block. The non-interactive route (`entire dispatch --local ...`) is untouched, and the code comment correctly points at `entire dispatch --jurisdiction &lt;slug&gt; --repos &lt;slug&gt;` as the non-wizard path.
- **ACCESSIBLE=1: improved, not regressed.** The form still goes through `NewAccessibleForm` (`dispatch_wizard.go:401`), per CLAUDE.md.
- **#2132 (huh MultiSelect height) interaction: none.** #2132 concerns TUI viewport height; accessible mode renders no viewport (`printOptions` lists everything). The only `Height(0)` in this file is on the unrelated branch-mode `Select` at `:344`. No duplication or conflict.
- `logging` vs `fmt.Print*`, `git.PlainOpen`, `worktree.Status()`, `testutil.InitRepo` — all N/A; no such code touched.

### Residual issues the fix does *not* address (pre-existing in #2153, for that author)
- In accessible + Local mode the user is still **prompted** for repos, just no longer trapped. The prompt itself is dead UI.
- `dispatch_wizard.go:303` calls `loadScope()` eagerly inside the `IsAccessibleMode()` branch, so an accessible-mode **local** dispatch still pays the full cloud-catalogue load (auth + network) — the exact cost the non-accessible path's `OptionsFunc` was designed to avoid. Directly contradicts the intent of the contributor's own comment.

### Good
Genuinely valuable. The contributor read past the surface into huh's accessible runner semantics, spotted a mode-interaction bug the original author missed, and picked the only fix that actually works given that `WithHideFunc` is ignored in accessible mode. They also under-sold it — this is a hang, not an inconvenience.

## Verdict: **merge-after-fixes** — the completed list is still incomplete by exactly one entry (`ToolInvocationScanner`).

**Verification actuals** (worktree `.../scratchpad/rev-2028`):
- `go build ./...` — exit 0
- `mise run lint` — **0 issues**; gofmt clean (`gofmt -l` empty). No stray-leading-space breakage.
- `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/agent/...` — all 16 packages `ok` (incl. `agent`, `agent/external`)

### Ground truth

The exclusion set is derivable mechanically: an `As*` helper in `cmd/entire/cli/agent/capabilities.go` is **ungated** iff it resolves by bare type assertion (`builtinCapability[T]`, or an inline assert) rather than `declaredCapability[T]`. That is exactly 10 helpers.

| Capability | Ungated in reality? | OLD comment | NEW comment |
|---|---|---|---|
| `HookFreshness` (L75) | yes | ✗ | **✓ added** |
| `SidecarImageProvider` (L95) | yes (inline assert) | ✗ | **✓ added** |
| `TranscriptSanitizer` (L106) | yes | ✓ | ✓ |
| `TranscriptFetcher` (L136) | yes | ✓ | ✓ |
| `SubagentSessionResolver` (L200) | yes | ✗ | **✓ added** |
| `SessionBaseDirProvider` (L207) | yes | ✓ | ✓ |
| `ModelExtractor` (L215) | yes | ✓ | ✓ |
| `SkillEventExtractor` (L222) | yes | ✓ | ✓ |
| **`ToolInvocationScanner` (L230)** | **yes** | ✗ | **✗ STILL MISSING** |
| `SessionEndBudgeter` (L240) | yes | ✗ | **✓ added** |
| *(gated, correctly absent)* `HookSupport`, `TranscriptAnalyzer`, `TranscriptPreparer`, `TokenCalculator`, `TextGenerator`, `StreamingTextGenerator`, `TranscriptCompactor`, `HookResponseWriter`, `PromptExtractor`, `SubagentAwareExtractor` | no | — | — |

Old list: 5/10. New list: 9/10. Every entry the PR adds genuinely belongs; nothing incorrect was added. The one omission is real.

Telling detail: the new list is in **source-declaration order** with exactly one gap — `ToolInvocationScanner` sits at L230, between `SkillEventExtractor` and `SessionEndBudgeter`, the two entries it's listed between. The contributor walked the file top-to-bottom and skipped one line. `AsToolInvocationScanner` landed 2026-08-24 (`a1dd3efc3`, "review: measure used_search from tool invocations"), i.e. *after* the PR was opened but *before* its 2026-08-26 main merge — so the list was already stale again at the head commit. Fix is one word.

Also: the PR body says "omitted three interfaces" and names `HookFreshness`, `SidecarImageProvider`, `SubagentSessionResolver` (matching the review note at #1877 it cites). The diff adds a fourth, `SessionEndBudgeter`, un-mentioned. That's correct initiative beyond the ask, but body and diff disagree.

### Is the rationale accurate?

Yes. "Built-in-only capabilities that have no external-protocol equivalent" holds: `cmd/entire/cli/agent/external/capabilities.go:163-171` asserts `wrappedAgent` against exactly the 9 gated interfaces and none of the 10 ungated ones, and `docs/architecture/external-agent-protocol.md` documents no protocol field for any of them. `AsSessionEndBudgeter`'s own comment even pre-registers the widening path ("Widen DeclaredCaps with a `session_end_budget` field when…").

One nit the PR inherits and slightly worsens: the trailing "(see `builtinCapability`)" now points at a helper that `AsSidecarImageProvider` does **not** call — L95-102 inlines its own assertion. Functionally identical, so "resolve by type assertion alone" stays true, but the cross-reference no longer covers every listed entry. Same was already true of `AsStreamingTextGenerator` on the gated side.

### Load-bearing? Duplicated?

**Not load-bearing, and not duplicated.** Nothing reads this list: no test, no lint rule, no `agent-help` output. `docs/architecture/agent-guide.md` has an optional-interface table but it does not mirror this exclusion set (it lists `SubagentSessionResolver` and `FileWatcher` among gated ones, with no gated/ungated distinction at all), and `agent-integration-checklist.md` doesn't reference it. Repo-wide grep for `SessionBaseDirProvider` in `*.md` returns nothing. So no copies are made inconsistent by this PR.

Its real audience is a human adding a new capability who needs to know whether to add a `DeclaredCaps` field — which is exactly the case where a missing entry causes a later bug, so completeness does matter.

### Drift prevention — the more valuable feedback

The list is hand-maintained with no sync mechanism, and it has now rotted twice (5→9 of 10, stale again within two days of being written). **All 10 ungated helpers already carry their own, more detailed rationale comment** — `AsHookFreshness` explains hook-config drift being template-based, `AsSessionEndBudgeter` explains the parse-hook/in-process split, and so on. The aggregate list is 100% duplication of information that already lives next to the code that defines it, which is why it rots.

Two durable options, in order of preference:

1. **Stop enumerating.** Replace the parenthetical list with a pointer: "…are intentionally excluded — their `As*` helpers resolve by type assertion alone (`builtinCapability`), each documenting its own reason at the helper." Zero maintenance, no information lost, drift surface eliminated.
2. **Make it testable.** A `go/ast` test over `capabilities.go` that classifies each `func As*` by whether its body calls `declaredCapability` vs `builtinCapability`/inline assert, then asserts the ungated set equals the identifiers named in `DeclaredCaps`'s doc comment. ~40 lines, single-file parse, and it fails the moment someone adds capability #11.

If the maintainers want the list kept, option 2 is what makes this fix the last one needed.

### What's genuinely good

- Correctly diagnosed a real, verifiable defect and did not invent entries — all four additions check out against the source.
- Went **beyond** the review note it was following: `SessionEndBudgeter` was not in the ask and is a legitimate find.
- Preserved declaration order rather than reshuffling, which keeps the diff reviewable and makes the remaining gap easy to spot.
- Genuinely zero-risk: comment-only, no field, wrapper, schema, or runtime change — the PR body's "API Notes: none" is accurate.
- Clean build/lint/test, correct conventional-commit subject, and it cited its source. For a first-pass doc correction from an external contributor this is well above the median.

**Ask before merge:** add `ToolInvocationScanner` between `SkillEventExtractor` and `SessionEndBudgeter`, and reconcile the PR body's "three interfaces" with the four actually added.</result>

<result># Review: entireio/cli#2012 — `fix(checkpoint): preserve newer same-ref queue entries`

## Verdict
**needs-different-fix** — the diagnosis is right and the regression test is genuine, but the fix breaks an existing green test on main (CI would be red) and does not fix the concurrency case that issue #2008 actually names as the trigger.

**Base branch: `main`** (`baseRefName: main`; 1 real commit `1136212e0` + 6 merge-from-main commits, no feature-branch shenanigans).

## Build / lint / test actuals
Isolated worktree at `.../scratchpad/rev-2012`, merged current `origin/main` (`e3437fd56`).

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | pass |
| `mise run lint` | **0 issues**; gofmt/gomod/shellcheck/mise all clean |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/strategy/...` | **ok** (63s) |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/` | **ok** (89s) |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/...` | **FAIL** — `TestMigrateBranchToRefs_ReenqueuesAlreadyImportedRef` |
| `go test -race -count=1 ./cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/...` | same single failure, **no data races** |

**Red/green: PASSES.** Reverting only `pushqueue.go` to `origin/main` while keeping the new test:
- `TestPushQueue_RemovePreservesNewerSameRefEntry` → **FAIL** (proves the defect and that the test is a real regression test, not a tautology).
- `TestMigrateBranchToRefs_ReenqueuesAlreadyImportedRef` → **PASS** (proves the failure is caused by this PR, not pre-existing).

Restored; `git status --porcelain` clean.

## Does the defect exist on main? **CONFIRMED**
`Remove` on main (`pushqueue.go:141-156`) reads through `readLocked`, which **de-duplicates**, then drops every entry whose ref name is in the drained set. The queue entry is `pushQueueEntry{Ref string}` (`pushqueue.go:29-31`) — **ref name only, no hash, no generation, no timestamp**. So gen1 and gen2 of the same ref are byte-identical lines and indistinguishable.

Interleaving (all three `Remove` call sites in `manual_commit_push.go:337-429` sit after one `Drain`):
1. `setRef` writes checkpoint ref A→gen1, `enqueueForPush` (`refs_store.go:207-236`) → file `[A]`.
2. Pre-push `flushCheckpointRefsQueue` → `Drain()` returns `[A]`, file still `[A]`.
3. `batchPushRefs` in flight (`push_common.go:58`); remote gets gen1.
4. Concurrent write advances A→gen2, `Enqueue(A)` → file `[A, A]`.
5. `queue.Remove(existing)` (`manual_commit_push.go:381`) deletes **all** A → file empty.

Result: remote=gen1, local=gen2, queue empty, `CountUnpushedCheckpoints` (`unpushed_checkpoints.go:31-46`) reports 0. Exactly as the issue states.

## Is the fix correct?
The fix converts `Remove` to a **multiset removal**: read raw (non-deduped) entries, remove one occurrence per drained ref. It works for the single-flusher case because `Drain` guarantees a useful invariant — `readLocked` compacts whenever `rawLines &gt; len(refs)`, and duplicates strictly imply that, so **after any `Drain` the file holds exactly one line per drained ref**. Removing one is then correct.

But positional/counting matching is the wrong invariant. The right one is **"drop the entry only if the object I published is still the local tip"** — a compare-and-swap on the ref value, not a count. That is cheap here: `partitionLocalRefs` (`push_common.go:29-45`) already calls `repo.Reference(ref, false)` and **throws the hash away with `_`**. Snapshot those hashes, pass them to `Remove`, and drop an entry only when the local ref still equals its snapshot. That fixes the single-flusher case, fixes the multi-flusher case below, and leaves `Remove`'s "this ref is gone" contract intact so the migrate test stays green. (`batchPushRefs` pushes `ref:ref`, so git re-resolves the ref itself; a snapshot taken just before the push is conservative in the safe direction — a ref that advanced in that sub-millisecond window merely causes one idempotent re-push.)

**Recommend: exact-identity (hash) matching, not counting.**

## Findings, most severe first

**1. CONFIRMED — breaks an existing test; CI would be red.** `pushqueue.go:141` + `migrate_test.go:458`
`MigrateBranchToRefs` deliberately double-enqueues every ref: `setRef`'s best-effort `enqueueForPush` (`refs_store.go:210`) plus migrate's guaranteed `queue.Enqueue` (`migrate.go:128`, `migrate.go:146`), both commented *"duplicates collapse on Drain."* After a migration with no intervening `Drain`, the file holds **2 lines per ref**. `Remove([ref])` now deletes only one, so the ref stays queued. Verified directly — a probe enqueuing twice then calling `Remove` leaves `[refs/entire/checkpoints/f6/a1b2c3d4e5f6]`, 1 raw line. The contributor only ran `-run TestPushQueue`, so they never saw it. This is a genuine contract break, not just a stale expectation: `Remove` is exported and documented as deleting the given refs.

**2. CONFIRMED — does not fix the concurrency case #2008 is actually about.** `pushqueue.go:131-157`
The queue lives in the **git common dir** (`pushqueue.go:18-26`), so every linked worktree shares one queue — exactly what the issue calls out ("reachable when concurrent processes or linked worktrees share the Git common directory"). Two concurrent flushers each `Drain()` (which is not a reservation — it doesn't remove), both get `[A]`; a write during the pushes enqueues A; both `Remove([A])` fire, each consuming one occurrence. Probe result: **queue empty, newer generation lost again.** The counting scheme is only correct while exactly one flusher is outstanding. Hash-CAS removal fixes this too.

**3. CONFIRMED — stale doc comment.** `pushqueue.go:160-162`
`rewriteLocked` still says it writes refs *"(de-duplicated, one line each)"*. `Remove`'s `kept` slice can now contain duplicates and they are all written out — probe: 5 enqueues during a push leave **5 raw lines** after `Remove`. This repo holds comment accuracy to a high bar; it should be corrected.

**4. SUSPECTED, low severity — memory shape.** `pushqueue.go:195-240`
`readEntriesWithRawLinesLocked` now retains every duplicate line in the returned slice where main deduped during the scan. Bounded by writes-between-drains, so not a practical concern — but it interacts with the pre-existing (not introduced here) fact that `Drain` is the sole compaction point, so with pushing disabled the file is never compacted at all.

**Not a leak.** I checked for unbounded growth and found none: `Drain` re-compacts on the next flush (probe: 5 raw lines → 1 after the next `Drain`), the retained entry drains and is removed on the following cycle, and `Peek` still de-dupes so `entire status` counts stay correct. The failure mode is at most one extra idempotent ref in the next push batch.

**Conventions:** clean. No `fmt.Print*` misuse, no user content logged, no `git.PlainOpen`, no `worktree.Status()`, new test uses `t.Parallel()` and `t.TempDir()`.

## Does it close #2008?
**Partially.** The single-flusher interleaving in the issue's "Steps to reproduce" Go snippet is fixed. The scenario the issue gives as the *reason it matters* — concurrent processes / linked worktrees sharing the git common dir — is **not** fixed (finding 2). Merging this and closing #2008 would leave the motivating case live.

## What's genuinely good
The diagnosis is accurate and well-localized — the contributor correctly identified that `Drain` snapshots without reserving and that `Remove` matches on ref name alone, and cited the right file and the right downstream call site. The regression test is a real red/green test (verified), is written in the repo's style with `t.Parallel()` and the local `mustRefName` helper, and slots into the existing `TestPushQueue_*` group next to the `RemovePreservesLaterEntries` test it correctly distinguishes itself from. The refactor into `readEntriesWithRawLinesLocked` / `readEntriesLocked` / `readLocked` preserves `rawLines` semantics exactly, so `Drain`'s compaction trigger is unchanged — confirmed by the compaction tests still passing. Doc comments were updated alongside the behavior change (just not `rewriteLocked`'s). The PR body is honest about what wasn't run. This is careful work from someone without the repo's tooling; the gap is scope (multi-process) and the one test they couldn't have known to run.</result>

# Review: entireio/cli#1926 — `fix(checkpoint): preserve concurrent persistent ref updates`

## Verdict: **merge-after-fixes** (one small correctness fix; the design is right and the red/green proof is solid)

The retry rebuilds from a fresh tip, it uses native `git update-ref` with a real old-value, it covers all four writer families, it cleans up losing objects, and the `shadow_ref.go` extraction is byte-for-byte behavior-preserving. One real defect: loose-object cleanup fires on error paths where the object may still be live.

## Build / lint / test actuals

Worktree at `/private/tmp/claude-501/.../scratchpad/rev-1926`, PR head `45afce62c` merged with `origin/main` (clean merge).

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | PASS |
| `mise run lint` | **0 issues**; `gofmt -l` clean |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/... ./cmd/entire/cli/strategy/... ./cmd/entire/cli/` | PASS (all 6 packages) |
| `mise run test:integration` | **PASS — 564 tests, 3 skipped, 65s** |
| `go test -race -count=1 ./cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/...` | PASS (4 packages, no race reports) |

**Red/green — the test flips.** Reverted `cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/` to `origin/main` (deleting the two new files), kept the PR's `integration_test/`:

- **RED**: `-count=3` → **6/6 concurrent subtests FAIL** across both backends with the intended message (`LOST UPDATE: session X's post-commit transcript content is absent…`). All 6 sequential controls PASS. This is not the trap Soph warned about.
- **GREEN**: restored → **12/12 PASS** at `-count=3`, plus again in the full suite.
- Tree restored; `git status --porcelain` empty; rebuild OK.

## Answers

**1. Rebuild or re-point? → REBUILD. CONFIRMED.**
`store.go:115-123` puts `getSessionsBranchRef()` *inside* the builder closure, so every attempt re-reads the tip and passes the fresh `rootTreeHash` into `build`, returning `(newHash, parentHash)` where `parentHash` is that attempt's freshly-read expected-old. Same shape at `refs_store.go:225-247` (`base()` re-runs per attempt) and `migrate.go:108-145` (re-reads parent *and* re-evaluates `treeInRefHistory` per attempt). The loop at `persistent_ref_update.go:94-95` calls `build()` at the top of each iteration — nothing built is carried across attempts. `persistent_ref_update_test.go:41` (`TestUpdatePersistentRef_RebuildsAfterCASConflict`) pins it: it asserts `seenParents == []{initial, externalTip}` and that the final tree contains `attempt-2`, not `attempt-1`.

**2. `CheckAndSetReference`? → NOT USED. CONFIRMED.**
`grep` over `cmd/` `internal/`: zero hits in the checkpoint package outside the explanatory comment at `shadow_ref.go:53`. It uses `exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "update-ref", refName, newValue, oldValue)` at `persistent_ref_update.go:47`. The old-value **is** passed and **is** checked: `persistent_ref_update.go:41-45` mirrors `newHash.HexSize()` zeros for the must-not-exist case (SHA-1/SHA-256 correct) and uses `expectedHash.String()` otherwise; conflict is classified from git's own stderr at `:57`. The red run proves git enforces it.

**3. Cross-process locking — mostly CONFIRMED, one gap.**
- *Location*: `filepath.Join(commonDir, "entire-persistent-ref-locks")` (`:63`), where `commonDir` comes from `resolveGitCommonDir` (`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`, memoized). **Correctly shared across worktrees.**
- *Scoping*: per-ref. git-refs → one lock per checkpoint ref (fine-grained). git-branch → `refs/heads/entire/checkpoints/v1` (`persistent_refs.go:29-36`), i.e. **one repo-global lock for every checkpoint write**. The author discloses this in the PR body.
- *Lock ordering / deadlock*: no path holds two persistent-ref locks. `enqueueForPush` (which takes the push-queue flock) runs *after* `updatePersistentRef` returns (`refs_store.go:241-246`, `migrate.go:150`), so persistent-ref ↔ push-queue are never nested. `ensureSessionsBranch` (`persistent.go:2114`) acquires and releases *before* `updatePrimaryRef`, not inside it. Build callbacks write no refs (checked `applySessionWrite`, `maybeMergeVercelConfig`). **No deadlock today** — but see finding D.
- *Dead holder*: flock is fd-held, released by the kernel on process death. **No stale-lock hang.**
- *Release on every path*: `defer release()` at `persistent_ref_update.go:79`; `release` only assigned on success. CONFIRMED.

**4. Both backends + init + migration → CONFIRMED, all four.**
git-branch writes: `persistent.go:83, 848, 1706, 1748` (writeSession + 3 backfills) via `updatePrimaryRef`. git-refs writes: `refs_store.go` writeSession/backfillTranscript/backfillSummary/backfillAttribution via `updateCheckpointRef`. Init: `ensureSessionsBranch` `persistent.go:2123`, with a re-check inside the lock for the "another process created it first" case. Migration: `migrate.go:108`. `setRef`/`setPrimaryRef` are **deleted** — no unconditional `Storer.SetReference` remains in non-test checkpoint code.

**5. Loose-object cleanup → CONFIRMED present, and the #2131 comparison holds.**
`persistent_ref_update.go:105` calls `tryDeleteLooseObject`, guarded by `newHash != expectedHash` so a no-op CAS never deletes a live object (pinned by `TestUpdatePersistentRef_NoOpConflictKeepsExistingCommit`). I checked #2131 directly: `tryDeleteLooseObject` appears only in `ephemeral.go` (2 calls) and `shadow_ref.go` (definition) — its new `ref_transaction.go` has **zero** calls. **#2131 does lack it.**

**6. `shadow_ref.go` extraction → behavior-preserving. CONFIRMED.**
`repoDirs` becomes a one-line delegate to an identical `repositoryDirs`. `casUpdateShadowBranchRef` becomes `casUpdateRef(ctx, repoRoot, plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchName), …)` — `NewBranchReferenceName(b)` is exactly `"refs/heads/"+b`, so the argv, the `LC_ALL=C LANG=C` env, `CombinedOutput`, the `"cannot lock ref" || "but expected"` classification, `ErrShadowRefBusy`, and the wrapped-error text are all identical. Retry count (`shadowRefMaxRetries`), `shadowRefBackoff`, `withShadowBranchFlock` (still unbounded `flock.Acquire`), and ephemeral's own cleanup are untouched — **`ephemeral.go` is not in the diff at all**. Ephemeral tests pass under `-race`.

**7. Retry budget / failure mode → fail-closed at the store, fail-open at the hook. Correct.**
Exhaustion logs WARN and returns a wrapped `ErrShadowRefBusy` (`persistent_ref_update.go:114-121`) — the store fails closed, no silent drop. The Stop hook then swallows it: `finalizeAllTurnCheckpoints` is documented best-effort and `HandleTurnEnd` (`manual_commit_hooks.go:2904-2916`) only logs `error_count`, hook exits 0. So capture degrades rather than aborting, which is CLAUDE.md's stated design. Worth knowing the residual: an exhausted budget produces the *same* user-visible symptom as the bug (missing finalize, exit 0) — but only 16 consecutive losses to a non-Entire writer get you there, and unlike the bug it leaves a WARN in `.entire/logs/`.

**8. Does the test reproduce #1917's real trigger? → YES. CONFIRMED.**
It is Soph's #1920 test, byte-identical except em-dashes rewritten as hyphens in three comment/message strings (diffed against `pull/1920/head`). It models exactly the described scenario: two sessions in one worktree (`NewSession` ×2), both write files, one `GitCommitWithShadowHooksAsAgent` producing **one** checkpoint asserted to hold ≥2 sessions, then two *real* `entire hooks claude-code stop` **subprocesses** (`stopHookCmd`) barrier-started together — not bare goroutines, and not a hand-authored interleave. Both markers must survive; sequential is the control.

## Findings, most severe first

**A. `tryDeleteLooseObject` runs on hard errors, not just CAS conflicts — can delete a live commit.** `persistent_ref_update.go:105` deletes *before* the `!errors.Is(refErr, ErrShadowRefBusy)` check at `:107`. The ephemeral path it was modelled on does the opposite: `ephemeral.go:158` checks busy first, `:163` deletes (same at `:339`/`:342`). Two concrete ways this bites: (i) `exec.CommandContext` kills `git update-ref` *after* it renamed the lock file into place — ref now points at `newHash`, exit code non-zero, output empty, so it's classified non-busy and we delete the object the ref points at; (ii) a future git wording change breaks the `"cannot lock ref"` substring match, turning every genuine CAS conflict into a hard error that deletes the *winner's* object when hashes coincide. Result is a checkpoint ref pointing at a missing commit — worse than the bug being fixed. Fix is two lines: move the delete below the busy check, matching `ephemeral.go`.

**B. Migration re-runs now pay a flock + fork/exec per already-imported checkpoint.** Old code short-circuited at `migrate.go:94` (`alreadyImported` → `Enqueue`, `Skipped++`, return) before touching any ref. New code always enters `updatePersistentRef` (`:108`) and does a no-op `git update-ref &lt;ref&gt; H H` even when `treeInRefHistory` hits at `:130`. On a repo with thousands of migrated checkpoints an idempotent `MigrateBranchToRefs` re-run now costs thousands of extra lock acquisitions and git subprocesses. Cheap fix: keep a `treeInRefHistory` fast path *outside* the transaction; the in-transaction re-check already makes it race-safe.

**C. Lock files accumulate unboundedly and nothing cleans them.** `persistentRefLockPath` (`:62-70`) `MkdirAll`s and creates one file per ref under `&lt;git-common-dir&gt;/entire-persistent-ref-locks/`. Under git-refs that is **one file per checkpoint, forever**; the migration in (B) creates N of them in one pass. `strategy/cleanup.go` references neither this dir nor the pre-existing `entire-shadow-locks`. Precedent exists, but the shadow one is bounded by base commits while this one is bounded by checkpoint count. Follow-up, not a blocker.

**D. Sharp edge: nesting `updatePersistentRef` would hang a hook forever.** `withPersistentRefFlock` uses `flock.AcquireContext` (`:76`); I found no `context.WithTimeout` on any checkpoint-write hook path, so it takes the unbounded blocking kernel branch. Two `flock(2)` calls in one process use separate file descriptions and therefore block each other. Nothing nests today (verified above), but a future refactor that calls `ensureSessionsBranch`, or any store write, from inside a build callback deadlocks with no timeout. One doc line on `updatePersistentRef` ("never call from within a builder") would close it.

**E. git-branch now serializes every checkpoint write repo-wide, with an unbounded wait.** Correct trade — correctness over latency, and git-refs is unaffected — but combined with the known ~3s p50 condensation, two concurrent turn-ends now add rather than overlap. Disclosed in the PR body; naming it so it isn't a surprise in hook-latency telemetry.

**F. Minor: docs lost in the hoist.** The "why shell out / why not `CheckAndSetReference`" rationale and the SHA-1-vs-SHA-256 zero-OID-width explanation stayed on the thin wrapper at `shadow_ref.go:41-55` and are absent from `casUpdateRef` (`:38-39`), which is now the function anyone reads first.

**G. Minor/latent: `repositoryDirs` calls `repo.Worktree()`, so persistent writes now require a non-bare repo** where `setPrimaryRef` worked anywhere. No production path constructs a writing `GitStore` on a bare repo (`registry.go:151`, `migrate.go:51`, `benchutil`), so latent only.

**H. Trivial:** lock-name escaping (`:67`) collapses `/` to `_`, so `…/ab/cd` and `…/ab_cd` would share a lock. Unreachable with hex/ULID shards; same weakness as `shadowBranchLockPath`.

**Conventions (9):** clean. No `git.PlainOpen` outside `gitrepo`, no `worktree.Status()`, `logging.Warn` (not `fmt.Print*`) for the exhaustion path, no user content logged (ref name + retry count only), unit tests use `t.Parallel()` and `setupBranchTestRepo`, integration test uses `NewFeatureBranchEnv` + `testutil.GitIsolatedEnv()`. The unrelated `setup_test.go` `.exe` suffix hunk is scope creep but is a genuine Windows fix from a Windows contributor.

## Does it fully close #1917?

**Yes for the defect as filed and as reproduced.** Both refs named in #1920 — `refs_store.go:206` and `store.go:91` — are gone, and the reachable trigger no longer loses data on either backend.

**No for the whole ref-clobber class on those same refs**, and the author says so explicitly in the PR body. `strategy` still writes them outside the new lock: `manual_commit_opf_rewrite.go:815` CAS-updates `refs/heads/entire/checkpoints/v1` and `manual_commit_opf_refs.go:169` CAS-updates the per-checkpoint refs — both via go-git's `CheckAndSetReference`, which is precisely the mechanism that does *not* interoperate with native git's `.lock` files. So a pre-push OPF rewrite racing a post-commit condensation is still unserialized. Soph already accepted this as follow-up scope in her review; #2131 is where it gets addressed.

## Landing order vs #2131

**Land #1926 first.** It is 731 lines against #2131's 3,907; it fixes a data-loss bug rather than bundling it with a batching perf refactor; it carries the flipping integration test; and it has the loose-object cleanup #2131 lacks. #2131 then rebases onto it — the two overlap heavily (`persistent.go`, `refs_store.go`, `migrate.go`, `store.go`, `shadow_ref.go`) and #2131's author has to reconcile in either order, but rebasing a large refactor onto a landed small fix is the cheaper direction, and it means the data-loss bug is fixed on `main` in days rather than behind a 3.9k-line review. Two things to carry into #2131's review: it drops the flock in favour of pure CAS+retry plus a process-local mutex, so it should be checked for the losing-object leak; and it is the PR that actually covers the `strategy`-side writers this one leaves open.

## What's genuinely good

This is a strong external contribution. The contributor did exactly what the maintainer asked, including the two things easiest to get wrong: retry rebuilds from the fresh tip (not re-points), and no `CheckAndSetReference`. He built on Soph's #1920 test rather than his own — which was the right call and the harder one, since his original test passed by observing the bug. He extracted the shared helper without disturbing the working ephemeral path. The unit tests are well-chosen: `NoOpConflictKeepsExistingCommit` specifically pins the `newHash != expectedHash` cleanup guard, which is the subtle part. The `ensureSessionsBranch` re-check inside the lock shows he thought about the create race, and the `migrate.go` `ErrObjectNotFound` branch CASes against the real ref hash while re-orphaning — a detail easy to fumble. He responded to review within the thread, addressed both items, and wrote a PR description that volunteers his own limitations (git-branch lock granularity, cancellation semantics, `strategy`-side writes still unguarded) instead of hiding them. Also a nice unremarked side-effect: routing v1 through native `git update-ref` means those updates are now reflogged, so #1920's "losing commit is un-reflogged and gc-able" caveat no longer holds for git-branch.

Given fork CI has never run, all of the above is from a local run: **build, lint (0 issues), unit, `-race`, and the full 564-test integration suite are green, and the regression test is verified red without the fix.**</result>
