## The core of the review: every pinned vector, derived independently

Derivations use `shasum -a 256` and `openssl dgst -sha256` on the raw bytes — never the Go code. All eight match.

### #2170 — `GetProjectHash` = `hex(sha256(raw bytes))` (`geminicli/gemini.go:205-208`)

| input | pinned value | `printf %s '&lt;in&gt;' \| shasum -a 256` | |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/Users/test/project` | `0654434d556baf695ffd946d23555e4f45e1ae867116ed11c5adca8fd5998a60` | identical | **MATCH** |
| `/Users/test/other` | `4fd9d0d2d64802efa27aad63ba36371dc2435256f5c3973d797c705e215239f7` | identical | **MATCH** |
| `` (empty) | `e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852b855` | identical (SHA-256 of zero bytes) | **MATCH** |
| `/repo` | `816fc349d3faebf805d1bed70fce7e14754cad5251c77dda31c414ee961a0bdd` | identical | **MATCH** |

`openssl` agrees with `shasum` on all four.

### #2174 — `HashWorktreeID` = `hex(sha256(raw bytes))[:6]` (`checkpoint/ephemeral.go:48-51`)

| input | pinned | full digest | first 6 | |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `` (main worktree) | `e3b0c4` | `e3b0c442…b855` | `e3b0c4` | **MATCH** |
| `test-123` | `37eb19` | `37eb1918…da32` | `37eb19` | **MATCH** |
| `feature/auth-system` | `16b0aa` | `16b0aa95…e286` | `16b0aa` | **MATCH** |
| `wt` | `a721db` | `a721dbc8…2851` | `a721db` | **MATCH** |

Composed branch names check out against the documented format `entire/&lt;HEAD-commit[:7]&gt;-&lt;worktreeHash[:6]&gt;`:
- `entire/abc1234-e3b0c4` — `abc1234567890`[:7] = `abc1234` ✓, hash 6 chars ✓
- `entire/abc1234-37eb19` ✓
- `entire/abc-a721db` — commit `abc` is shorter than 7, passed through unmodified, matching `ShadowBranchNameForCommit`'s guard. Correctly documents the short-hash edge case.

---

## Does #2170 cement an Entire-side bug? No — verified against the real thing

This was the biggest risk, and it is settled empirically rather than by reading docs. This machine has a real Gemini CLI (`/opt/homebrew/bin/gemini`, v0.46.0) with 24 hash-named project dirs under `~/.gemini/tmp/`, created by Gemini itself across several versions (chat files contain an in-band `0.28.2 → 0.29.7` update banner, and the newest dirs are dated 2026-02-24).

I brute-forced 18,557 candidate filesystem paths against those 24 directory names. **Four exact matches:**

```
2517dc1021d4ce2aab466368da05a3604d21d674bd6b04635c64bdb4422bd51f &lt;- /Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli
d1bd8fd5b01a0323e5f3a61aec5f5213fbce147d2a3a7547ca69c9a826c77543 &lt;- /Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/entire.io
241d6c19403fd81a0d5f6bd41f9ad4edc44ee45e4783b306b05cfba3cc3a870b &lt;- /Users/soph/Work/entire/test/gemini_test
354ffe8947db533afd645888b263323750b95bfc36c398d816a66149263722b8 &lt;- /Users/soph/Work/entire/test/test-multi
```

Directories the real Gemini CLI created are exactly `sha256(absolute path)` hex — no prefix, no suffix, **no trailing separator**, no normalization. Each also contains the `chats/` subdirectory the impl expects at `gemini.go:126`. So Entire's derivation matches upstream, and the vectors pin the correct value. The KAT does not cement a bug.

Two things I checked and dismissed:
- One dir is slug-named (`-Users-soph-Work-entire-test-test-multi`, dated 2026-02-10). It is *older* than the hash-named dirs, and the same path also has a correct hash-named dir. Hash naming is the current behaviour; the slug is not produced by any Go code in this repo. Not a threat to the vectors.
- Chat JSON carries an explicit `"projectHash": "d9324b39…"` field matching its own directory name, confirming Gemini treats this as its project identity.

**Out of scope but worth knowing:** the vectors pin the *transform*, not the *argument*. Production passes `repoRoot` (`transcript.go:35`, `resume.go:922`, `manual_commit_pending.go:407/643`) while Gemini hashes its own target dir. If an agent is launched from a subdirectory, the two diverge and Entire reads an empty directory — the exact failure the PR describes, via a path the KAT cannot see. Correctly not cemented by this PR; just still untested.

---

## Mutate-and-confirm-red

All mutations applied to the production source, tests run, source restored (`git status` clean afterward in both worktrees).

**#2170** — in every case the pre-existing property test `TestGetProjectHash` stayed **green**, confirming the PR's premise that it is blind:

| mutation | new KAT |
|---|---|
| `sha256([]byte(projectRoot + "/"))` | **RED** — all 4 vectors fail |
| `strings.ToUpper(hex.EncodeToString(...))` | **RED** — all 4 vectors fail |
| `sha256([]byte(filepath.Clean(projectRoot)))` | **RED** — but *only* the `empty path` vector fails |

That third result is the interesting one: it confirms the empty-string vector is load-bearing and is the sole guard against a normalizing change (`Clean("")` = `"."`, while `Clean` is a no-op on the other three inputs). The test comment claims exactly this and is accurate.

**#2174:**

| mutation | result |
|---|---|
| `sha256([]byte("wt:" + worktreeID))` | **RED** — 3/3 `TestHashWorktreeID` subtests + 3/3 `TestShadowBranchNameForCommit` subtests. `_Deterministic` and `_DifferentInputs` stayed green, confirming the blind spot. |
| `WorktreeIDHashLength` 6→8 | **RED** — caught by the value vectors |
| `ShadowBranchHashLength` 7→8 | **RED** — 2/3 `TestShadowBranchNameForCommit` subtests (the `abc` case is shorter than either length, so it correctly still passes) |

---

## #2174: what the 9 deleted lines covered

Nothing was lost. Breakdown:

- 6 lines from `TestHashWorktreeID`: the `wantLen int` field, three `wantLen: 6` entries, and the 2-line length assertion. The length check was **kept**, retargeted at the `WorktreeIDHashLength` constant.
- 3 lines from `TestShadowBranchNameForCommit`: the `want: "entire/abc1234-" + HashWorktreeID("")` style expectations, replaced by literals.

Both property tests survive as separate top-level functions (`TestHashWorktreeID_Deterministic:67`, `TestHashWorktreeID_DifferentInputs:77`), as do `TestParseShadowBranchName` and the `TestParseShadowBranchName_RoundTrip` inverse check. Strictly stronger.

One nit: the retained length assertion is now vacuous — `len(got) != WorktreeIDHashLength` compares the output against the very constant the function truncates with, so it can never fire. The old `wantLen: 6` literal at least could. It is harmless because the 6-char value vectors catch a length change (verified above), but the line now earns nothing; either drop it or restore a literal `6`.

---

## Platform independence

Both are clean, and this matters because `ci.yml:71` runs a `windows-latest` job.

- Both new test blocks use **only string literals** — no `filepath.Join`, no `t.TempDir()`, no `os.Getwd()`, no `runtime.GOOS`, no separators. (`grep` over the new #2170 block returns only the word "filepath" inside a comment; `ephemeral_test.go` has zero such constructs anywhere.)
- Both functions under test are pure byte-level transforms with no OS-dependent behaviour.
- `GOOS=windows go build ./...` and `go vet` on both packages: clean.

No config/cache/keyring exposure either — neither test touches `~/.config/entire`, `~/.cache/entire`, or the keyring; both are pure-function tests with no filesystem or env access at all.

## Conventions

- **#2170** follows the repo `t.Parallel()` convention on both the parent and subtests.
- **#2174** does **not** use `t.Parallel()` — but that is pre-existing (`ephemeral_test.go` has zero `t.Parallel()` calls across all six of its test functions), and the PR neither introduced nor worsened it. Worth adding opportunistically since these functions are pure, but not a blocker for a test-only PR that didn't touch that aspect.
- Test naming, table-driven style, and error-message style all match surrounding code.

## What's genuinely good

- Both PRs identified a real, specific blind spot and proved it by mutation rather than asserting it — and my independent mutations reproduce their claims exactly, including the subtle one (only the empty-string vector catches `filepath.Clean`).
- The vector choices are deliberate, not decorative. Each of the four #2170 inputs guards a distinct failure mode, and the empty-string case is the only thing standing between the repo and a silently-normalizing change.
- #2174 correctly identifies that `want: "entire/abc1234-" + HashWorktreeID("")` compared the function against itself, and that the integration suite is not a backstop because it routes through `ShadowBranchNameForCommit` too. Both claims verified.
- The comments explain *consequence* (orphaned checkpoints, empty session directory) rather than restating the code, and both include a reproduction command plus an explicit "a failure means a regression or a verified upstream change — never a value to paste over from the output." That instruction is the thing that stops the next person from doing precisely the damage this review was looking for.
- Assertion failure messages carry the operational meaning, so a CI failure is self-explaining without opening the file.

## Optional follow-ups (neither blocks merge)

1. **#2174**: drop or de-vacuum the `len(got) != WorktreeIDHashLength` check; add `t.Parallel()` while in the file.
2. **#2170**: `TestGetProjectHash` is now fully subsumed by the KAT (determinism, 64-char length, and distinct-inputs all follow from two distinct literal vectors). Harmless, but it is now dead weight.
3. **#2170**: consider a vector with a Windows-form path (`C:\repo`) and a non-ASCII path, to pin the UTF-8 encoding assumption that `crypto.createHash('sha256').update(str)` on Gemini's side matches Go's `[]byte(string)`. The current vectors are all ASCII, so the "different encoding" failure mode named in the comment is not actually covered.</result>

# Review: PRs #2169 and #2172 (suhaanthayyil)

Both PRs base on **`main`**. No GitHub state was touched — read-only, local analysis only.

---

## PR #2169 — `test(summarize): stop the nil-generator test launching the real claude CLI`

**Verdict: MERGE.** The reported bug is real and demonstrated; the fix injects a stub rather than skipping, and turns a test that pinned nothing into one that pins the actual contract.

Base branch: `main` · Branch: `audit/summarize-test-spawns-real-claude` · +34/−6, 1 file

### Actuals (worktree = PR merged with `origin/main`)

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | clean |
| `go vet -tags=e2e ./e2e/...` | clean |
| `gofmt -l cmd/entire/cli/summarize/` | no output |
| `mise run lint` | **0 issues** (gofmt/go/shellcheck/gomod/mise all pass) |
| `go test -race -count=1 ./cmd/entire/cli/summarize` | `ok` 4.69s |
| `go test -shuffle=on` ×3 | `ok` each time |

### CONFIRMED: the real `claude` is spawned on `main`, and is not after the PR

Method: a fake `claude` on `PATH` that appends its argv to a log and exits 1. Reachable because `claudecode/generate_streaming.go:53` invokes the bare name `claude` (`commandRunner(ctx, "claude", …)`), resolved via `PATH`.

**On `main`** (`go test -run TestGenerateFromTranscript_NilGenerator ./cmd/entire/cli/summarize`):

```
--- PASS: TestGenerateFromTranscript_NilGenerator (0.26s)
FAKE CLAUDE INVOKED: args=--print --output-format stream-json --include-partial-messages --verbose --model sonnet --setting-sources
cwd=/private/var/folders/.../T HOME=/Users/soph
```

**On the PR**: 0 invocations, for the single test and for the whole package.

I widened the sweep: on `main`, `go test ./cmd/... ./internal/...` spawns `claude` **exactly once**, and it is this test. So #2169 closes the only occurrence in the unit suite.

### CONFIRMED: the test on `main` asserts nothing

Mutation — replace the fallback in `summarize.go:60-62` with `return nil, nil`:

- `main`: `ok github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/summarize` — **passes with the feature deleted**
- PR: fails, with both messages the author quoted:
  ```
  summarize_test.go:753: default text generator factory called 0 times, want 1 …
  summarize_test.go:756: summary is nil; a nil generator must still produce the default generator's summary
  ```

### Is it the right fix?

Yes — it injects, it does not skip. `GenerateFromTranscript(..., nil, nil)` is still called, so the nil-generator branch at `cmd/entire/cli/summarize/summarize.go:60-62` is still executed; only `defaultTextGeneratorFactory` (`claude.go:53`) is stubbed. The test went from 0 assertions to 3 (factory consulted exactly once, summary non-nil, summary is the default generator's output).

### Blast radius on `main` (all CONFIRMED)

- **Developer machine, on every `mise run check`** — `mise.toml:22-27` puts `go test ./...` in `test:ci`, and `check` depends on it. So the pre-commit gate fires a live Sonnet call.
- **Tokens/network**: yes. Real model call with `--model sonnet` and the `summarizationPromptTemplate` prompt on stdin.
- **Real user config, twice**: the log shows `HOME=/Users/soph` — not a temp HOME. `generate_streaming.go:45` calls `readUserAPIKeyHelper()`, which `os.ReadFile`s the developer's real `~/.claude/settings.json` (`claudecode/generate.go:121-136`), and the spawned `claude` rewrites `~/.claude.json` and writes a session transcript under `~/.claude/projects`. This is a direct violation of CLAUDE.md's "Config/Cache/Keyring Isolation in Tests".
- **Hang risk**: bounded in practice — `exec.CommandContext` with the test's context — but an unauthenticated/interactive `claude` could stall until the package timeout.
- **CI**: unaffected, as the author says — `claude` is not on the runners, so `LookPath` fails and the test's `err != nil` branch is taken. That is exactly why this never surfaced.

### Conventions

- `t.Parallel()` correctly **omitted**, and the reason is documented in the comment (`defaultTextGeneratorFactory` is package-global). Correct call.
- `t.Cleanup` restores the factory. No `t.TempDir()` needed. No `execx` needed (nothing is spawned any more).

### Findings

1. **SUSPECTED (pre-existing, not this PR's bug, worth a follow-up):** `cmd/entire/cli/summarize/claude_test.go:79-90` — `TestClaudeGenerator_UsesDefaultTextGenerator`, the pattern this PR cites, calls `t.Parallel()` *and* mutates the same package global. It is safe today only because Go defers parallel tests until after all sequential top-level tests, and no other parallel test touches the factory. The new test picked the safer shape; the older one should lose its `t.Parallel()`. Not a merge blocker.
2. **SUSPECTED (minor fidelity):** `stubTextGenerator` (`claude_test.go:13-54`) does not implement `GenerateTextStreaming`, so the test exercises the non-streaming branch (`claude.go:110`) while production takes the streaming branch (`claude.go:102`). The test's stated contract — factory consulted, output returned — is still pinned correctly. Note only.

### Genuinely good

- The mutation-testing evidence in the PR body is real and I reproduced it exactly.
- The comment above the test explains *why* it must not be parallel and *what* happens without the stub — future readers won't re-break it.
- Reuses the existing in-package stub rather than inventing a new one.

---

## PR #2172 — `test(e2e): fail the canary when no agent registered instead of skipping everything`

**Verdict: MERGE.** No legitimate configuration reaches the skip; it fires only when something is genuinely wrong, and it cannot turn any currently-green CI leg red.

Base branch: `main` · Branch: `audit/e2e-canary-requires-agent` · +28, 2 files

### Actuals (worktree = PR merged with `origin/main`)

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | clean |
| `go vet -tags=e2e ./e2e/...` | clean |
| `gofmt -l e2e/` | no output |
| `shellcheck mise-tasks/test/e2e/canary` | clean |
| `mise run lint` | **0 issues** |
| **`mise run test:e2e:canary`** | **vogon: ALL 56 TESTS PASSED; roger-roger: ALL 4 TESTS PASSED; exit 0** |

### CONFIRMED: the silent-skip reproduces, and the PR converts it

`E2E_AGENT=vogon`, `vogon` off `PATH`, `git`+`tmux` present, same `E2E_ENTIRE_BIN`:

```
MAIN:  EXIT=0   top-level PASS 2  SKIP 54  FAIL 0    ok ... 1.442s
       skip reason: "no agents registered (check E2E_AGENT filter)"
PR:    EXIT=1
       preflight: no agents registered (E2E_AGENT="vogon"); every test would skip and the run would report success
```

Matches the PR body's numbers exactly.

### The enumeration — every configuration where the skip fires today

The skip is reachable iff `agents.All()` is empty. Registration is `E2E_AGENT` name-match **AND**, for four agents only, `exec.LookPath` at process start. Empirically probed on the PR branch (which error wins tells you whether registration happened):

| `E2E_AGENT` | Gated on `LookPath`? | Behaviour when binary absent | Legitimate to skip? |
|---|---|---|---|
| *unset / `""`* | — (6 agents register unconditionally) | **unreachable** — pre-existing `missing required binaries: [claude, codex, copilot, agent, gemini, pi]` | n/a |
| `claude-code` | no (`claude.go:31`) | pre-existing `missing required binaries: [claude (claude-code)]` | n/a |
| `codex` | no (`codex.go:16`) | pre-existing missing-binaries error | n/a |
| `copilot-cli` | no | pre-existing missing-binaries error | n/a |
| `cursor-cli` | no | pre-existing missing-binaries error | n/a |
| `gemini-cli` | no | pre-existing missing-binaries error | n/a |
| `pi` | no | pre-existing missing-binaries error | n/a |
| **`vogon`** | yes (`vogon.go:20`) | **silent skip → now fails** | **No** — canary must run vogon |
| **`opencode`** | yes (`opencode.go:23`) | **silent skip → now fails** | **No** — the leg exists to run opencode |
| **`factoryai-droid`** | yes (`droid.go:19`) | **silent skip → now fails** | **No** — same |
| **`roger-roger`** | yes (`roger_roger.go:17,20`) | **silent skip → now fails** | **No** — same |
| any unrecognized value (typo, new agent) | — | **silent skip → now fails** | **No** — a typo must not read as success |

**No legitimate case exists.** The six unconditional agents already hard-fail via the pre-existing preflight at `e2e/tests/main_test.go:82-90`; the four `LookPath`-gated ones were the asymmetry, and the PR closes it with the same failure mode. That asymmetry is the whole bug: register-then-check-binary = loud, don't-register = silent.

### Will this break CI? No — CONFIRMED

Every configuration that invokes the e2e suite:

1. **`ci.yml:47-68` `test-canary`** — `ubuntu-latest` only, matrix `checkpoint_store: [git-branch, git-refs]`, both running `mise run test:e2e:canary`. That task **builds** vogon (`mise-tasks/test/e2e/canary:12`) with `set -eu` and puts `$PWD` first on `E2E_PATH`, and preflights both roger-roger binaries (`:42-50`). Neither leg can reach the new check without a real breakage. Not run on Windows, so no `vogon.exe` concern.
2. **`e2e.yml`** matrix `[claude-code, opencode, gemini-cli, factoryai-droid, cursor-cli, copilot-cli, roger-roger, codex]`. Three legs are `LookPath`-gated. I checked the latest `main` run (33428984614): **factoryai-droid 1273s, opencode 424s, roger-roger 85s, all `success`** — they are running real work, not skipping. No currently-green leg turns red.
3. **`e2e-checkpoint-store.yml`, `e2e-checkpoints-v2.yml`, `e2e-isolated.yml`, `e2e-windows.yml`** — all `workflow_dispatch`, all pin an agent, all install/build it (vogon → `/usr/local/bin/vogon`, on default `PATH`).
4. **Developer**: `mise run test:e2e --agent opencode` without opencode installed goes from "everything skipped, exit 0" to a clear exit 1. Improvement.

### Shell half

`PATH="$E2E_PATH" command -v vogon` — verified the idiom in `sh`, `dash`, and `bash`: the temporary `PATH` **is** honoured for the `command` builtin and does **not** leak afterwards. Logic verified in isolation both ways (absent → exit 1 with the message; present → proceeds).

### Findings

1. **CONFIRMED (minor, not blocking): the shell preflight is fully subsumed by the Go one.** `mise-tasks/test/e2e/canary:29-35` fires iff `vogon` is off `E2E_PATH`; `main_test.go`'s new check fires under exactly the same condition for that leg (registration happens in `init()`, before `main_test.go:68` mutates `PATH`, so both see the same process-start `PATH`). And `canary:12` builds vogon under `set -eu`, so it is near-unreachable. It buys an earlier, cheaper message — fine to keep, but it is belt-and-braces, and the Go check is the actual fix.
2. **CONFIRMED (message quality): the failure diagnoses the *reason* but not the *remedy*.** `main_test.go` prints `no agents registered (E2E_AGENT=%q); every test would skip and the run would report success`. Compare the roger-roger message it mirrors: `…install/configure it before running roger-roger canary tests`. For the four gated agents the cause is always "binary not on PATH at process start", which the message never says. Appending something like `— the agent's binary must be on PATH when the test binary starts` would make it self-service. Worth asking for; not a blocker.
3. **SUSPECTED (adjacent gap the PR leaves open):** `e2e/bootstrap/main.go:19-30` ranges over `agents.All()` and exits 0 on an empty registry. In `e2e.yml`, the `Bootstrap agent` step therefore also passes silently when a gated agent's binary is missing — the same masking one step earlier. The PR's `TestMain` check catches it downstream, so nothing escapes, but a `len(agents.All()) == 0` guard there would fail at the more diagnostic step. Follow-up, not this PR.
4. **Nit:** `e2e/README.md:53` still documents `E2E_AGENT` default as "all registered", which is now slightly misleading for the gated agents. Pre-existing.

### Conventions

The change is in `TestMain` (before any test runs), so `t.Parallel()` / `t.TempDir()` don't apply. It reads `os.Getenv("E2E_AGENT")` only, adds no config/cache/keyring access, and sits **after** the existing isolation setup at `main_test.go:33-42` and after the missing-binaries loop — correct ordering, so the more specific "missing required binaries" error still wins for the six unconditional agents (verified in the probe table above).

### Genuinely good

- The author found the precedent in the codebase (`mise-tasks/test/e2e/roger-roger:13-16`, "so the suite cannot silently 'succeed'") and generalised it rather than inventing a new mechanism.
- The code comment correctly identifies the load-bearing subtlety — registration is in `init()`, so it's the **process-start** `PATH` that matters, not the one `TestMain` builds.
- Measurement in the PR body (54/56 skipped, exit 0, 1.5s) reproduced exactly.
- Chose the Go-side floor *and* the shell check, so the floor covers every entry point into `./e2e/tests`, not just the canary task.

---

Both PRs are the same class of fix and both are correct: a test-harness path that treats *absence* as *success*. #2169's `if err == nil { t.Log(...) }` and #2172's `t.Skip` are the same anti-pattern at different scales, and in both cases the author replaced it with an assertion rather than deleting the coverage. Neither has a test covering the new behaviour itself (hard for a `TestMain` preflight; unnecessary for #2169, where the mutation test is the proof) — acceptable, and I independently reproduced both claims rather than taking the PR bodies on trust.

# Review: entireio/cli #2171 and #2173

Both by suhaanthayyil, both `baseRefName: **main**`, both merge-base `60773bd4b`, both `MERGEABLE`. No comments posted, nothing pushed, PRs untouched.

## #2171 — `test(strategy)`: isolate hooks_test git commands

**Verdict: MERGE.** The leak is real, the fix closes it exactly, zero production surface.

### Actuals
| | |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | ok |
| `gofmt -l` | clean |
| `mise run lint` | `[lint:go] 0 issues` |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/strategy` | ok 85.0s |
| `go test -race ./cmd/entire/cli/strategy` | ok 73.5s |
| GitHub CI | all green incl. `test-windows`, `test-integration a/b/c`; only `Entire Gates` fails — and it fails identically on #2173, so not PR-specific |

### Demonstrated proof

Hostile global config (`…/scratchpad/hostile/gitconfig`):
```
[init]
	defaultBranch = feature
[commit]
	gpgsign = true
[core]
	hooksPath = /nonexistent/hostile-hooks
```

**On `origin/main`, clean env** → `ok  github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy  2.053s`

**On `origin/main`, hostile env:**
```
$ GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=$SP/hostile/gitconfig go test ./cmd/entire/cli/strategy -count=1
--- FAIL: TestInstallGitHook_WorktreeInstallsInCommonHooks (0.11s)
--- FAIL: TestIsGitSequenceOperation_Worktree (0.09s)
--- FAIL: TestGetHooksDirInPath_Worktree (0.62s)
--- FAIL: TestGetGitDirInPath_Worktree (0.81s)
FAIL	github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy	81.574s
```
Exactly the four tests the PR names, with the same opaque `failed to create worktree: exit status 255`. The full-package hostile run failing *only* in `hooks_test.go` also confirms the one-file scope is correct — no other file in the package leaks these settings.

**On the PR branch, same hostile env:** `ok … 65.357s` (full package).

Root-cause chain confirmed: the 38 `exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", …)` in `hooks_test.go` leave `cmd.Env` nil → child git inherits `os.Environ()` → reads `~/.gitconfig`. With `init.defaultBranch=feature`, `git init` pre-creates `feature`, so `git worktree add … -b feature` aborts. `cmd.Env = testutil.GitIsolatedEnv()` strips inherited `GIT_CONFIG_*` and repoints global+system at an empty file (`cmd/entire/cli/testutil/testutil.go:308-331`).

Coverage is complete: 42 `exec.Command` sites in the file = 38 git + 4 `sh` (hook-script execs, which already use `envWithPath`). All 38 git sites got the assignment; scripted check found **0** misses.

### Findings

- **F1 — SUSPECTED (durability, non-blocking).** 38 identical `cmd.Env = testutil.GitIsolatedEnv()` lines with nothing structural preventing the 39th call site from forgetting it — the exact failure this PR is repairing. A file-local `runGit(t, dir, args...)` would collapse both the Env line and the repeated 4-line error check and make the invariant unforgettable. `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/hooks_test.go:25` through `:695`, plus `:1139`, `:1184`.
- **F2 — CONFIRMED (scope note, not a defect).** The PR isolates the *test's own* git calls, not the git calls the **code under test** makes. `getGitDirInPath` (`cmd/entire/cli/strategy/hooks.go:98`) and `getHooksDirInPath` (`hooks.go:121`) shell out with `cmd.Env` nil, so `git rev-parse --git-path hooks` still honours the developer's global `core.hooksPath`. That is precisely why `clearGlobalHooksPath` must stay — the PR keeps it and says so, which is the right call. Verified: hostile `core.hooksPath` breaks neither branch today, so coverage is complete; the defence is just per-setting rather than structural.
- **F3 — CONFIRMED (informational).** 16 sibling unisolated git-exec sites remain repo-wide: `cmd/entire/cli/review/scope_test.go` (6), `cmd/entire/cli/gitrepo/gitstatus_guard_test.go` (3), `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/commit_hook_perf_test.go` (3), plus singles in `review_context_test.go`, `integration_test/resume_test.go`, `checkpoint/redact_cache_scope_test.go`, `checkpoint/remote/git_test.go`. The 3 in the same package are behind `//go:build hookperf`, so the default build is clean. Follow-up, not this PR.

### Conventions
`t.Parallel()` legality is the reason this PR uses per-command `cmd.Env` instead of `testutil.IsolateGitConfigEnv(t)`: 33 of 53 tests in the file are parallel, and `IsolateGitConfigEnv` is `t.Setenv`-based. Correct choice, correctly justified in the description. No spawned-`entire` calls in this file, so `execx.NonInteractive` is not in play. No `fmt.Print*` added.

## #2173 — `fix(auth)`: keep auth-go's process lock out of the real user cache

**Verdict: MERGE** (ideally retitled `test(auth)`). Leak proven, fix proven, correct layer; one residual gap worth a follow-up.

### Actuals
| | |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | ok |
| `gofmt -l` | clean |
| `mise run lint` | `[lint:go] 0 issues` |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/auth` | ok; both new tests PASS |
| `go test -race ./cmd/entire/cli/auth` | ok 2.9s |
| GitHub CI | all green except the same `Entire Gates` |

### Demonstrated proof

**Mechanism, from the dependency source** (`auth-go@v0.5.3-0.20260814125012-86c083be00da`):
```go
// tokenmanager/tokenmanager.go:124-128
// LockDir is the directory holding the cross-process advisory lock
// file. Empty → os.UserCacheDir()/auth-go …

// tokenmanager/tokenmanager.go:755-765  (processLock)
dir := m.cfg.LockDir
if dir == "" {
    if cache, err := os.UserCacheDir(); err == nil {
        dir = filepath.Join(cache, "auth-go")
```
`LockDir` appears **only** in those three places in the whole module — `Config.validate()` never inspects it, so `LockDir: ""` is byte-identical to omitting the field.

**Leak, controlled:** compiled the auth test binary from `origin/main` and from the PR, ran each with `HOME` and `XDG_CACHE_HOME` pointed at a throwaway tree:
```
### main rc=0 (PASS)
  &lt;fakeHOME&gt;/Library/Caches/auth-go/c907b2a07e9c….lock
  &lt;fakeHOME&gt;/Library/Caches/auth-go/0595ddec6cc2….lock
  &lt;fakeHOME&gt;/Library/Caches/auth-go/52bec8ea3510….lock
  &lt;fakeHOME&gt;/Library/Caches/auth-go/9b38e80efe43….lock
  count: 4

### pr rc=0 (PASS)
  count: 0
```
Note `rc=0` on main — it leaks and still reports `ok`, exactly as the PR says.

**Leak, in the wild:** the developer's real `~/Library/Caches/auth-go` holds **604** lock files, **all 604 hashes distinct**, spanning Jun 4 → today. Production derives the name from `sha256(ClientID \x00 Issuer)`, so a real user accumulates ~1 per core; 604 unique names is the signature of `httptest` servers on random ports, i.e. the test suite.

**Real-HOME regression check** (no fake env): 604 lock files before running the PR's auth package, **604 after — delta 0**.

**Mutation** (deleted the `LockDir:` line, then restored):
```
--- FAIL: TestNewContextTokenManager_LockNeverInRealUserCache (0.00s)
    refresh_test.go:470: auth-go lock directory ".../Library/Caches/auth-go" was created;
    the token manager must not write into the user cache dir under test
--- PASS: TestTokenManagerLockDir_NeverRealUserCache
```
The e2e guard is a genuine guard. The second test is not (see F6).

Single call site: `tokenmanager.New` appears exactly once in the repo (`cmd/entire/cli/auth/refresh.go:125`), reached from `refresh.go:255` and `refresh.go:352`. Fix is complete for in-process code.

### Production vs test-only

**Test-only in effect, production file in form.** `tokenManagerLockDir()` (`cmd/entire/cli/auth/refresh.go:156`) returns `testdirs.Dir("authlock")` under `go test` and `""` otherwise; `testdirs.Dir` gates on `testing.Testing()`. In a production binary `LockDir` is `""`, so auth-go takes the identical `os.UserCacheDir()/auth-go` branch it takes today — same path derivation, same `proclock`, same cross-process semantics. **The lock still works correctly for real users; nothing about production changed.** This is why the `fix(auth)` type is a mislabel (F5) — cosmetic, but it makes the PR read bigger than it is.

### Second path resolver?

**No — and this is the part the PR got right.** `tokenManagerLockDir` does not compute a home-relative path of its own; it delegates to `testdirs.Dir`, the same call shape as `internal/entireclient/userdirs/userdirs.go:30` (`"config"`), `:45` (`"cache"`), and `internal/entireclient/tokenstore/tokenstore.go:147` (`"tokenstore"`), and otherwise hands resolution back to auth-go. `userdirs` remains the sole resolver of Entire's own config/cache dirs. The auth-go lock dir is a third-party surface that neither `userdirs` nor `XDG_CACHE_HOME` covers on macOS, so this genuinely was the one uncovered surface.

### Findings

- **F4 — CONFIRMED (real residual gap; the one worth acting on).** The fix is **in-process only**. `testdirs.Dir` returns `ok=false` in a subprocess (documented at `internal/testdirs/testdirs.go:26-29`), no harness sets `HOME` — `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/setup_test.go:47-48` and `e2e/tests/main_test.go:41-42` set only `ENTIRE_CONFIG_DIR` + `XDG_CACHE_HOME`, and on macOS `os.UserCacheDir()` ignores `XDG_CACHE_HOME`. So a spawned `entire` that reaches a token refresh still writes into the developer's real `~/Library/Caches/auth-go`. I could not get a spawned binary to actually reach the lock (`api /me`, `org list`, `auth status` all failed at the network before refreshing), so **practical exposure is unproven** — but the mechanism is confirmed. The structural point: `userdirs` and `tokenstore` each pair `testdirs` with an env override (`ENTIRE_CONFIG_DIR`, `ENTIRE_TOKEN_STORE_PATH`); this fix implements only half that pattern. An `ENTIRE_AUTH_LOCK_DIR` check ahead of `testdirs.Dir` would let the integration/e2e TestMains close it in one line.
- **F5 — CONFIRMED (minor).** Commit type `fix(auth)` implies a user-visible auth fix; there is none. `test(auth)` is accurate.
- **F6 — CONFIRMED (minor).** `TestTokenManagerLockDir_NeverRealUserCache` (`cmd/entire/cli/auth/refresh_test.go:481`) does **not** guard the wiring — mutation-proven above, it passes against the mutant. It only pins `tokenManagerLockDir` itself. Additionally its checks, and the e2e test's dir list at `refresh_test.go:462-464`, have no Windows arm (`os.UserCacheDir()` there is `%LocalAppData%`, not HOME-derived), so on `windows-latest` both assert nothing. Not a failure; just no coverage.
- **F7 — informational.** Neither test can assert the production branch (`tokenManagerLockDir() == ""` outside `go test`), since `testing.Testing()` is always true in-process. Acceptable — the auth-go contract is documented and I verified it against the module source.

### Conventions
`TestNewContextTokenManager_LockNeverInRealUserCache` correctly omits `t.Parallel()` (it uses `t.Setenv`) and says why in a comment; `TestTokenManagerLockDir_NeverRealUserCache` is parallel and mutates nothing. Go's sequential-then-parallel scheduling means the `t.Setenv("HOME", …)` cannot be observed by the parallel test. `tokenstore.UseFileBackendForTesting` is used with `t.Cleanup`, per CLAUDE.md. Hermetic (`httptest`, `allowInsecureHTTP=true`); no network.

---

## What's genuinely good

- Both descriptions state a falsifiable claim with the commands to check it, and **both claims reproduce exactly** — the same 4 named tests, the same 4 lock files. That is rare and it made this review fast.
- #2171 picks `cmd.Env` over `t.Setenv`-based `IsolateGitConfigEnv` *because* 33 of 53 tests in the file are parallel, and says so. Right call, right reason.
- #2171 keeps `clearGlobalHooksPath` instead of assuming the new isolation subsumes it. It doesn't (F2) — the production code under test shells out to git with inherited env. Good instinct.
- #2173 routes through `testdirs` rather than inventing a path resolver — the CLAUDE.md-correct layer, and the tempting wrong fix (hardcode a second resolver, or `t.Setenv("HOME")` in a helper) was avoided.
- #2173's e2e guard is mutation-verified real, not a tautology, and the comments explain the non-obvious macOS `os.UserCacheDir`-vs-XDG divergence that makes the bug invisible on Linux.

1. `ENTIRE_AUTH_LOCK_DIR` override in `tokenManagerLockDir` + wire it into the integration/e2e TestMains (F4).
2. Retitle #2173 to `test(auth)` (F5).
3. A `runGit` helper in `hooks_test.go`, and eventually a `forbidigo`-style rule for bare `exec.Command(…, "git", …)` in `_test.go`, in the spirit of the existing `gitrepo.Status` rule (F1/F3).</result>

Had it not been honored, the pattern would have matched *nothing* and the task would still have exited 0 — worth knowing that trap was live and was avoided.

No `sed -i`, `readlink -f`, `grep -P`, or `mapfile` anywhere in the diff.

(The body says "seven"; it's eight instances across six table rows. Irrelevant.)

## Findings

### 1. CONFIRMED — the SC2044 justification is factually false. `scripts/migrate-sessions.sh:426-428`

&gt; `-name '[0-9]*' restricts matches to numeric session directories, which cannot contain whitespace or globbing characters, so word splitting is safe here.`

`[0-9]*` constrains only the **first** character. Fixture with dirs `0`, `1`, `12 34`, `9*x`, `2[a]`: find matches all five, and the unquoted `$(...)` loop iterates `9*x`, `0`, `1`, `2[a]`, `12`, `34` — `12 34` split into two bogus iterations.

Not a live bug: these are tool-generated integer session indices under an `mktemp -d`-rooted `SOURCE_DIR` (migrate-sessions.sh:243, 467/544/619), so whitespace can't occur in practice. But the stated reason will mislead the next reader into trusting `-name` for something it doesn't do. A glob avoids the disable entirely and is unconditionally safe:

```sh
for SUBDIR in "$CHECKPOINT_PATH"/[0-9]*/; do
```

### 2. CONFIRMED — the SC2294 disable is avoidable; the comment argues against a fix shellcheck didn't ask for. `scripts/test-codex-agent-integration.sh:161-163`

The comment says executing `"$@"` directly would break re-parsing. True, but SC2294's own remedy is "*or eval as string*" — `eval "$*"`. Verified: `eval "$*"` produces **zero** shellcheck findings, `eval "$@"` produces SC2294. Behavior is identical here (eval concatenates its arguments with a space either way, and IFS is default). A one-character change removes the suppression.

### 3. CONFIRMED — SC2034: the "parity" justification doesn't survive inspection. `scripts/test-codex-agent-integration.sh:4-6`

`grep -n AGENT_NAME` over both scripts: codex has exactly one occurrence (its own assignment); copilot has two (assignment + `echo "  ${AGENT_NAME} Integration Probe"` at line 75). The codex script prints **no banner at all** — so nothing is being kept parallel, and a reader diffing the two won't find the symmetry the comment promises. Two honest options, both better than a dead variable plus a suppression:

- delete it, or
- add the banner to the codex script, which actually delivers the claimed parity *and* deletes the disable.

I'd take the second.

### 4. CONFIRMED — "output-identical" is over-stated for the `ls -d */` → `find` rewrite. Cosmetic in practice.

Ran 18 adversarial fixtures. Five divergences:

| case | old | new | assessment |
|---|---|---|---|
| dir named `-foo` / `-n` | `` (ls ate it as an option) | `-foo,bar` | **new is a fix** — this is the SC2035 bug |
| hidden dir `.hidden` | excluded | **included** | behavior change |
| symlink→dir | included | **excluded** (`-type d` ≠ `-type l`) | behavior change |
| `foo` + `foo-bar` | `foo-bar,foo` | `foo,foo-bar` | order flip — inherent to sorting with vs without the trailing `/`, locale-independent (checked C and en_US.UTF-8) |

Unreachable with the real data: the e2e agent slugs (`claude-code`, `opencode`, `gemini-cli`, `factoryai-droid`, `cursor-cli`, `copilot-cli`, `roger-roger`) contain no prefix pair, `gh run download` produces no dot-dirs or symlinks, and `agents_found` feeds only a log line and a `.run-info.json` field that nothing in the repo reads (`grep -rn run-info` → only its own writer). Space/unicode/glob-metachar/quote/newline/empty/`a"b,c"d` cases are all byte-identical.

The SC2001 rewrite is likewise identical except where the old `echo "$v"` swallowed `-n`/`-e` as flags — again the new code is strictly better. The SC2231 quoting fix preserves glob expansion in both the match and no-match cases.

### 5. CONFIRMED, pre-existing — the gate still fails silent on zero matches.

`find ... -print0 | xargs -0 shellcheck` exits **0** when find matches nothing (verified on macOS BSD xargs). That is precisely the mechanism that let four env-bash tasks go unlinted with nothing reporting it. The PR fixes the instance but not the class. A `[ "$(printf %s "$files" | tr -cd '\0' | wc -c)" -gt 0 ]` style assertion — or just piping through a count — would make the next silent-skip regression loud. Reasonable to leave for a follow-up.

### 6. Informational — 7 shell scripts still outside the gate, including a second installer.

Repo-wide there are 39 shebang'd shell scripts; 32 are covered. Uncovered: `.cursor/install.sh` (88 lines, `set -euo pipefail`, apt/PPA operations), `.devcontainer/post-create.sh`, `.devcontainer/run-with-keyring.sh`, `.claude/scripts/remote-setup.sh`, `.claude/skills/test-repo/test-harness.sh`, `.gemini/test-hooks.sh`, `.ferrata/setup`. I ran shellcheck on all seven: clean apart from 3 intentional SC2016 infos, so this is a dormant gap, not a live one.

Also still skipped by the matcher (verified): `#!/usr/bin/bash`, `#!/usr/local/bin/bash`, `#!/usr/bin/env -S bash`, `#!/bin/dash`, `#!/bin/ksh`, and any file relying on `# shellcheck shell=bash` with no shebang. And `run:` blocks in `.github/workflows/*.yml` are not shell-linted at all. None of that is in scope for this PR.</result>

## Verdict

The fix is correct and the perf win is real and larger than the PR claims. I found **no correctness defect**. The one thing I'd ask for before merge is a test, not a code change.

## What's good

- The approach is exactly what #2196 asked for, and it restores the pre-#1926 structure verbatim rather than inventing something new.
- Hoisting `refBase` above the `if dryRun` block (`migrate.go:84-93`) also de-duplicates the dry-run and real paths onto one `alreadyImported` computation — a genuine simplification, not just a hoist.
- The new test is well designed as a *negative* assertion: instead of counting subprocesses, it makes both the flock and `git update-ref` **fail if attempted**. That's stronger than a counter and can't rot into a tautology.
- The concurrency argument in the PR body holds (see below). `mise run fmt` produces no changes; `mise run lint` → `0 issues`.

## Findings

### 1. CONFIRMED — the inner guard the PR's safety argument depends on is now completely untested

`cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/migrate.go:137-140` (the `treeInRefHistory` re-check inside `updatePersistentRef`'s builder) is the sole thing standing between two racing migrations and a duplicate import. The PR body rests its entire correctness case on it. After this change, no test reaches it — the outer check at `:106` short-circuits every existing test before the lock is ever taken.

Mutation test, both directions:

| tree | inner guard deleted → |
|---|---|
| `main` | **3 tests fail**: `TestMigrateBranchToRefs`, `..._ReenqueuesAlreadyImportedRef`, `..._SkipsRefAdvancedPastBranchSnapshot` |
| PR #2200 | `ok github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint 11.932s` — **entire package green** |

This matters more than usual here: #1926 already deleted the outer check once by accident while refactoring. The symmetric accident — a future `/simplify` pass deleting the *inner* one as "dead, the caller already checked" — now has zero regression protection. `MigrateBranchToRefs` is only reachable from `doctor_migrate.go`, so no integration or e2e test covers it either (grep for `MigrateBranchToRefs` hits exactly 3 files, two of them the file and its own test).

**Ask:** one test that pre-empts the outer check and forces the inner one — e.g. seed, migrate, then have the *builder* observe a ref that was advanced between the outer read and the lock. A seam or a second goroutine racing on the flock both work. Alternatively a comment at `:137` explaining it is load-bearing under concurrency, but a test is what actually holds.

### 2. CONFIRMED — perf claim reproduced, and it is understated

Reproduced independently with `GIT_TRACE2_EVENT` on a 50-checkpoint repo, tracing only the second (idempotent) pass:

```
main:      SECOND pass 611ms | git processes=50, of which update-ref=50 | flock files created=50
PR #2200:  SECOND pass  48ms | trace2 file never created -&gt; 0 git subprocesses | flock files created=0
```

(flock count measured by deleting `.git/entire-persistent-ref-locks` after the first pass and re-reading it after the second.) Exactly linear in checkpoint count, 12.7x wall-clock. The PR body's "two `update-ref H H` calls before, zero after" was measured on a 2-checkpoint repo; the real shape is one subprocess + one flock per already-imported checkpoint, which is what #2196 described.

### 3. CONFIRMED — the new tests are RED on old code, deterministically, and are not a hang risk

Reverting only `migrate.go` to `main` and keeping the new tests:

```
--- FAIL: .../native_git_ref_lock_held (0.77s)
    walk v1 checkpoints: set ref for checkpoint a1b2c3d4e5f6: update persistent ref
    refs/entire/checkpoints/f6/a1b2c3d4e5f6: git update-ref ...: : signal: killed
--- FAIL: .../persistent_ref_flock_held (0.78s)
    walk v1 checkpoints: set ref for checkpoint a1b2c3d4e5f6: acquire persistent ref flock
    refs/entire/checkpoints/f6/a1b2c3d4e5f6: flock: context deadline exceeded
```

On the timeout concern: the 500ms deadline at `migrate_test.go:252` bounds the whole second `MigrateBranchToRefs`, so failure is a bounded ~0.78s, never a hang. I measured the fixed second pass at **2.3–3.9ms** — ~200x headroom. Not a CI flake risk. The `flock.Acquire` in the same process does block (locks are per open-file-description), confirmed by the failure above.

### 4. Race analysis — the argument holds, no reintroduced race

Traced both directions of staleness in the unlocked outer read:

- **Outer says "not imported", another writer imports first.** Falls through to `updatePersistentRef`, flock, inner re-read, inner `treeInRefHistory` → no-op CAS `H H`. Caught. This is precisely the interleaving two concurrent migrations produce, and it still resolves correctly.
- **Outer says "imported".** `treeInRefHistory` returns true only on positive evidence: a readable commit chain from a non-zero tip carrying that exact tree. Any read failure (`CommitObject` error, `ErrObjectNotFound`, missing ref) returns `false` and falls through to the locked path — the safe direction (redo, not skip). There is no input for which a stale read produces a skip that a locked read would not have produced at that same instant.

One asymmetry worth naming, **PLAUSIBLE but negligible, not a blocker**: pre-PR, the skip path still executed `git update-ref &lt;ref&gt; H H`, which *asserts* the ref is still `H`; an external (non-flock) writer moving it in the read→CAS window produced `ErrShadowRefBusy` → retry → re-evaluate. Post-PR the skip decision is never revalidated. Such a writer does exist — `remote/checkpoint_ref.go:319` fetches with a **force** refspec `"+" + ref + ":" + ref`, so a concurrent read-path fetch can non-fast-forward a local checkpoint ref. But the window this closes is only read→CAS (sub-millisecond); once the walk has passed a checkpoint, both designs are equally exposed, and migration is re-runnable. I would not hold the PR for it.

### 5. First-parent predicate — sound for this change; one pre-existing gap (PLAUSIBLE, not reproduced, out of scope)

The predicate is byte-identical before and after, so #2200 neither improves nor worsens it. Answering the question directly: OPF does **not** change the tree when it finds nothing to redact (trailer-only rebuild → same tree → correctly skipped). But `strategy/manual_commit_opf_refs.go` (`RewriteQueuedCheckpointRefsWithOPF`) *rebuilds* queued checkpoint-ref commits rather than appending, so when OPF actually redacts something the tree changes and the pre-OPF commit leaves first-parent history. A later `entire doctor migrate-checkpoints` would then see `alreadyImported == false` and re-import the un-OPF-redacted v1 tree on top of the OPF-redacted tip. Worth a separate issue; it is not this PR's to fix.

### 6. Minor — restored code, dropped comment

`migrate.go:106-112` restores the pre-#1926 skip block but not its 7-line comment explaining *why* an already-imported ref is enqueued (a prior run may have written the ref then crashed before enqueuing). That "why" now lives nowhere at the call site, even though `TestMigrateBranchToRefs_ReenqueuesAlreadyImportedRef` exists solely to pin it. Also no blank line between the `dryRun` block's closing brace at `:105` and `if alreadyImported {` at `:106` (gofmt-clean, just inconsistent with the surrounding file).

### 7. Minor perf follow-up left on the table

`migratedCheckpointTree(ctx, repo, cid, cpTreeHash, !dryRun)` at `migrate.go:74` still writes a normalized blob + tree for every already-migrated checkpoint on every re-run, because `changed` is always true for un-normalized v1 metadata. Measured on the same 50-checkpoint repo: second pass **52ms with `persist=true` vs 19ms with `persist=false`** — another ~2.7x is available by probing with `persist=false` and only re-computing with `persist=true` on the import path. Objects are content-addressed so there's no repo growth; purely wasted work. Optional, and arguably a separate change.

## Verdict

The PR does what it claims. Both sites named in #2204 are converted, the tests are genuinely red without the fix, and everything passes locally. The one real defect is an error-classification widening at the v1 call site that I reproduced. The bigger issue is not a bug at all — it's that **open PR #2131 (same author) already contains this exact fix**, and the two conflict hard.

---

## Findings

### 1. MEDIUM — CONFIRMED (reproduced). Transient ref-lock contention now aborts the user's `git push` with a self-contradictory message, and with zero retries

`cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_opf_rewrite.go:815` now keys on `checkpoint.ErrShadowRefBusy`. That sentinel is produced by `cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/persistent_ref_update.go:56`:

```go
if strings.Contains(out, "cannot lock ref") || strings.Contains(out, "but expected") {
    return ErrShadowRefBusy
}
```

`"cannot lock ref"` covers two different situations: a real CAS mismatch (`... is at X but expected Y`) **and** plain lock contention / a stale `.lock` (`Unable to create '.../v1.lock': File exists`). The old code used go-git's `storage.ErrReferenceHasChanged`, which fires **only** on a real mismatch — the file's own deleted doc comment said so explicitly ("Other errors (I/O, packed-ref locks, storage bugs) get wrapped as-is so they aren't misreported as concurrency failures"). That distinction is now gone at this call site.

**Scenario:** an external native git process (`gc`/`pack-refs`/`fetch`, another `entire` hook, or a leftover `.lock` from a SIGKILLed one) holds `refs/heads/entire/checkpoints/v1.lock` and then *aborts* — the ref never moves. OPF's CAS fails, is classified as "ref moved", and `hooks_git_cmd.go:337-341` propagates `V1RefMovedError` so `git push` is rejected. The whole OPF rewrite (up to ~30s/commit of model inference) is discarded.

**Reproduced** with a throwaway probe (native `git update-ref --stdin` → `start`/`update`/`prepare`, then `atomicSetV1Ref`, then `abort`):

```
ref BEFORE probe = 57b7f8ff5e79c561855f42995db13645227ba527
ref AFTER  probe = 57b7f8ff5e79c561855f42995db13645227ba527   (moved? false)
atomicSetV1Ref err = entire/checkpoints/v1 moved during OPF rewrite (expected 57b7f8f, found 57b7f8f);
  another local worktree advanced the ref mid-rewrite — re-run `git push` (no fetch needed; the move was local)
classified as V1RefMovedError: expected=... actual=... SAME=true
```

Note `expected == found` in a message whose whole point is that they differ.

Making it worse: the sibling helper `updatePersistentRef` (same file, line 127-136) retries `ErrShadowRefBusy` 16× with jitter precisely so transient contention isn't fatal. `CASPersistentRef` has **no** retry. So the new code is strictly less tolerant than the mechanism it was aligned with. Fix is small: at this call site, treat only `"but expected"` as a conflict, or give `CASPersistentRef` the same bounded retry.

(For the git-refs path this is fail-soft — `runOPFForCheckpointRefs` withholds the flush and the user's push proceeds — so it's v1-only in severity.)

### 2. MEDIUM — CONFIRMED. Direct collision with open PR #2131, which already contains this fix

`gh pr diff 2131` changes the **same two lines** to `checkpoint.CompareAndSwapRef(ctx, repo, pr.ref, rebuilt[i], pr.old)` and `checkpoint.CompareAndSwapRef(ctx, repo, refName, newHash, expectedOld)`, with `errors.Is(err, checkpoint.ErrRefConflict)`. Its body lists "OPF publication" under the CAS conversion and states "OPF uses one CAS attempt by design."

`git merge-tree pr2131 HEAD` (both PRs' heads):

```
CONFLICT (modify/delete): cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/persistent_ref_update.go deleted in pr2131 and modified in HEAD.
CONFLICT (modify/delete): cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/persistent_ref_update_test.go deleted in pr2131 and modified in HEAD.
CONFLICT (content): cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_opf_refs.go
CONFLICT (content): cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_opf_rewrite.go
CONFLICT (content): cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/ephemeral_test.go
```

This is a real sequencing problem, not a cosmetic rebase: #2131 **deletes** the file #2211 adds `CASPersistentRef` to, replacing it with `ref_transaction.go` (`CompareAndSwapRef` / `RunRefTransaction` / `ErrRefConflict`). Resolving the modify/delete by keeping HEAD's file would resurrect a deliberately-deleted file and leave two parallel CAS APIs with two conflict sentinels — and #2131 adds `persistent_ref_inventory_test.go`, a lexical inventory of persistent-ref writers, which a resurrected writer would likely trip.

Two clean options: land #2211 now and have #2131 rebase onto it (its OPF hunks become no-ops), or drop #2211 and let #2131 close #2204. Landing both as-is is the one thing that doesn't work. Worth noting #2131 also lists, as its own non-blocking hardening idea, "Narrow native Git's `cannot lock ref` error classification if callers need more precise operational errors" — i.e. finding 1 is already known to that author.

### 3. LOW — CONFIRMED by inspection. New unbounded blocking wait on the interactive push path, for little correctness gain

`CASPersistentRef` → `withPersistentRefFlock` → `flock.AcquireContext`. Per `cmd/entire/cli/internal/flock/flock_unix.go:50-56`, when the context carries **no deadline** it takes the blocking `syscall.Flock(LOCK_EX)` kernel path and ignores cancellation entirely. The pre-push hook context (`hooks_git_cmd.go:41-52`) sets no deadline. So `git push` can now block behind another Entire process's condensation for as long as that holds the ref lock, un-cancellable.

The correctness value of the flock here is thin: `pr.old` / `localTip` were read long before the lock (correctly — the scan is outside it, see below), so the lock does not make the read-modify-write atomic. It mostly converts "immediate CAS conflict" into "wait, then CAS conflict". The genuine benefit is that it stops OPF from burning a concurrent `updatePersistentRef`'s retry budget. Defensible, but #2131 deliberately chose the bare `CompareAndSwapRef` without the flock for this path — worth a conscious decision rather than divergence by accident.

### 4. LOW. Test gaps

- `TestOPFV1RewriteDoesNotLoseConcurrentCheckpointUpdate` calls `atomicSetV1Ref` directly, not `RewriteUnpushedV1WithOPF`. The git-refs sibling test does go through the public entry point. Asymmetric — the v1 test wouldn't catch a future change that stops routing through `atomicSetV1Ref`.
- No test covers "external process holds the lock but the ref never moves" — exactly the case that exposes finding 1. Adding it would have caught the misclassification.

### 5. INFORMATIONAL. Remaining unconditional go-git writers on the same v1 ref

`setRefHash` (`strategy/common.go:300`, plain `Storer.SetReference`, no CAS at all) writes `refs/heads/entire/checkpoints/v1` from `common.go:163,194,246,254,854`, `push_common.go:542`, `metadata_reconcile.go:198`, `cleanup.go:395` — the fetch/rebase-recovery, reconcile and cleanup paths, some of which run *during pre-push*. The PR body correctly scopes these out ("initialization, recovery, and migration are unchanged") and #2204 named only the two OPF sites, but after this PR they are the only remaining go-git writers of checkpoint refs, and they're the #1917 class (unconditional), not the #2204 class. #2131 converts them. Worth a follow-up issue if #2211 lands standalone.

---

## Verified good

- **Both #2204 sites converted.** `manual_commit_opf_rewrite.go:811` and `manual_commit_opf_refs.go:169`. A tree-wide grep for `CheckAndSetReference` / `SetReference(` finds no remaining go-git CAS on checkpoint refs: the only hits are `gitrepo/reftable.go` (the storer implementation itself), `benchutil`, `trail_cmd.go` (trail branches), `checkpointpolicy/store.go` (policy ref), `git_operations.go:351` (a user branch) — none of them checkpoint refs.
- **No deadlock, no lock nesting.** Every `flock.Acquire*` scope in the tree is self-contained (`grep flock.Acquire`): the push-queue lock is released inside `Peek()`/`Drain()` before the CAS loop starts; `updatePersistentRef`'s four call sites (`store.go:116`, `persistent.go:2123`, `refs_store.go:235`, `migrate.go:108`) are all inside checkpoint store writes, which OPF never invokes; the shadow-branch flock is a different lock directory and untouched by OPF. `RewriteQueuedCheckpointRefsWithOPF` takes and releases one ref lock at a time — no hold-and-wait.
- **The expensive scan really is outside the lock.** `manual_commit_opf_refs.go`: pass 1 (blob collect, lines 84-123), pass 2 (the single OPF shell-out, 130-143), pass 3 (rebuild, 149-164) all precede the CAS loop at 168-172, and the lock lives inside `CASPersistentRef`. Same shape for v1: `atomicSetV1Ref` is called at line 406, after the rebuild loop.
- **#1926's delete-before-confirming-conflict bug is NOT repeated.** `CASPersistentRef` (persistent_ref_update.go:101-110) performs no object cleanup at all, and `updatePersistentRef`'s `tryDeleteLooseObject` remains correctly gated behind `errors.Is(refErr, ErrShadowRefBusy)` (line 127-132). Orphaned rewrite commits are left for `git gc`, which `V1RefMovedError`'s doc already documents as intended.
- **Context cancellation is benign.** `exec.CommandContext` killing `git update-ref` yields empty output, so it does *not* match the busy substrings and is returned as a wrapped error rather than being misread as a conflict. If git had already renamed the lock into place, the ref lands on the OPF-rewritten tip — the safe direction, no privacy regression.
- **go-git sees the native CAS result.** Probed: after `atomicSetV1Ref` succeeds, `repo.Reference(refName, true)` returns the new hash (no stale-cache hazard on the subsequent push), and a genuinely stale expectation correctly returns `ErrShadowRefBusy`.
- **The tests are genuinely red without the fix.** I reverted only the two production call sites (keeping `CASPersistentRef` and all tests):
  ```
  --- FAIL: TestOPFCheckpointRefRewriteDoesNotLoseConcurrentCheckpointUpdate (0.08s)
      Error: An error is expected but got nil.
      Messages: the OPF rewrite must not report success while a native update owns the ref lock
  --- FAIL: TestOPFV1RewriteDoesNotLoseConcurrentCheckpointUpdate (0.08s)
      Error: An error is expected but got nil.
  ```
  Notably the `require.Equal(t, nativeTip, finalRef.Hash())` assertion *passed* in the red run — so the tests pin the actual lost update (go-git wrote the ref, native git's commit clobbered it, and OPF reported success), not just a missing error.

## Recommendation

Approvable on correctness after fixing finding 1 (a few lines: either restrict the conflict match to `"but expected"` at this call site, or reuse the 16× backoff loop). **But settle the #2131 question first** — the two PRs make the identical change through two different APIs and cannot both land. That's a maintainer sequencing call, not a code defect in #2211.</result>

# Review: PR #2199 — "Fix stale lock cleanup"

## Verdict

**Request changes.** The PR introduces a real, reproducible mutual-exclusion failure in the flock protocol that `withPersistentRefFlock` / `withShadowBranchFlock` depend on, plus a lint failure and a probable hard break on Windows. The diagnosis of the problem is right and the shape of the feature is right; the deletion protocol is not.

---

## CONFIRMED — Critical: acquire-then-unlink silently destroys flock mutual exclusion

**`cmd/entire/cli/strategy/cleanup.go:712-718`**

```go
release, err := tryAcquireCleanupLock(ctx, path)   // holds inode I1
...
removeErr := os.Remove(path)                       // path unlinked, I1 detached
release()                                          // only here
```

flock is bound to the inode, not the path, and `flock.AcquireContext` opens with `O_RDWR|O_CREATE` (`internal/flock/flock_unix.go:44`). Once cleanup unlinks the path, any process already holding the old inode open and any process that opens the path afresh end up locking **two different inodes** and both enter the critical section. Two distinct windows produce it: (a) between `os.Remove` and `release()`; (b) between `open()` and `flock()` inside `AcquireContext` for any concurrent acquirer.

### Reproductions (three, all failing)

1. **Deterministic** — cleaner acquires with the PR's exact `tryAcquireCleanupLock`, unlinks, and while it *still holds the lock* a second `flock.Acquire(path)` (the primitive `withShadowBranchFlock` uses) succeeds immediately. Fails in 0.2s. A pre-unlink control acquire in the same test correctly blocks, proving exclusion was intact until the unlink.

2. **Four real OS processes** + one goroutine looping `deleteCleanupLockFiles` on the same lock ID, overlap detected cross-process via an `O_EXCL` sentinel:

   | | entries | violations |
   |---|---|---|
   | with cleanup running | 19,161 | **4,334 (23%)** |
   | control, no cleanup | 18,429 | **0** |

3. **In-process, separate fds**, 4 workers, 5s: 627 violations / 38,870 entries. Control: 0 / 37,476.

**Blast radius:** the persistent-ref lock is what serializes Entire's writers around `casUpdateRef` for `entire/checkpoints/v1` (`checkpoint/persistent_ref_update.go:70-82`). Losing it drops the CAS to its retry budget against concurrent Entire writers it was explicitly designed not to race — and `updatePersistentRef` calls `tryDeleteLooseObject` on CAS conflict. This is strictly worse than the disk-usage problem it fixes.

**The fix belongs in `flock.AcquireContext`, not in the reaper.** I prototyped the standard post-acquire inode revalidation (`f.Stat()` vs `os.Stat(path)`, `os.SameFile`, reopen on mismatch) in `flock_unix.go` and reran both stress harnesses: **0 violations in both** (19,181 and 39,210 entries). With that in place, acquire-then-unlink becomes safe; without it, no amount of care in `cleanup.go` can make it safe. The `flock_windows.go` path needs the equivalent.

---

## CONFIRMED — High: no staleness or liveness check at all

**`cleanup.go:485-511`, `699-730`**

The PR treats "not held at this instant" as "stale". There is no age threshold, no PID/liveness probe, no correlation with active sessions. Verified with the real binary: a freshly created, perfectly live `entire-persistent-ref-locks/refs_heads_entire_checkpoints_v1.lock` — the single hottest lock in the system, taken by every condensation — was deleted because nobody happened to hold it during the scan.

Compounding this: **`entire clean --all` has no active-session guard.** `runCleanCurrentHead` has one (`clean.go:96-113`, "wait for sessions to finish"), `runCleanAll` (`clean.go:259`) has none — it goes straight to `ListAllItems`/`DeleteAllCleanupItems`. And `entire clean --all --force` is documented as routine troubleshooting in `README.md:590` and `docs/architecture/checkpoint-scenarios.md:657-661` with no quiescence caveat. So the answer to "does clean already assume quiescence?" is **no for `--all`** — the hazard is fully in play, in the exact way the docs tell users to invoke it.

---

## PLAUSIBLE (reasoned from Go source, not executed on Windows) — High: the feature cannot work on Windows and breaks `clean --all` there

`os.OpenFile` → `openFileNolog` (`os/file_windows.go:158`) → `syscall.Open`, whose share mode is `FILE_SHARE_READ|FILE_SHARE_WRITE` — **no `FILE_SHARE_DELETE`** (`syscall/syscall_windows.go:395`). `os.Remove` is `DeleteFile` (`os/file_windows.go:230`), which fails with `ERROR_SHARING_VIOLATION` while *any* handle lacking `FILE_SHARE_DELETE` is open — including the cleaner's own flock handle, still open at line 717.

This repo already documents exactly this: `agent/opencode/stage_export.go:38-49` ("Go opens files without `FILE_SHARE_DELETE`") with a retry helper at `stage_export_windows.go`. That retry works there because the blocking handle belongs to *another* process; here it is the cleaner's own, so no retry can ever succeed.

Predicted Windows behaviour: every lock file lands in `FailedLockFiles`, nothing is reclaimed, and `entire clean --all` exits 1 for anyone who has ever taken a checkpoint. `GOOS=windows go build ./...` passes, so this is a runtime break, not a compile break — and **CI would not catch it**: `ci.yml:86` runs `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/... -run '(Windows|MSYS)'`, which matches neither new test.

Reorder to `release(); os.Remove(path)` and this specific problem goes away on both platforms (it does not fix the hazard above, which needs the flock-side revalidation).

---

## CONFIRMED — Medium: lint fails; CI would be red

`mise run fmt` changed nothing. `mise run lint` on the formatted tree:

```
cmd/entire/cli/strategy/cleanup.go:748:9: error returned from external package is unwrapped:
  sig: func .../internal/flock.AcquireContext(...) (release func(), err error) (wrapcheck)
	return flock.AcquireContext(lockCtx, path)
1 issues: * wrapcheck: 1
```

---

## CONFIRMED — Medium: a healthy held lock is reported as a failure and turns `clean --all` into exit 1

Real binary, one lock held by another process:

```
Failed to delete 1 item:
Lock files (1):
  entire-shadow-locks/entire_abcdef1.lock
failed to delete 1 item
EXIT=1
```

A currently-held lock is the *correct, healthy* state during any active session. It should be skipped silently (or reported as "in use"), not counted into `totalFailed` (`clean.go:376`), which now makes the command exit non-zero.

---

## CONFIRMED — Medium: a lock-dir read error now aborts the entire clean

`ListAllItems` propagates `listCleanupLockFiles`'s error (`cleanup.go:463-465`), so an unreadable lock directory kills the whole run — shadow branches, session states and checkpoints included. Compare the redact cache immediately above (`cleanup.go:451-459`), which is deliberately best-effort. Make lock listing best-effort too.

---

## PLAUSIBLE — Low/Medium: lock files are deleted first, before the git work

`DeleteAllCleanupItems` runs the lock-file block (`cleanup.go:557-579`) *before* `DeleteShadowBranches`, `DeleteOrphanedSessionStates`, `DeleteOrphanedCheckpoints`. This maximizes the unprotected window: the locks are gone for the whole remainder of the run, during which clean itself mutates refs. Lock reaping should go last.

---

## Low / nits

- **`cleanup.go:68-71`** duplicates the directory-name literals produced at `checkpoint/persistent_ref_update.go:63` and `checkpoint/shadow_ref.go:84`. Rename either producer and cleanup silently stops working with no compile error. Export constants from `checkpoint`.
- **`cleanup.go:744-748`** — `context.WithTimeout(ctx, 0)` gets try-once semantics only because `AcquireContext`'s loop attempts `LOCK_NB` *before* checking `ctx.Err()` (`flock_unix.go:64-76`). Correct today, undocumented, and silently becomes "never delete anything" if that loop is ever reordered. A named `TryAcquire` in the flock package would be honest.
- `cleanLongDescription()` (`clean.go:22-41`) is not updated to mention lock files.
- `isCleanupLockFileID` does not skip symlinks; `AcquireContext`'s `O_CREATE` would follow one and create the target. Inside `.git`, marginal.

---

## Test assessment (`cleanup_lock_files_test.go`)

Happy path plus one narrow guard; the failure mode is untested.

- `TestLockFiles_ListedAndDeletedByCleanAll` — pure happy path.
- `TestDeleteCleanupLockFiles_HeldLockIsPreserved` — covers only *held at this exact instant*. It is the test that makes the PR look safe, and it passes for a system that is not.

Missing: a waiter blocked on the lock; the open→flock window; the unlink-while-holding window (the actual bug); any concurrency at all; staleness vs live-but-unheld; dry-run; Windows. Both tests correctly omit `t.Parallel()` because of `t.Chdir`, and use `testutil.InitRepo` per repo convention — only the first documents why.

---

## What is good about this PR

- The diagnosis matches the issue exactly, and it correctly closes the pre-existing `entire-shadow-locks` gap alongside the persistent-ref one.
- **`--dry-run` is genuinely safe** — verified with the real binary: identical inodes before and after, no acquire, no delete. `runCleanAllWithItems` returns before `DeleteAllCleanupItems`, and `listCleanupLockFiles` only calls `os.ReadDir`.
- `isCleanupLockFileID` (`cleanup.go:731-742`) is a real path-traversal guard on an exported entry point that accepts caller-supplied IDs — good defensive instinct.
- It quietly fixes a pre-existing reporting gap: redact-cache results were previously missing from `printResultSection` and from both totals (`clean.go:375-376`, `cleanup.go:665-666`). Worth calling out in the description, since it changes the exit code when a redact cache fails to delete.
- Grouping, logging, and result-struct changes follow the existing per-type pattern faithfully.

---

## Suggested path forward

1. Add post-acquire inode revalidation to `flock.AcquireContext` (both `_unix` and `_windows`) — verified to reduce violations from 4,334 to 0 in the multi-process harness. **This is the prerequisite; everything else is secondary.**
2. `release()` before `os.Remove` (fixes Windows; also narrows window (a)).
3. Add an age threshold (e.g. mtime older than some hours) so live-but-unheld locks aren't reaped.
4. Skip held locks silently instead of counting them as failures.
5. Make lock listing best-effort; move lock reaping last.
6. Fix the wrapcheck issue.
7. Add a concurrency test — the 4-process sentinel harness is ~120 lines and would have caught this. I can hand over the three repro files (currently in scratchpad: `zz_repro_deterministic_test.go`, `zz_repro_multiproc_test.go`, `zz_repro_unlink_hazard_test.go`).

The PR branch is fetched locally as `pr-2199` in this worktree; the working tree is back on its own branch and clean.</result>
