## Verified head

`f342d4fbd` (matches; head has not moved). All work in an isolated worktree, nothing pushed, nothing posted to GitHub, no `git stash`.

## Command output

- `mise run fmt` — rewrote nothing (tree clean afterwards)
- `mise run lint` — `0 issues`, exit 0 (re-run after fmt and again at the end)
- `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/... ./cmd/entire/cli/strategy/... ./cmd/entire/cli/gitrepo/... ./cmd/entire/cli/internal/flock/...` — all `ok` (checkpoint 12.9s, remote 14.4s, strategy 72.8s, gitrepo 2.4s, flock 2.0s)
- `-race -count=5` on the new tests — green (flock 1.3s, gitrepo, strategy 5.4s, checkpoint 2.3s)
- `GOOS=windows go build ./...` — exit 0; `go vet ./cmd/...` — exit 0
- Environment: git 2.50.1 (Apple Git-155), darwin

## The two prior findings are genuinely fixed

**1. `ErrShadowRefBusy` conflation — CONFIRMED FIXED.** I reproduced the exact prepare→**abort** scenario (a temporary test through `RewriteUnpushedV1WithOPF`, since the PR's own test uses prepare→commit and never exercises abort). Result:

```
ref after abort  = 8959f14b…   oldTip = 8959f14b…   (ref untouched, as expected)
opfErr = set v1 ref: git update-ref refs/heads/entire/checkpoints/v1: fatal: …
         cannot lock ref '…': Unable to create '….lock': File exists. …
         : git reference lock is unavailable
is ErrRefLocked      = true
is ErrRefCASConflict = false
V1RefMovedError: not produced
```

No more `expected X, found X`. **Caveat below (Finding 1): the push is still rejected**, just with an honest message.

**2. flock predicate — CONFIRMED FIXED, and correctly scoped.**
- 11 `Acquire` callers unaffected: `Acquire` → `AcquireContext(context.Background())`, `Background().Done()` is nil → same blocking `LOCK_EX`.
- `strategy/session_state.go:707` **unaffected** — it only reaches `AcquireContext` inside `if deadline, ok := sessionLockDeadlineFromContext(ctx); ok`, and wraps in `context.WithDeadline`. It already polled. The hot-hook-path concern does not materialize.
- Only `checkpoint/persistent_ref_update.go:65` and `plugin_index.go:199` change behavior. Both receive the root ctx from `cmd/entire/main.go:29` (`context.WithCancel(context.Background())`) → now poll at 25ms. Impact is negligible: the wait they are polling through is another process's condensation (seconds), so ≤25ms added latency and one timer per tick; in exchange both become Ctrl-C-interruptible, which was the point.
- `flock_windows.go` got the identical predicate and identical loop structure with the platform-correct retryable-error set (`ERROR_LOCK_VIOLATION`/`ERROR_IO_PENDING` mirroring `EWOULDBLOCK`). Semantically aligned.

## Findings

### 1. MEDIUM (CONFIRMED) — `ErrRefLocked` is classified but never retried in either OPF path; on reftable repos, entire's *own* concurrent hook aborts the user's `git push`

`cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_opf_rewrite.go:808-824` and `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_opf_refs.go:169`.

The PR deliberately splits `ErrRefLocked` out because "lock contention … does not prove the ref moved" — then treats it as terminal anyway. `atomicSetV1Ref` maps only `ErrRefCASConflict`; everything else falls through to `fmt.Errorf("set v1 ref: %w", err)` → `RewriteUnpushedV1WithOPF` → `manual_commit_push.go:164-170` `return rewriteErr` → **the user's `git push` fails**. Same for the refs path via `warnOPFCheckpointRefsWithheld`.

Every other CAS caller in the PR's call graph *does* retry this: `persistent_ref_update.go:118-127` and both shadow loops (`ephemeral.go:158-166`, `:338-346`) treat `ErrShadowRefBusy` — which `casUpdateRef:45` builds from **both** sentinels — as retryable.

Why it matters in practice: **reftable's ref lock is repo-global.** Verified directly (probe, git 2.50.1) — a prepared `git update-ref --stdin` transaction on a *shadow branch* makes a CAS on the *unrelated* v1 ref fail:

```
--- shadow-branch txn prepared; now CAS the UNRELATED v1 ref ---
fatal: update_ref failed for ref 'refs/heads/entire/checkpoints/v1': cannot lock references
```

Both sides of that collision are entire's own native `git update-ref` (TurnEnd shadow write vs. pre-push OPF). The shadow writer retries; the OPF writer aborts the push.

Loose-ref repos are much narrower — I checked whether `packed-refs.lock` widens it and it does **not**: a held `packed-refs.lock` blocks ref *deletions* only, not updates (updates always land loose). So on loose refs this needs an external writer holding exactly `refs/heads/entire/checkpoints/v1.lock`.

Suggested fix: give the two OPF CAS sites the same bounded retry the persistent path has, on `ErrRefLocked` only.

### 2. LOW (CONFIRMED) — silent contract change + stale doc on `reftableStorer.CheckAndSetReference`

`cmd/entire/cli/gitrepo/reftable.go:213-241`. Unifying on `ref_cas.go`'s classifier widened `isRefCASConflict` with `"unable to resolve reference"`, which also changes the *reftable storer's* mapping to `storage.ErrReferenceHasChanged` (a vanished ref now reports as a conflict). Harmless — I grepped and no CLI code consumes that sentinel any more, only go-git internals — but the comment at `:215-224` still enumerates what must not map to it and still names `strategy.atomicSetV1Ref` as the consumer, which is no longer true after this PR removed that call. Also worth noting the two CAS implementations now diverge on `--no-deref` (`ref_cas.go` has it, `reftable.go:235` does not; the latter special-cases symrefs earlier, so it is correct, just inconsistent).

### 3. INFO (residual scope, pre-existing) — unconditional go-git writes to the same ref remain on the same pre-push flow

`#2204` named two sites and both are closed, but `refs/heads/entire/checkpoints/v1` is still written unconditionally via `repo.Storer.SetReference` (no flock, no native lock participation) by `setRefHash` at `strategy/common.go:246,254,756,775,787,854,1060`, `cleanup.go:395`, `metadata_reconcile.go:198`, `push_common.go:542`. `common.go:246/254` is `fetchAndRebase`, which runs in the **same pre-push flow**, immediately after the newly-hardened OPF CAS, when the remote rejects. The PR's Scope section declares these out of scope and that is defensible — but "v1 can no longer lose an update" is not yet true end-to-end.

### 4. INFO (PLAUSIBLE, pre-existing) — retry-on-vanished-ref can resurrect an externally deleted ref

`persistent_ref_update.go:114-124` + `ref_cas.go:58-62`. Scenario: writer holds the per-ref flock, `build()` reads expected `X`; an external `git update-ref -d`/`git branch -D` removes the ref; the CAS returns `unable to resolve reference` → `ErrRefCASConflict` → `ErrShadowRefBusy` → retry; `build()` re-reads, sees nothing, returns `expected = ZeroHash`; the create-if-absent CAS recreates the ref. Bounded at 16 retries. **Not new** — the pre-PR classifier matched `"cannot lock ref"`, which the vanished-ref message also contains, so this path already retried. `entire clean` cannot trigger it: `cleanup.go:227` CAS-deletes only shadow branches, and nothing in the tree deliberately deletes v1 or `refs/entire/checkpoints/*`.

## What is good

- **Both #2204 sites converted**, and no go-git CAS remains on checkpoint refs anywhere: `CheckAndSetReference` outside `reftableStorer` is gone (only a doc mention at `shadow_ref.go:52`).
- **The unified classifier is correct for both backends.** I captured real git stderr for both formats. All three conflict shapes are **byte-identical** across loose and reftable:
  - `cannot lock ref '…': is at X but expected Y`
  - `cannot lock ref '…': reference already exists`
  - `cannot lock ref '…': unable to resolve reference '…'`
  
  Contention differs and both are matched: loose → `Unable to create '….lock': File exists.` (second clause); reftable → `cannot lock references` (first clause). Precedence is right — a loose CAS mismatch contains `cannot lock ref` but the conflict test runs first; a held reftable lock returns `cannot lock references` for *both* correct and wrong old values, and lock wins, which is correct since git never compared. `gitPlumbingEnv()` pins `LC_ALL=C`, which the English-substring approach requires. Nothing regressed for reftable.
- **`--no-deref` is correct.** Nothing in the codebase creates a symbolic reference under `refs/heads/entire/*` or `refs/entire/*` (only `HEAD` and `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` are ever read as symrefs), so it is a no-op for every ref this code touches and a genuine hardening against a symref redirecting a checkpoint write.
- **`tryDeleteLooseObject` is still gated behind a CONFIRMED conflict.** The #1926 bug stays fixed: `CompareAndSwapRef` checks `ctx.Err()` *before* classifying, so a cancelled `update-ref` returns a context error, which is not `ErrShadowRefBusy`, so `ephemeral.go:159` / `persistent_ref_update.go:118` return early without cleanup. Context cancellation is benign throughout.
- **No lock nesting or deadlock.** Shadow-flock bodies never touch persistent refs and vice versa; `RewriteQueuedCheckpointRefsWithOPF` acquires and releases one per-ref lock at a time (no hold-and-wait); `ensureSessionsBranch` is called *before* `updatePrimaryRef`, not inside it. This matters because flock does self-conflict across two fds in one process — the PR's own `flock_test.go` proves it.
- **The expensive OPF scan is still outside the lock** in both paths; the CAS is the last statement.
- `withLockedPersistentRef` is a faithful behavior-preserving extraction.

## Tests

All four new/changed test groups are genuinely red without the production change. Exact output:

| Reverted hunk | Test | Failure |
|---|---|---|
| `flock_unix.go` predicate → `!hasDeadline` | `TestAcquireContext_CancelWithoutDeadline` | `AcquireContext ignored cancellation because the context had no deadline` |
| `atomicSetV1Ref` → go-git CAS | `TestOPFV1RewriteDoesNotLoseConcurrentCheckpointUpdate` | `Expected error with "git reference lock is unavailable" in chain but got nil` — the #2204 lost update exactly |
| `opf_refs.go:169` → go-git CAS | `TestOPFCheckpointRefRewriteDoesNotLoseConcurrentCheckpointUpdate` | same `…but got nil` |
| drop `--no-deref` | `TestCASPersistentRef_DoesNotDereferenceSymbolicRef` | `CAS must not update the target of a symbolic ref` |
| drop `cannot lock references` clause | `TestCompareAndSwapRef_ReftableLockContention` + classification subtest | `expected: "git reference lock is unavailable" in chain: "…cannot lock references: exit status 128"` |
| drop `unable to resolve reference` clause | `TestUpdatePersistentRef_RebuildsAfterRefIsDeleted` | `Received unexpected error: …unable to resolve reference…` |

Entry points: the strategy tests go through the **public** `RewriteUnpushedV1WithOPF` / `RewriteQueuedCheckpointRefsWithOPF` against a **real** `git update-ref --stdin` transaction — these are the strongest tests here. `checkpoint` tests use public `CASPersistentRef` plus package-internal `updatePersistentRef`. `gitrepo` is mixed: `TestCompareAndSwapRef_ReftableLockContention` uses the public API on a real reftable repo but fabricates the lock by writing `tables.list.lock` directly rather than running a real transaction, and `TestRefCASErrorClassification` reaches into unexported `isRefCASConflict`/`isRefLockContention` with **hand-written** stderr strings, two of which are not verbatim git output (the loose-lock case drops git's `cannot lock ref '…':` prefix; the mismatch case uses `aaaa`/`bbbb`). Nothing in the suite asserts against captured real git stderr for loose-ref contention — I did that out-of-band and the classifier is correct, but the guard against a future git wording change is weaker than it looks. Gaps: no test drives the OPF paths on a reftable repo, and the prepare→**abort** variant (the prior review's actual scenario) is untested.

## Forward look: PR #2199

#2199 adds `entire clean --all` reaping of `entire-shadow-locks` and `entire-persistent-ref-locks`. Its `LOCK_NB`-before-unlink guard is not sufficient: flock binds to the inode, so a process already blocked in `AcquireContext` on the old inode plus a process that `open()`s the path afterwards (fresh inode) can both hold "the" lock. The correct fix is post-acquire `fstat`/`stat` (dev,ino) revalidation with reopen-and-retry, inside `flock.AcquireContext`.

Net effect of #2211 on that fix: **mildly mixed, not a reason to expand this PR.** The poll loop is the natural home for revalidation, and the predicate change moves `entire-persistent-ref-locks` (one of the two reaped dirs) onto it. But `entire-shadow-locks` — the other reaped dir — is acquired exclusively through `flock.Acquire` (`shadow_ref.go:103`), which still takes the `ctx.Done() == nil` blocking `LOCK_EX` path, so the revalidation still has to be written twice. My recommendation: leave #2211 as is, and require the revalidation to land in `flock` as part of #2199 rather than in `clean.go` — #2199 should not merge without it.</result>

## Verdict on the primary question

**Q1 — can this change cause un-redacted (pre-OPF) content to be pushed? No.** I traced every CAS error path that is classified differently after the PR and confirmed each one still lands on a non-nil error, and that every non-nil error aborts the push (`cmd/entire/cli/hooks_git_cmd.go:337-340`, git-branch path) or withholds the flush with the refs left queued (`cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_push.go:399-401`). No reclassification converts a hard failure into a soft one, and no partial rewrite reaches a push.

I measured git's actual stderr for every failure mode against both ref backends (git 2.50.1, `files` and `reftable`) using the exact argv `CompareAndSwapRef` builds:

| failure | files stderr | reftable stderr | class |
|---|---|---|---|
| stale expected value | `cannot lock ref …: is at X but expected Y` | same | CONFLICT |
| ref deleted under a non-zero expected | `…: unable to resolve reference '…'` | same | CONFLICT |
| create-only, ref exists | `…: reference already exists` | same | CONFLICT |
| lock held | `Unable to create '….lock': File exists.` | `cannot lock references` | LOCKED |
| nonexistent object | `trying to write ref … with nonexistent object` | same | HARD |
| bad refname | `refusing to update ref with bad name` | same | HARD |
| D/F conflict | `'refs/heads/dfa' exists; cannot create …` | same | HARD |
| lock held **and** value stale | lock message only | `cannot lock references` | LOCKED → retried, then hard error |

The only widening is `"unable to resolve reference"` joining the conflict set. On the old `casUpdateRef` that message was already caught by the broader `strings.Contains(out, "cannot lock ref")`, so nothing changed there; on `reftableStorer.CheckAndSetReference` (`cmd/entire/cli/gitrepo/reftable.go:239`) it newly maps to `ErrReferenceHasChanged`, but that sentinel now has no consumer in this codebase — `atomicSetV1Ref` was the only one and the PR removed it.

**Q4 — information disclosure: clean.** The nine new error strings (`ref_cas.go:42-52`, `persistent_ref_update.go:46,48,127`) carry only ref names, attempt counts, git hashes and git's own stderr. No prompt text, file contents, or commit messages. The one thing that newly reaches the user's terminal via `warnOPFCheckpointRefsWithheld` is git's absolute lock-file path plus its multi-line "Another git process seems to be running" block — operational, but noisy.

**Q3 — argv injection: clean.** `--end-of-options` works: I verified `--do-not-parse-me` and `-C/tmp/evil` as ref names are passed through as operands, not options, on both backends. Ref names cannot contain spaces (git rejects them), so no ref name can inject the `"but expected"` / `"reference already exists"` substrings the classifier keys on. Findings 1 and 2 below are the *semantic* ref-targeting issues, not argv parsing.

---

## Findings

### 1. `--no-deref` silently clobbers a symbolic ref where the replaced go-git CAS refused it — Medium
`/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/gitrepo/ref_cas.go:34`

**Confirmed by execution.** I ran the old and new CAS side by side against a symbolic ref:

- old (`repo.Storer.CheckAndSetReference`): `reference has changed concurrently`, symref preserved as `symbolic-reference → refs/heads/master`.
- new (`CASPersistentRef` → `git update-ref --no-deref`): `&lt;nil&gt;` — the symref is replaced by a hash reference.
- against `HEAD` directly: `&lt;nil&gt;`, `HEAD` goes from `symbolic-reference → refs/heads/master` to `hash-reference`. **HEAD is detached.**

git accepts the update because it still compares `&lt;oldvalue&gt;` against the *resolved* hash while `--no-deref` makes the *write* target the symref itself. The doc comment ("without dereferencing a symbolic ref") and the PR's own `TestCASPersistentRef_DoesNotDereferenceSymbolicRef` read as protection, but the assertion is `require.NoError` — the test pins the clobber as intended behaviour. The replaced code failed closed here; this one fails open. Suggested fix: reject `plumbing.SymbolicReference` targets in `CompareAndSwapRef`, or pass the symref's literal value as `&lt;oldvalue&gt;` so git rejects the mismatch.

### 2. Queued ref names are never validated, so the OPF rewrite will rewrite and CAS *any* local ref — Medium (pre-existing, but on a line this PR changes)
`/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_opf_refs.go:84-103,169` and `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/pushqueue.go:234`

`readLocked` accepts any non-empty JSON `ref` string verbatim; nothing between there and the CAS checks `checkpoint.CheckpointRefPrefix`.

**Confirmed by execution.** I enqueued `refs/heads/master` (the user's own branch) and ran `RewriteQueuedCheckpointRefsWithOPF`:

```
user branch refs/heads/master before: 01be64d
✓ OpenAI Privacy Filter: done (0.0s, 6 blobs)
user branch refs/heads/master after:  cf2a380
rewritten user commit message: "init\n\nEntire-OPF-Applied: true\n"
```

The user's source blobs were passed to the OPF shell-out, the commits were rebuilt with an `Entire-OPF-Applied` trailer appended to the user's own commit message, and the branch was CAS-moved to the rewritten tip. Precondition is a poisoned queue file (write access to `.git`) or a future caller enqueuing the wrong ref — a weak boundary, but the blast radius is history rewriting plus sending non-checkpoint content to an external filter. `PushQueue.Enqueue` (`pushqueue.go:83`) does not validate either. A one-line guard in the `existing` loop (`if _, ok := checkpoint.ParseRef(refName); !ok { continue }`) closes it. Composed with finding 1, an enqueued `HEAD` both detaches HEAD and rewrites the branch it pointed at.

### 3. New unbounded flock wait on the user's `git push` path — Low/Medium (availability)
`/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_opf_rewrite.go:812` → `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/persistent_ref_update.go:65`

Before the PR `atomicSetV1Ref` took no flock at all. It now routes through `CASPersistentRef` → `withPersistentRefFlock` → `flock.AcquireContext(ctx, path)` with the hook's root context, which is cancelable but has **no deadline** (`cmd/entire/main.go:29`). There is no bound on that wait.

**Confirmed by execution.** Holding `entire-persistent-ref-locks/refs_heads_entire_checkpoints_v1.lock` from another fd, `atomicSetV1Ref` was still blocked after 3s and completed in `3.017s` the instant the lock was released. The same flock is held by `GitStore.updatePrimaryRef` (`cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/store.go:115-124`) across its whole `build` closure — i.e. across condensation tree building — so a concurrent turn-end hook stalls `git push` for as long as that takes. Not a wedge (flock dies with the process) and it fails closed on Ctrl-C, but a bounded acquire here would be cheap.

Related, and **not** a problem: the `retryPersistentRefLockContention` budget itself is small. 16 attempts with `shadowRefBackoff` (8ms jitter for attempts 0-4, 16ms for 5-14, +1ms floor) caps at ~215ms of sleep plus 16 `git update-ref` spawns. It cannot be used to stall a push. And on reftable it is a strict improvement: the pre-PR path (`reftableStorer.CheckAndSetReference` → `cannot lock references` → wrapped error) had *zero* retries, so an unrelated shadow-branch write in another worktree aborted the push outright. *(Reasoned from the verified mechanism — I confirmed by probe that any held `tables.list.lock` makes every ref write in the repo report `cannot lock references`, but did not drive an end-to-end concurrent shadow-branch/v1 race.)*

### 4. The flock change is broader than "OPF ref CAS" — Low
`/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/internal/flock/flock_unix.go:66` and `flock_windows.go:64`

The fast-path guard moved from `!hasDeadline` to `ctx.Done() == nil`. Since the production root context is `context.WithCancel(context.Background())`, **every** context-taking flock call in the real binary switches from a blocking `flock(LOCK_EX)` to a 25ms poll loop. That is three call sites today (`persistent_ref_update.go:65`, `plugin_index.go:200`, `session_state.go:724`), none of which swallow the resulting error, so it is safe — but it also loses kernel wait-queue fairness and adds up to 25ms of latency per contended acquire.

**Confirmed by execution:** a cancelable, deadline-free context now aborts the wait with `flock: context canceled`; pre-PR it would have blocked in the kernel. The PR's new `TestAcquireContext_CancelWithoutDeadline` covers this, but no test covers the deadline-free *production* shape on the CAS path — `TestCASPersistentRef_StopsWaitingWhenContextDeadlineExpires` uses an already-expired deadline, which is the one shape that returns immediately.

### 5. Residual TOCTOU between the OPF CAS and the push — Low, **not introduced by this PR**
`/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_opf_rewrite.go:407-410` vs `manual_commit_push.go:193-199`

`atomicSetV1Ref` releases the persistent-ref flock as soon as the CAS lands; `pushRefIfNeeded` then re-opens the repo (`push_common.go:104-113`) and resolves the ref fresh. Anything that advances `entire/checkpoints/v1` in that window ships un-OPF'd.

I demonstrated the state sequence: after `RewriteUnpushedV1WithOPF` the tip carries `Entire-OPF-Applied`; one normal checkpoint write later the tip a push would resolve does not (`v1 advanced from OPF-applied 25107cc to un-applied a3a05db after the CAS`). This is a design property that predates the PR — the old go-git CAS held no lock either — but this PR is the first to put a lock in this path, so it is the natural place to widen it to cover the push, and the "serializes with Entire's persistent ref writers" comment on `CASPersistentRef` reads as more protection than is actually delivered.

### 6. Out of diff, but on the same invariant: `PushQueuedCheckpointRefs` bypasses OPF entirely
`/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_push.go:428-444`, called from `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/doctor_migrate.go:100`

The migration command's opt-in "push now" flushes the queue with no `redact.OPFEnabled()` check and no `RewriteQueuedCheckpointRefsWithOPF` call, unlike `prePushCheckpointRefs` at line 399. With OPF configured, that path pushes 8-layer content untagged. Untouched by this PR; flagging it because it defeats the fail-closed invariant the PR is protecting.

---

## Clean categories

- **Fail-open from the new classification: none found.** Every one of the eight measured git failure modes maps to a non-nil error, and both callers propagate it.
- **Argv / option injection into `git update-ref`: none.** `--end-of-options` verified effective on both backends. One incidental note: a ref name outside `refs/` writes to `$GIT_DIR/&lt;name&gt;` (my probe left `.git/--do-not-parse-me` and `.git/-C/tmp/evil` on the files backend), which only matters in combination with finding 2 — `..` components are rejected by git, so there is no traversal out of `$GIT_DIR`.
- **User content in the new error/log paths: none.** Metadata only.
- **Retry budget as a DoS: no.** ~215ms ceiling, and the loop cannot be re-armed by an attacker.</result>

<result># Review: PR #2227 — `docs: disclose that pasted images bypass all redaction`

## Verdict: **NEEDS CHANGES**

The core privacy warning is correct and worth shipping, but the mechanism it describes is **opt-in and off by default**, and the PR leaves four absolute contradicting statements plus the file's authoritative coverage table untouched **in the same document**. A security doc that says both "the remote only ever sees 9-layer-redacted content" and "images ship raw to your remote" is worse than either alone.

Nothing here is a *safety* regression — every inaccuracy errs toward claiming less protection than exists, which is the safe direction. That is why this is NEEDS CHANGES, not DO NOT MERGE.

---

## Per-claim findings

### Claim 1 — images externalized as raw binary blobs, no redaction in the write path: **TRUE (mechanism) / OVERSTATED (framing)**

The write path is exactly as described:

- `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/.claude/worktrees/agent-a73fb28a0f0b4b73d/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/persistent.go:1875-1911` — `writeAssets` passes `a.Data` straight to `CreateBlobFromContent`. No redaction call. It is the **only** asset writer; both entry points (`:678`, and `:403` via `writeAssetsForBackfill` at `:1856-1864`) funnel through it.
- Bytes are decoded binary, so "raw binary blob… byte-for-byte identical" is right: `imageextract.go` builds `Asset{… Data: raw}` after `base64.StdEncoding.DecodeString` **and** a re-encode equality check.
- Ordering confirms images are out of the redactor's reach by construction — `manual_commit_condensation.go:216-224` (`prepareTranscriptForStorage`) returns `externalized` (placeholder-bearing) bytes, and redaction runs only on those. Zero `redact.` references exist anywhere in `cmd/entire/cli/transcript/imageextract/` or `cmd/entire/cli/agent/cursor/images.go`.

**But the doc states this unconditionally, and it is gated twice:**

- `cmd/entire/cli/settings/settings.go:1768-1781` — *"Opt-in via `redaction.externalize_images`, or the `ENTIRE_EXTERNALIZE_IMAGES=1` env override… **Off by default**."* Confirmed again at `settings.go:293-296`. All three call sites gate on it (`manual_commit_condensation.go:182`, `:470`; `manual_commit_hooks.go:3200`).
- `cmd/entire/cli/transcript/imageextract/imageextract.go:105-113` — only **Claude Code and Codex** have codecs. Every other agent gets a nil codec and a pass-through.

So in a default install there **is no `assets/` folder**. The paragraph as written reads as false to most users. It needs to cover both states.

Two smaller gaps: `redaction.externalize_images` is documented in **no** markdown file in the repo (zero hits), and the title "pasted images" is under-inclusive — Cursor sidecar images are pulled from Cursor's own SQLite store, never pasted into a transcript, and land in the same unredacted `assets/` store (`cmd/entire/cli/agent/cursor/images.go:59`, invoked at `manual_commit_condensation.go:477`).

### Claim 2 — both OPF rewrite walkers skip `assets/`: **TRUE**

- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_opf_rewrite.go:634` — `collectTreeBlobs`: `if e.Name == assetsDirName { continue }`
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_opf_rewrite.go:709` — `rebuildTreeWithCachedRedaction`: appends the subtree entry verbatim.

Both backends share this single pair: `manual_commit_opf_refs.go:110` calls `collectTreeBlobs`, and `:157` calls `rebuildCheckpointCommit`, which calls `rebuildTreeWithCachedRedaction` at `manual_commit_opf_rewrite.go:562`. The PR's "(or the equivalent per-checkpoint ref on the `git-refs` backend)" is accurate. The code's own comment at `:630-633` calls the transcript "already redaction-skipped".

### Claim 3 — shadow/inline path: **the brief's premise is wrong, and the PR is OVERSTATED for one agent**

I ran a probe against `redact.JSONLBytes` with a real 900-byte PNG payload (test written, run, then deleted; tree is clean):

| Shape | base64 survives | `REDACTED` present |
|---|---|---|
| Claude Code `{"type":"image","source":{"type":"base64",…}}` | **yes** | no |
| Codex `{"type":"input_image","image_url":"data:image/png;base64,…"}` | no | **yes** |
| Codex `{"type":"function_call_output","output":"…data:image/png;base64,…"}` | no | **yes** |

Mechanism: `redact/redact.go:989-991` does `if shouldSkipJSONLObject(val) { return }` — an early return that skips the **entire subtree**, including `source.data`. `shouldSkipJSONLObject` (`redact/redact.go:1052-1055`) matches `strings.HasPrefix(t, "image")`. `"input_image"` does **not** have prefix `"image"`, so Codex blocks fall through to the entropy layer and get destroyed.

Consequences, both ways:

- **The PR is broader-true than the brief assumed.** For Claude Code, images are unredacted *inline too* — no externalization needed. The right scoping is therefore **not** "committed/pushed checkpoints"; it is that the `assets/` mechanism is opt-in while the unscanned property holds inline regardless.
- **The PR is overstated for Codex.** "None of them ever inspect image content" is false there: the entropy scanner reads the base64 and replaces it.
- **Two existing docs are simply wrong** and the PR leaves them standing: `docs/architecture/sessions-and-checkpoints.md:276-280` and `CLAUDE.md:1284` both assert "inline images in a shadow transcript are subject to redaction". True for Codex, false for Claude Code.

### Claim 4 — the JSONL skip-rules footnote: **substantively RIGHT**

The reframing is correct and is a genuine improvement — main's `docs/security-and-privacy.md:515` presents the image skip as pure false-positive tuning, when `shouldSkipJSONLObject` is unconditional and the OPF code comment itself describes the transcript as "already redaction-skipped".

One carried-over imprecision, pre-existing rather than introduced: "fields named `signature`" — the code skips **any key ending in** `signature` (`redact/redact.go:1032`, `strings.HasSuffix`), e.g. `thinkingSignature`. Cheap to fix while the line is being edited.

The **"nine passes"** count checks out: doc enumeration at `docs/security-and-privacy.md:28-37` (6 secret + 7th custom + 8th PII + 9th OPF) matches `redact/redact.go:184-262` (8 regex layers) plus OPF appended by `StringWithPrivacyFilter`.

- `docs/security-and-privacy.md:246` — "**The remote only ever sees 9-layer-redacted content** when OPF is enabled." Falsified by `manual_commit_opf_rewrite.go:634`.

## 1. Merge cleanliness

Merges cleanly, but **it is not based on current main.** Base is `4c68dd5b5`; `origin/main` has moved 7 commits ahead. The merge is conflict-free and touches disjoint files (`repo_clone.go`, `runnerdefaults/`, `CLAUDE.md`), so no rebase is required for correctness.

## 2. Is the factual claim TRUE? — **Partly. This is the blocking finding.**

The two flags named are **verbatim correct**, not paraphrases:

- `cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/spawner.go:25` — `claude -p --permission-mode bypassPermissions &lt;prompt&gt;` ✅
- `cmd/entire/cli/agent/codex/spawner.go:31-38` — `codex exec --skip-git-repo-check --dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox -` ✅

Both are unconditional — nothing gates them on `--issue-link` or any flag. That half of the PR's premise holds.

**But `entire investigate` spawns a third agent that the notice misdescribes.** `cmd/entire/cli/investigate_bridge.go:37-46` (`launchableSpawnerFor`) returns spawners for **claude-code, codex, AND gemini** — and `cmd/entire/cli/agent/geminicli/spawner.go:22-27` builds:

```go
cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "gemini", "-p", " ")
cmd.Stdin = strings.NewReader(prompt)
```

No bypass flag at all. Pinned by its own test at `cmd/entire/cli/agent/geminicli/spawner_test.go:25` (`wantArgs := []string{"gemini", "-p", " "}`). I also grepped `geminicli/` and `agentlaunch/` for `yolo|approval-mode|autoAccept|trust` — zero hits, and `geminicli/generate.go` writes no auto-approval settings either.

Gemini is genuinely reachable, not theoretical: `investigate/picker.go:67-80` makes eligibility "non-nil spawner + hooks installed", and `investigate/cmd.go:846-866` (`verifyAgentsLaunchable`) applies the same rule. `entire investigate --agents gemini` is a supported configuration.

So:

- **`cmd.go:378-379`** — the doc comment asserts *"Every agent this package spawns runs with sandbox and approval checks unconditionally disabled"*. **This is false** and it gets committed as an in-code fact future readers will trust.
- **`cmd.go:392-395`** — the user-facing string leads with the unqualified *"entire investigate spawns AI agents with sandbox and approval checks disabled"*, then enumerates only two agents. On a gemini-only run the operator is handed a security banner naming two flags that will never be passed, about a run that is actually approval-gated. The notice is printed unconditionally regardless of which agents were selected.

**Suggested fix** (cheap, and both call sites already have `agents` in scope): either scope the sentence — "Claude Code and Codex agents in this run spawn with…" — or, better, name the agents actually selected for this run and only warn for the two that bypass.

Secondary wording nit at the same lines: for Claude Code, `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` disables *permission prompting*, not a sandbox. Only Codex's flag genuinely covers both. Lumping them under "sandbox and approval checks disabled" overstates the Claude Code case. This echoes pre-existing house wording at `cmd.go:419` ("permission/sandbox bypass"), so it is a nit on its own — but worth fixing in the same pass since the sentence is being rewritten anyway.

I verified the string concatenation renders correctly (no missing or doubled spaces) by compiling the const standalone.

## 3. Streams and print count — **correct**

- Both call sites use `cmd.ErrOrStderr()` (`cmd.go:528`, `cmd.go:730`), never `fmt.Print*`. Complies with CLAUDE.md "Logging vs User Output". Stdout stays clean for `writeRunManifest`.
- **No double-print with `confirmUntrustedIssueSeed`.** `cmd.go:726-731` is a strict `if len(issueSeed) &gt; 0 { confirm… } else { notice }`. And `validateFlags` (`cmd.go:163-169`) makes `[seed-doc]`, `--issue-link`, and `--continue` mutually exclusive, so `runFresh` and `runContinue` are exclusive dispatch targets (`cmd.go:368-372`). Exactly one notice per run.

Two placement imprecisions, both cosmetic:

- **`cmd.go:528` sits before the guard at `cmd.go:540`** (`state.NextAgentIdx &gt;= len(agents)`), so a `--continue` that hard-fails still emits a security banner followed immediately by an error. Same shape in `runFresh`: printed at `cmd.go:730`, before `Bootstrap`/`NewStateStore`/manifest. The doc comment's "printed once per investigate run … that reaches agent spawn" is therefore slightly stronger than the code.
- **`cmd.go:730` fires *after* the interactive multipicker** (`cmd.go:680-690`). Interactively, the operator sees the warning only once they have already committed their agent selection. Arguably it belongs before the picker. Not a correctness problem — it still lands before any spawn, and it goes to stderr while the inline Bubble Tea TUI writes to stdout (`tui_sink.go:32-36`, no `WithAltScreen` at startup), so it stays in scrollback.

## 4. Non-blocking — **confirmed**

Plain `fmt.Fprintln` at both sites. No prompt, no `interactive.CanPromptInteractively()`, no `IsTerminalWriter`, no stdin read, no TTY branch. Non-interactive/agent use is entirely unaffected, and because it is on stderr it cannot corrupt machine-readable stdout. Satisfies CLAUDE.md "Agent-Safe CLI Fallbacks".

Correctly **absent** from `entire investigate fix`: `agentlaunch/launch.go:31-58` uses `agent.LauncherFor(...).LaunchCmd(...)`, a normal interactive session with no bypass flags — so omitting the notice there is right, not an oversight.

## 5. Scope and conventions — **clean**

Two files, +64/-0, nothing beyond the title. New test at `cmd_test.go:300-338` mirrors its neighbor's structure exactly and correctly omits `t.Parallel()` (the helper `setupInvestigateRepo` calls `t.Chdir`). `dupl` did not fire.

**Test gap worth closing with the fix:** only the fresh non-issue-link path is covered. The PR description claims the notice fires "on `--continue`" too, and that is untested. The assertions also use `strings.Contains`, which structurally cannot detect a double print — `strings.Count(...) == 1` plus a `--continue` case would actually pin the claim being made.

Placement, streams, print count, and non-blocking behavior are all correct, and the two flags it names are real. The blocker is narrow: the notice and its doc comment claim *every* investigate agent runs unsandboxed, when the third spawnable agent (gemini) passes no bypass flag at all. Fixing the sentence to name only the agents that actually bypass — ideally the ones selected for the run — plus the `cmd.go:378-379` comment is roughly a 10-minute change, after which I would approve.

<result>## Verdict: **NEEDS CHANGES** — the core fix is correct and genuinely proven, but `mise run lint` fails (exit 3), the exported API has a verified unbounded self-deadlock, and one error path silently orphans checkpoint data.

### 1. Merge against `origin/main`: clean, but semantically broken against #2211

- `git merge-tree --write-tree --messages origin/main pr2226` → tree `2627dca27`, **no conflicts**. Merges cleanly with `main` at `fce049cd3`.
- **#2211 (`fix(checkpoint): serialize OPF ref updates`, OPEN/MERGEABLE) — silent compile break.** It touches both files this PR touches. #2211 rewrites `casUpdateRef` to delegate to a new `gitrepo.CompareAndSwapRef` and **removes the `os/exec` import** from `cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/persistent_ref_update.go`. This PR adds `casDeleteRef` to that same file using `exec.CommandContext`. I merged them: `git merge-tree --write-tree pr2226 pr2211-review` reports only "Auto-merging" — **no conflict** — but the resulting file (tree `d7649b4cc`) has no `os/exec` in its import block and `exec.CommandContext` at line 57. Whichever lands second reds `main` with an undefined-symbol build failure that git will not flag.
  - Also semantic: #2211 redefines `ErrShadowRefBusy` from "CAS mismatch" to "changed **or** its native Git lock is held" (`ErrRefLocked`). `migrateShadowBranchRefLocked` treats `ErrShadowRefBusy` as a *benign* race and bails with `(false, nil)` / `(true, nil)`. After #2211, transient lock contention would be misread as a benign race and the migration would silently no-op.
  - Finally, #2211 exists precisely to consolidate the `strings.Contains(out, "cannot lock ref")` output-scraping into `gitrepo`; this PR adds a fresh copy of it at `persistent_ref_update.go:77`.
- **#2131 (`perf(checkpoint): batch persistent session writes with CAS`, OPEN/CONFLICTING) — duplicate implementation.** It adds `checkpoint.MoveShadowBranch` (`shadow_ref.go:150` on that branch) which does *the same thing* — locks both branches in a sorted, **de-duplicated** list and CASes — and rewires the *same* body of `migrateShadowBranchToBaseCommit`. `git merge-tree pr2226 pr2131-review` gives 5 content conflicts plus a modify/delete on `persistent_ref_update.go` (#2131 deletes the file). One of these two implementations should be dropped before either lands.
- **#2190** touches `strategy/` but not the migration or shadow-ref code — no overlap.

### 2. Build / lint / tests

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | **PASS** |
| `mise run lint` | **FAIL — exit 3, 5 issues** |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/... ./cmd/entire/cli/strategy/...` | **PASS** (checkpoint 34.6s, strategy 178.7s) |

Lint failures (all introduced by this PR):
- `cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/shadow_ref_migration_test.go:20` and `:121` — `noctx`: `os/exec.Command must not be called. use os/exec.CommandContext`
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_migration.go:98` — `revive` unused-parameter: `repo *git.Repository` is now dead in `migrateShadowBranchToBaseCommit` (the go-git calls were the only users). `unparam` runs with `check-exported: true` here too.
- `shadow_ref_migration_test.go:75,76` — `testifylint` go-require: `runGitOut` (which calls `require.NoErrorf`) is invoked from the writer goroutine. This is not just style — `require` there calls `t.FailNow()`/`runtime.Goexit()` off the test goroutine, so a `git commit` failure would skip the `writerErr` assignment entirely and `require.NoError(t, writerErr)` at line 106 would pass on a bogus nil.

Per CLAUDE.md's "Before Every Commit (REQUIRED)", `mise run check` was clearly not run.

### 3. Does the new test prove the fix? **Yes — red/green verified.**

- **RED**: I reverted only the production change (replaced the two `withShadowBranchFlock` wrappers at `shadow_ref.go:168-169` with a direct call to `migrateShadowBranchRefLocked`, keeping the test verbatim). `go test -run TestMigrateShadowBranchRef -count=5` → **5/5 FAIL** at `shadow_ref_migration_test.go:106` (`require.NoError(t, writerErr)`) — the unlocked migration deletes the ref under the writer, whose CAS then fails. Exactly the described race.
- **GREEN**: restored, then `-race -count=10` → ok (10.0s); `-race -count=30` → ok (25.8s). **No flakiness, no race reports.**

Two caveats on the test's own claims:
- Its comment says "with real synchronization (not sleeps)" and then does `time.Sleep(20 * time.Millisecond)` at line 96. The *green* direction is deterministic (the flock guarantees ordering), but the *red* direction depends on that 20ms window, so the test's ability to catch a future regression is timing-dependent on a loaded CI box. Fix the comment, or replace the sleep with a signal.
- It calls `checkpoint.MigrateShadowBranchRef` directly. **No test covers the production call path** (`migrateShadowBranchToBaseCommit` → the new helper), so nothing verifies the strategy-side wiring — including the `state.WorktreePath`/`GetGitCommonDir` argument sourcing flagged in §4.

### 4. Is `MigrateShadowBranchRef` correct?

**(a) Two-flock ordering — correct for distinct names, but self-deadlocks on equal names.**

The sort at `shadow_ref.go:163-166` is a stable lexicographic total order, so two migrations touching the same pair from opposite directions cannot deadlock. Both orderings *are* reachable: two sessions sharing one worktree (documented: main agent + Task subagent) with different `BaseCommit`s can migrate A→B and B→A if HEAD moves back and forth. I also grepped every `withShadowBranchFlock` caller — `ephemeral.go:117` and `:316` each take exactly one lock and never nest — so no writer/migrator cycle exists. The ordering is genuinely needed and genuinely correct.

**But the degenerate `oldBranch == newBranch` case is not handled.** `flock.AcquireIn` opens a fresh fd each call, and POSIX `flock` locks bind to the open file *description*, so the second acquire blocks even in the same process. I proved this against the real code with a throwaway probe in the `checkpoint` package: nested `withShadowBranchFlock(commonDir, "entire/same", …)` **DEADLOCKS** (still blocked after 3s). The only current caller guards this via the hash-prefix-collision check at `manual_commit_migration.go:112`, but the function is **exported**, its doc comment doesn't warn, and #2131's competing version de-duplicates. Add a `if oldBranch == newBranch { return false, nil }` guard.

**Relatedly, the locks ignore `ctx`.** `withShadowBranchFlock` → `flock.AcquireIn` → `AcquireContext(context.Background())` — the unbounded blocking kernel path. This function is now called from the **TurnStart** hook (`manual_commit_hooks.go:2620`), and it now takes **two** unbounded locks there. CLAUDE.md documents `flock.AcquireContext` as existing specifically so "latency-critical hooks (turn-start) bound their wait and degrade gracefully instead of stalling behind a long-running lock holder (e.g. checkpoint condensation)". `MigrateShadowBranchRef` accepts a `ctx` but only threads it to the git subprocesses. It should use the ctx-bounded acquire.

**(b) Crash between CAS-create and CAS-delete — recoverable, and explicitly handled.** Both refs exist at the same hash; the next run reads old (exists) and new (exists, `newHash == oldHash`) at `shadow_ref.go:192-207` and finishes the delete. Good, deliberate design. Residual: if the crash lands after create but before `state.BaseCommit` is persisted *and* state was advanced by another path, the old ref lingers as an orphan — harmless, and `cleanup.go` covers it.

**(c) Lock window — correct.** Both reads and both writes happen inside both flocks, in the same lock domain as `writeCheckpoint`/`writeTask`. That is the right span.

**Two correctness problems inside the window:**

- **`readRefHash` (`shadow_ref.go:126-137`) conflates "ref absent" with "git failed for any reason."** It maps *any* `*exec.ExitError` to `(ZeroHash, false, nil)`. I verified with real git: `git rev-parse --verify refs/heads/nope` exits **128**, and `git rev-parse --verify` in a non-repo also exits **128**. So a wrong/stale `repoRoot`, a corrupt repo, or a permissions failure all report "the old shadow branch doesn't exist" → `migrateShadowBranchRefLocked` returns `(false, nil)` → the caller advances `state.BaseCommit` and logs INFO "updated session base commit" (`manual_commit_migration.go:135-141`). The old shadow branch and its uncondensed checkpoints are **silently orphaned** — condensation resolves the shadow ref from `state.BaseCommit` (`manual_commit_condensation.go:532-533`), so that content is unreachable. It also uses `cmd.Output()`, so stderr is discarded and nothing is logged. This is the exact class of silent loss the PR is meant to close. Only exit code 1, or an empty-output/`unknown revision` classification, should mean "absent".

- **The destination-exists-with-different-content path (`shadow_ref.go:197-201`) silently orphans too.** It returns `(false, nil)`, which the caller cannot distinguish from "old branch never existed" — same INFO log, no warning. Two sessions in one worktree with different `BaseCommit`s migrating to the same new base hit this: the second session's shadow branch is abandoned. `main`'s behavior was different (`SetReference` clobbered the destination). Neither is right, but the PR changes *which* data is lost without saying so and without a `logging.Warn`. This needs a distinguishable return or at minimum a warn-level log naming the orphaned branch.

### 5. Scope — tight, with one unadvertised behavior change

Nothing beyond the title's subject matter: `casDeleteRef` and `readRefHash` are supporting pieces, and no drive-by edits. However, the migration's **error posture changes from soft to hard** and this isn't mentioned: `main` logged-and-continued when `DeleteBranchCLI` failed (`manual_commit_migration.go`, old lines 137-142), whereas the new code hard-errors the whole hook on a `GetGitCommonDir` failure (line 126-129), a non-`ExitError` `readRefHash` failure, or any non-busy CAS failure. Concretely: `state.WorktreePath` is passed as `repoRoot` at line 131, and a stale/removed worktree path (a scenario the repo tests explicitly — `manual_commit_reconcile_worktree_test.go:24` uses `WorktreePath: gonePath`) makes `cmd.Dir` chdir fail with an `*os.PathError`, which is *not* an `ExitError`, so it propagates and fails TurnStart. The old go-git path used the already-open `repo` and could not fail this way.

Also note the argument sourcing is inconsistent: `repoRoot` comes from `state.WorktreePath` while `commonDir` comes from `strategy.GetGitCommonDir(ctx)`, which runs git with `cmd.Dir = "."` (process cwd) and — despite its comment — does *not* absolutize (`filepath.Join(".", ".git")` is still `.git`). I verified the shapes git actually returns: `.git` from a repo root, `../.git` from a subdir, absolute from a linked worktree. These happen to resolve to the same inode as the writer's absolute `resolveGitCommonDir` result in every case I could construct, so the lock domain does hold today — but the two halves of one call should not be sourced from two different notions of "which repo", especially given CLAUDE.md's rule against cwd-derived git paths.

### 6. Repo conventions

- No `git.PlainOpen`/`git.Open` outside `gitrepo` — the PR actually **removes** go-git ref usage here. Good.
- No go-git `worktree.Status()`. Good.
- `t.Parallel()` on both new tests, isolated `t.TempDir()` repos via `testutil.InitRepo`/`WriteFile`/`GitAdd`/`GitCommit`. Good.
- Violations: the `noctx` `exec.Command` calls and the `require` calls from a non-test goroutine (both above).

---

### What was done well

- The diagnosis is right and the fix is the right shape: routing the migration through the *same* `withShadowBranchFlock` + CAS discipline as `writeCheckpoint`/`writeTask`, rather than inventing a parallel mechanism.
- The red/green is real, not decorative — 5/5 red with only the locking removed, 30/30 green under `-race`.
- The create-then-delete crash window is genuinely thought through and handled idempotently.
- Comments explain *why* (the doc block on `MigrateShadowBranchRef` and the replacement rationale in `manual_commit_migration.go:117-125`) rather than restating the code.

### Minimum to merge

1. Fix the 5 lint issues (drop the dead `repo` param; `exec.CommandContext`; hoist the goroutine's assertions onto the test goroutine).
2. Guard `oldBranch == newBranch` in `MigrateShadowBranchRef`, and use the ctx-bounded flock acquire on the TurnStart path.
3. Make `readRefHash` distinguish "ref absent" (exit 1 / unresolvable) from a real git failure, and warn+return a distinguishable result on the destination-conflict path at `shadow_ref.go:197`.
4. Decide between this and #2131's `MoveShadowBranch`, and coordinate the `os/exec` import with #2211 — otherwise the second of the two to merge breaks the build with no conflict marker.

Roughly half a day of work. The design does not need to change.</result>

### 3. Red/green claim — VERIFIED, with one correction

I reverted **only** `checkpoint_api_reader.go` to `origin/main` and kept the new tests:

- `TestAPICheckpointReader_RepoFullNameMismatchIsRejected` → **FAIL**, "An error is expected but got nil" (test:298)
- `TestAPICheckpointReader_CheckpointIDMismatchIsRejected` → **FAIL** (test:344)
- `TestAPICheckpointReader_RepoFullNameAsRepoIDIsAccepted` → **PASSES pre-fix too**

So the claim holds for the two mismatch tests. The third is an over-rejection guard, not a reproduction — worth saying so, since the PR body describes the test work as one reproduction.

### 4. `verifyResponseIdentity` correctness

**Case handling is correct, and `EqualFold` is required — not merely conventional.** `parseExplainRepoFlag` → `parseMirrorCloneRef` lowercases both coordinates (`repo_clone.go:162`), so `r.ownerRepo` is *always* lowercase, while the cell's `repo_full_name` is a GitHub display name that preserves case. A plain `==` would have broken every repo with capitals in its name. I verified empirically: `"Acme/Widgets"` → accepted, `err = nil`. The PR gets this right.

**Important — the empty-field handling is asymmetric, and backwards** (`checkpoint_api_reader.go:388` vs `:399`):

- empty `repo_full_name` → deliberately tolerated (documented in the comment)
- absent/empty `checkpointId` → **hard failure of the whole command**. Verified by dropping the field: `checkpoint identity mismatch: … the server returned checkpoint ""; … (this looks like a server-side bug, please report it)`

Why that matters more than it looks: **both fields were declared but never read before this PR** (`git grep '\.CheckpointID' origin/main -- cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint_api_reader.go` returns no read site). There is no OpenAPI spec or generated client for the cell's `CheckpointInfo` anywhere in the repo — the struct is hand-written to "mirror entire-api's httpapi.CheckpointInfo". The envelope even mixes conventions in a single payload: snake_case at top level (`checkpoint_id`, `repo_full_name`) and camelCase inside `checkpoint` (`checkpointId`, `commitSha`). So this PR promotes an unverified decode assumption into a hard 100% failure of `explain --repo`, with an error that blames the server and tells the user to file a bug. Either tolerate empty `checkpointId` the way empty `repo_full_name` is tolerated, or confirm the field against a live cell first. **I could not verify against a live cell** (no credentials, and I would not hit prod for a review). Note the envelope's top-level `checkpoint_id` is a second identity signal that is available and not decoded at all.

**Does sourcing `CheckpointID` from the response introduce new rejections? No.** The check requires exact equality, so whenever it passes, `info.CheckpointID` and `checkpointID.String()` are the same string and `--json` output is byte-identical. All rejection risk lives in the check, not the sourcing. The re-sourcing is defensible belt-and-braces but strictly redundant while the check stands; its stated benefit is guarding a hypothetical future regression in the check.

**One real legitimate-response rejection:** a GitHub repo rename where the cell's metadata is staler than the control plane's. `lookupRepoIndexEntry` (`cell_target.go:257`) already EqualFold-matched the name against the control plane before the reader is built, so these are two independently-synced services; a disagreement now hard-fails the read with "this looks like a server-side bug". Consider degrading the *repo-name* mismatch to a warning while keeping the *checkpoint-ID* mismatch fatal — the checkpoint ID is what actually scopes the data.

**Minor, all verified:**
- `:399` omits the `strings.TrimSpace` that the cited convention at `cell_target.go:257` applies to both sides. `"acme/widgets "` → rejected. Gratuitous divergence from the pattern the PR's own comment invokes.
- `got != r.repoID` on the same line is case-*sensitive* while its sibling is `EqualFold`. Low risk (ULIDs are canonical uppercase), but inconsistent within one expression.
- **Ordering:** the check sits at `:369`, *after* the no-sessions guard at `:366`. A wrong-repo payload with zero sessions reports "has no sessions to explain" instead of the mismatch. No data is rendered, so not a leak — but a check whose whole point is "don't trust this payload" should be the first thing after the nil guard.
- Correctly placed **before** the cache write (`:369` precedes `:373`), so a poisoned response is never memoized. Good.
- No nil-panic: `verifyResponseIdentity` dereferences `env.Checkpoint` unguarded, but `loadDetail:363` checks it. Single call site, fine — the doc comment just doesn't state the precondition.

Two files, 107 insertions, one package. One construction site (`explain_repo.go:48`), so blast radius is `entire checkpoint explain --repo` only. The `CheckpointID` re-sourcing is slightly past the literal title but directly motivated and documented. No CLAUDE.md update — defensible at this granularity.

Observation, not a defect of this PR: `experts_cmd.go:80` decodes the same `repo_full_name` from the same cell with no equivalent check, so the new "content-layer self-check" pattern lands inconsistently.

Harmless (the reader doesn't validate response IDs), but a real server bug would return a *valid* different ULID, so a valid fixture tests more.

## Verdict: **DO NOT MERGE**

The fix is directionally right about the threat but the mechanism it chose — "is this position inside a tool payload?" keyed on Anthropic block names — is wrong in both directions. It closes the hole for 3 of 8 agents, leaves it open for 5, and on the paths where it *does* fire it shreds legitimate image data.

---

### 1. The bypass survives for 5 of 8 agents — and for Claude Code itself

`isToolPayloadField` (`redact/redact.go:1051-1059`) matches only `type:"tool_use"`+`input` and `type:"tool_result"`+`content`/`output`. I checked the real on-disk fixtures, not the docs. None of these agents produce that shape:

| Agent | Real invocation `type` / args key | Real result `type` / output key | Fixture |
|---|---|---|---|
| **codex** (rollout) | `function_call` / `arguments` | `function_call_output` / `output` | `cmd/entire/cli/transcript/compact/testdata/codex_full.jsonl` |
| **gemini** | *no `type` key at all* / `args` | *no `type` key* / `result[].functionResponse.response.output` | `.../testdata/gemini_full.jsonl` |
| **opencode** | `tool` / `state.input` | same object / `state.output` | `.../testdata/opencode_full.jsonl` |
| **copilot-cli** | `function` or `tool.execution_start` / `arguments` | `tool.execution_complete` / `data.result.content` | `.../testdata/copilot_full.jsonl` |
| **pi** | `toolCall` / `arguments` | `message`+`role:"toolResult"` / `content` | `cmd/entire/cli/transcript/compact/pi_test.go:13` |

Conforming: claude-code, cursor, factoryai-droid only.

Note the near-miss on Codex: `function_call_output` genuinely uses the key `output`, but the PR only honours `output` under `blockType == "tool_result"`, so it does not match.

I reproduced the PR's own evasion shape in each agent's real wire format and ran it against the PR build:

```
claude-code (PR's shape, expected FIXED)      -&gt; caught (fixed)
claude-code toolUseResult (sibling of message) -&gt; *** BYPASS SURVIVES ***
codex rollout function_call_output            -&gt; *** BYPASS SURVIVES ***
gemini toolCalls[].args                       -&gt; *** BYPASS SURVIVES ***
opencode part type:tool state.output          -&gt; *** BYPASS SURVIVES ***
copilot tool.execution_complete data.result   -&gt; *** BYPASS SURVIVES ***
pi toolCall arguments                         -&gt; *** BYPASS SURVIVES ***
```

The `toolUseResult` row is the one I'd flag hardest, because it defeats the fix on the agent the PR was actually written for. Claude Code writes raw tool output to a **top-level `toolUseResult` object that is a sibling of `message`**, not inside any `tool_result` block. I confirmed the shape in `claude_full.jsonl`: top-level keys include `toolUseResult` with `{interrupted, isImage, noOutputExpected, stderr, stdout}` on a line whose `type` is `"user"`. `isToolPayloadField("user", "toolUseResult")` returns false, so a spoofed `{"type":"base64",…}` landing there is still skipped wholesale. That is where an MCP tool's structured result lives.

Redaction runs on the **raw native transcript before compaction** (`cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/persistent.go:1011`), so no normalization saves this — the walk sees each agent's own key names.

### 2. It over-corrects, and destroys real image data

The code comment at `redact/redact.go:991-994` asserts: *"Genuine externalized image assets (pasted screenshots) are message content blocks, not tool call input/output."* The repo's own image externalizer contradicts this in a comment at `cmd/entire/cli/transcript/imageextract/imageextract.go:284-285`: images are collected *"at any nesting depth (**top-level content, tool_result content**, etc.)"*, and for Codex *"input_image image_url values, user messages, and **function_call_output** content alike"* (`imageextract.go:272-274`). Any image-returning tool — `Read` on a `.png`, a Playwright/browser MCP screenshot — puts its base64 in exactly the position the PR now un-skips.

This matters because the shadow/Stop write path does **not** externalize images before redacting. `cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/redact_cache.go:55-58` states it directly: *"the metadata walk stores a sanitized transcript, while condensation and finalize store a sanitized and image-externalized one."* So on every Stop, tool-returned screenshots now reach the regex/entropy layers raw.

Measured on the PR build, `JSONLContent` on a single realistic line (`go test ./redact/`, scratch harness, since removed):

| Decoded image | Top-level image block (Case A) | Same image inside `tool_result.content` (Case B) |
|---|---|---|
| 100 KB | 10.5 ms, unmodified | **82.8 ms, modified** |
| 400 KB | 1.9 ms, unmodified | **382 ms, modified** |
| 1 MB | 4.5 ms, unmodified | **1.194 s, modified** |

`modified=true` is the worse half. A 68,471-byte tool_result image line came out at 12,237 bytes, with the base64 shredded:

```
"data":"U4x/REDACTED/REDACTED/REDACTED/REDACTED/qONXa2/tJ/REDACTED/REDACTED/…
```

The file-path layer matches `/`-containing base64 runs and the entropy layer takes the rest. That is destructive corruption of a legitimate asset in the stored shadow transcript, which `CLAUDE.md` lists as the source for rewind/resume/explain. The identical top-level image is untouched, which is precisely the asymmetry the PR introduces.

Two amplifiers: `imageextract.go:110-113` registers codecs for **Claude Code and Codex only**, so for Cursor/Gemini/OpenCode/Copilot/Droid/Pi there is no externalization on *any* path and `shouldSkipJSONLObject` is the sole protection; and `minExternalizedBase64Len`/`maxExternalizedImageBytes` (`imageextract.go:96-104`) leave small and oversized images inline even on committed paths.

### 3. Tests: red/green verified, but only one direction is covered

I reverted `redact/redact.go` to `origin/main` and kept `redact_test.go` from the PR. Confirmed RED on both sub-assertions:

```
redact_test.go:1323: secret inside a tool_use block's own input must not be hidden … got [], want [{"payload" … "REDACTED"}]
redact_test.go:1338: secret inside a tool_result block's own content … got [], want [{"data" … "REDACTED"}]
--- FAIL: TestCollectJSONLReplacements_ToolPayloadCannotSpoofImageSkip
```

Restored, GREEN. So the spoof direction is genuinely proven.

The other direction is **not** covered. The only "genuine image still skipped" test, `REDACTED` (`redact_test.go:1263-1289`), uses a bare top-level `{"type":"image","data":…}`. There is no test for an image block nested in `tool_result.content` — and the PR's own new test at `redact_test.go:1327-1334` asserts that shape *is* now scanned, using `{"type":"image","data":secret}` inside `tool_result.content`. That test is indistinguishable from the legitimate screenshot case; it encodes the regression as intended behavior. Tests for the five non-conforming agent shapes are absent entirely.

Minor: the new test omits `t.Parallel()`, which `CLAUDE.md` requires (the neighbouring test omits it too, so pre-existing).

The PR's own comment points readers at that doc for the rationale, so it needs updating with the behavior change.

---

### What would make this mergeable

The position-based gate is the wrong axis. Two directions worth considering instead, either of which fixes both failure modes at once:

1. **Skip on structure, not on a self-declared tag.** Only skip a subtree when it actually looks like an image block — e.g. Claude's `{"type":"image","source":{"type":"base64","media_type":…,"data":…}}` with a decodable `data` leaf, matching what `collectClaudeImages` (`imageextract.go:287-300`) already recognizes — and skip only the `data` leaf rather than the whole subtree. A spoofed `{"type":"base64","payload":secret}` has no such structure and would be scanned regardless of where it sits; a real screenshot stays skipped regardless of where it sits. This is agent-independent, which is what the per-agent table above says you need.
2. **Skip only the leaf, never the subtree.** The original bug is the unconditional `return` at what is now `redact.go:1011` discarding *siblings*. Continuing the walk but exempting only the large base64-looking value keeps the entropy-layer cost off the image while leaving no hiding place next to it.

Whichever route, please add: the errcheck fix, a test per non-Anthropic agent shape (codex `function_call_output`, gemini `args`, opencode `state.output`, copilot `data.result`, pi `arguments`, plus Claude's `toolUseResult`), a test that a genuine image inside `tool_result.content` is still skipped and byte-preserved, and the `docs/security-and-privacy.md:515` update.

**What I ran:** `git fetch origin pull/2234/head:pr2234`; `git diff origin/main...pr2234`; `git merge-tree --write-tree`; `go build ./...`; `mise run lint`; `go test` on `./redact/...`, `./cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/...`, `./cmd/entire/cli/transcript/...`, `./cmd/entire/cli/strategy/...`; a red/green revert of `redact/redact.go` alone; and three scratch tests in package `redact` (timing, corruption, per-agent bypass) which I deleted afterwards — worktree is clean at `a71196b98`. Nothing was pushed or posted; no `git stash` used.</result>

## 1. Does it strand old-format branches? Yes, it does — and the code comment says the opposite

The old format is real and shipped. `ShadowBranchNameForCommit` had no suffix at all until `4b7c71d21` (2026-02-02, PR #135, "make shadow branches worktree-specific"):

```go
func ShadowBranchNameForCommit(baseCommit string) string {
	if len(baseCommit) &gt;= ShadowBranchHashLength {
		return ShadowBranchPrefix + baseCommit[:ShadowBranchHashLength]
	}
	return ShadowBranchPrefix + baseCommit
}
```

That bare form shipped in **v0.3.0 – v0.3.9** (2026-01-03 → 2026-01-30); the suffix first shipped in **v0.3.10** (2026-02-05). The optional `(-[0-9a-fA-F]{6})?` group was added deliberately in the same PR (`0854bd7ba`, review-comment fix: *"Updated regex to match both old and new formats"*). **No migration has ever existed** — `manual_commit_migration.go:108-109` is a *base-commit* migration and computes the suffixed name on both sides, so an old-format branch is never renamed or adopted. There was also no CHANGELOG entry.

So the premise is sound. The conclusion drawn from it is not. `cleanup.go:128-135`:

&gt; *"Excluding it from auto-delete eligibility does not orphan cleanup of those, though: nothing in this codebase can ever protect a bare-format branch … so a genuine old-format Entire branch has been unconditionally eligible for automatic deletion on every push … **restricting auto-delete here does not change whether they get caught**"*

That is a non-sequitur, and false. The premise ("never protected → always eligible") establishes that pre-PR they **were** caught and deleted on the next push. Post-PR they never are. The restriction changes exactly the thing the comment says it doesn't.

The saving grace — which the PR *body* states correctly but the *comment* buries under the false sentence — is that two user-initiated paths still use the broad pattern and still reach them:
- `entire clean --all` → `ListAllItems` (`cleanup.go:412`) → `DeleteShadowBranches` (`cleanup.go:360`), confirmed unless `--force`
- `entire disable` uninstall → `removeAllShadowBranches` (`setup.go:2920`) → same broad list

**Practical impact is low.** `CleanupPushedShadowBranches` itself only landed 2026-05-19 (`e1a0ce0c1`), so any repo that has pushed through Entire since then already had its old-format branches swept. The residue is repos last used with a January-2026 build that haven't pushed since May. Not a merge blocker on its own — but the comment must not tell the next maintainer that no cleanup gap was introduced.

**Fix:** replace `cleanup.go:133-135` with the honest version — auto-deletion of old-format branches stops here; they remain reachable via `entire clean --all` and `entire disable`; that trade is accepted because the shape can't be distinguished from a user branch.

## 2. The `HashWorktreeID` claim: TRUE, verified

`checkpoint/ephemeral.go:52-55` is `sha256` → `hex.EncodeToString(...)[:6]`, which is total and always 6 hex chars. `sha256("")[:6]` = `e3b0c4` (computed). `ShadowBranchNameForCommit` (`ephemeral.go:721-728`) appends `"-" + worktreeHash` **unconditionally** — there is no early return, so no "main worktree, no suffix" branch exists. The main-worktree case is what the repo's own fixtures already encode (`content_overlap_test.go` passes `e3b0c4` throughout). The safety argument holds.

One nuance the comment overstates by calling the shape the "exact output shape": `ShadowBranchNameForCommit` falls through to `commitPart = baseCommit` when the base commit is shorter than 7 chars, emitting e.g. `entire/abc-e3b0c4`, which the strict pattern rejects. Not a regression — the old pattern rejected it too — and unreachable with real SHAs. Worth a word, not a change.

- **Against current `origin/main` (`fce049cd3`): clean.** I performed a real merge in my worktree — ort strategy, no conflicts, 7 unrelated files from main.
- **vs #2199 ("Fix stale lock cleanup"): conflicts.** Pure add/add — both insert a new top-level `var` block immediately after `shadowBranchPattern`. #2230 adds `autoDeletableShadowBranchPattern` (`cleanup.go:88`); #2199 adds `cleanupLockDirs` + `errCleanupLockHeld`. Resolution is "keep both". #2199 leaves every shadow-branch symbol untouched and only appends lock items to `ListAllItems`, so there is **no semantic conflict**.
- **vs #2190 (gitrepo worktree-metadata consolidation): clean.** Its `cleanup.go` footprint is two hunks in `redactCacheDir`/`deleteRedactCache`, 350+ lines away; all eight shadow-branch symbols untouched.

PR state is **OPEN**, `mergeable: MERGEABLE`, `mergeStateStatus: BLOCKED` (checks/review gate, not a conflict).

## 5. Tests: both directions covered, but half the change is dead weight

The positive direction **is** asserted — `cleanup_pushed_shadow_test.go:164-168` requires `deleted == 1` and `require.False(env.branchExists(realShadow))`, so a legitimately suffixed orphan is still deleted. Good; that's the direction most reviewers would find missing.

But I mutation-tested both gates independently:

| Mutation | Result |
|---|---|
| Remove only the `continue` filter at `cleanup.go:256` | **PASS** — test does not notice |
| Also revert `cleanup.go:291` to `IsShadowBranch` | **FAIL** — `expected: 1, actual: 2` (original bug reproduced) |

So the test is a genuine regression test for the bug, but it exercises **only** the `DeleteShadowBranchesIfUnchanged` gate. The `CleanupPushedShadowBranches` filter — one of the two production changes — is unasserted. Its unique contribution is real (without it the human branch lands in `failed` and fires the `"some shadow branches failed to delete"` WARN on **every push**, forever), and nothing tests that. The PR body discloses this honestly but treats it as proof the defense-in-depth works rather than as a coverage gap.

Also missing: no predicate table for `isAutoDeletableShadowBranch` (compare `IsShadowBranch`, which has a 24-case table at `clean_test.go:17`), and no test that `DeleteShadowBranchesIfUnchanged` now routes an unsuffixed branch into `failed` — a behavior change on an exported function.

**Suggested:** assert `failed` is empty (or that no WARN-worthy failure occurred) in `TestCleanupPushedShadowBranches_HumanBranch_Survives`, which would kill mutation 1.

## 6. Scope and conventions

Scope is tight — two files, nothing beyond the title. Conventions are met: the test reuses `newShadowCleanupEnv` (`testutil.InitRepo` + `t.TempDir` + `t.Chdir`), never touches the real repo CWD, and correctly omits `t.Parallel()` because `t.Chdir` is process-global — consistent with every other test in the file. The `git.PlainOpen` at `cleanup_pushed_shadow_test.go:31` is pre-existing, not this PR's.

One style note: **~63 lines of comment for ~13 lines of code**, with editorializing that doesn't survive scrutiny ("unspoofable-in-practice", "positive-enough proof of Entire ownership"). The name-shape check is a heuristic; a real ownership check would inspect the branch's commit (committer `Entire CLI`, or the checkpoint trailer). Worth trimming to what a maintainer needs, and worth saying plainly that this is a heuristic rather than proof.

## Correctness of the targeting

## VERDICT: MERGE AS IS — the fix is correct and complete for `findInaccessiblePlugin`; two follow-ups (one of them a live sibling ACE elsewhere) should be separate PRs, not blockers.

---

### 1. Does the fix close it? Yes — and the predicate is exactly the right one

`/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/.claude/worktrees/agent-a6aa0cfcd10cc6652/cmd/entire/cli/plugin.go:147` — `if dir == "" || !filepath.IsAbs(dir)`.

This is **predicate parity with `LookPath` itself**, which is the strongest form this fix could take. From `$GOROOT/src/os/exec/lp_unix.go:66-72` the ErrDot trigger is `path := filepath.Join(dir, file); if !filepath.IsAbs(path)`. Windows uses the same test (`lp_windows.go:188`). Because `filename` here is `"entire-"+name` and `validatePluginName` (`cmd/entire/cli/plugin_store.go:164-166`) already rejects `/` and `\`, `IsAbs(Join(dir, file)) == IsAbs(dir)` for every non-empty `dir` — so the filter admits exactly the set LookPath would have resolved without ErrDot. Not an approximation.

Measured shapes (probe run on darwin, plus Go source for Windows):

| entry | `IsAbs(dir)` | LookPath verdict | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| `""` | false | `""`→`"."` → ErrDot | skipped ✓ (probe: `ok=false`) |
| `.` / `./` / `./bin` / `bin` | false | ErrDot | skipped ✓ (probe: `ok=false` for all three) |
| `~/bin` | false | ErrDot (Go never expands `~`) | skipped ✓ — and it could never have legitimately run anyway |
| `C:foo` (Windows drive-relative) | false | `Join`→`C:entire-x`, non-abs → ErrDot | skipped ✓ parity |
| `\bin` (Windows drive-rooted) | false | non-abs → ErrDot | skipped ✓ parity |
| `\\host\share` (UNC) | **true** on Windows | absolute, resolves normally | scanned ✓ parity, no new exposure |
| `/a/../b` | true | absolute | scanned ✓ |

Only theoretical divergence: Windows reserved device names (`filepath.IsAbs("NUL")` is `true` via `isReservedName`, while `IsAbs("NUL\\entire-x")` is `false`). Inert — `os.Stat` can't succeed on a path under a device name.

The PR's stated mechanism also checks out against the toolchain: `$GOROOT/src/os/exec/exec.go` `Command()` re-applies LookPath only `if filepath.Base(name) == name`, so a separator-bearing path genuinely skips the ErrDot re-check.

**Residual behaviour, verified not to be a hole.** When a relative entry ErrDots *and* a later absolute entry has the same name, the fallback returns the absolute one:
- absolute copy non-executable → returned, `runPlugin` fails to exec → "Failed to run plugin". Planted binary not touched.
- absolute copy executable → returned and run. That is the trusted binary from the absolute dir, **never** the planted one. I confirmed the returned path in both cases.

### 2. The second hole you asked about: still there, but not exploitable

`plugin.go:114-116` still returns `(p, args[1:], true)` with no `isAgentProtocolBinary(p)` guard — unchanged by this PR. It is **unreachable in the ACE sense**: `isPluginCandidate` → `validatePluginName` rejects any name starting with `agent-` (`plugin_store.go:161-163`), so `binName` can't be `entire-agent-*` on the reachable path.

The one way past it is case folding on a case-insensitive FS, and I confirmed that leak lives on the **LookPath success path too**, so it is pre-existing on main and not something this PR made worse: with a real `entire-agent-foo` (0755) on an absolute PATH dir, `resolvePlugin(["Agent-foo"])` returns `.../entire-Agent-foo` with `ok=true` on macOS — `validatePluginName`'s `agent-` check and `isAgentProtocolBinary`'s `strings.HasPrefix` are both case-sensitive, APFS is not. That runs a binary the user installed in a trusted dir with plugin argv/env: a reserved-name bypass, **not** ACE.

Follow-up (cheap, separate PR): add the guard to the fallback branch for symmetry, and case-fold both prefix checks.

### 3. A genuine SECOND ACE of the same class the PR does not touch — `agent/external/discovery.go`

This is the finding worth acting on. `cmd/entire/cli/agent/external/discovery.go:154-158` globs `filepath.Join(dir, "entire-agent-*")` over **every** `$PATH` entry, relative ones included, and `registerExternalAgent` → `external.New` → `cmd/entire/cli/agent/external/external.go:445` runs `exec.CommandContext(ctx, e.binaryPath, "info")`. `binaryPath` is a relative path containing a separator, so `exec.Command`'s ErrDot re-check does not fire — the identical bypass this PR fixes, one directory over.

Reproduced, not inferred: with `PATH="bin"`, cwd = temp dir, and a planted 0755 `bin/entire-agent-probez`, a bare call to `external.DiscoverAndRegisterAlways(ctx)` executed it (marker file written; the earlier run also showed its stdout being parsed, failing only on protocol version). Reachable from `entire enable`/setup, which use the **ungated** `DiscoverAndRegisterAlways` (`cmd/entire/cli/setup.go:211,404,475,498,881,2440`), and from hooks/explain/resume/attach/review via the `external_agents`-gated variant.

Recommend a follow-up PR extracting one "absolute PATH entries only" helper and using it in both places.

### 4. Legitimate behaviour

Preserved where it matters: `TestResolvePlugin_NonExecutableSurfacesAsLaunchError` (`plugin_test.go:146-166`) uses an absolute `t.TempDir()` and still passes.

Deliberately dropped for relative entries — confirmed by probe: a non-executable `bin/entire-probee` with `PATH="bin"` now gives `ok=false` (Cobra "unknown command") instead of "Failed to run plugin". Correct: even *with* the exec bit that file could never have been legitimately run.

Two consequences worth a line each, neither blocking:
- `docs/architecture/external-commands.md` rule 4 ("Found-but-not-executable surfaces as a launch error") now has an undocumented exception. One sentence would fix it, and CLAUDE.md asks for docs to track behaviour.
- **The exec bit is still never checked** (`plugin.go:151-154` only tests `err != nil || info.IsDir()`). That is now slightly more consequential: the function named `findInaccessiblePlugin` can return an *accessible* binary and cause it to be executed (the case-B path above). Benign, since the entry is absolute and user-trusted, but adding `info.Mode()&amp;0o111 == 0` would make the name true again.
