<result>## Verdict

**NEEDS CHANGES — cannot merge as is.** It fails lint (and CI), the transcript guard it adds is dead code, and the 50MB cap does not actually bound memory on three of the four paths a checkpoint blob can arrive on.

---

### 1. Merge cleanliness — clean

`git merge-tree --write-tree origin/main pr2229` exits 0 with no conflict messages (tree `622b4f7b2`). GitHub agrees: `mergeable: MERGEABLE`. `mergeStateStatus: BLOCKED` is the failing checks, not a conflict. One commit, `13bea3e33`, two files, +95/−10, no test files.

### 2. Build, lint, tests

| What I ran | Result |
|---|---|
| `go build ./...` | pass |
| `mise run lint` | **FAIL** |
| `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/...` | pass (4 pkgs) |
| `mise run test` (full unit) | pass — 10313 tests, 7 skipped, 196s |
| `mise run test:integration` | pass — 562 tests, 3 skipped, 76s |

The exact lint failure, reproduced locally and confirmed byte-for-byte in the PR's own CI log (run 33582762116):

```
cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/persistent.go:1474:18: directive `//nolint:nilerr // Keep committed prompt reads best-effort.` is unused for linter "nilerr" (nolintlint)
```

This is caused by the PR. On `origin/main` the function is `if err != nil { return "", nil }`, so `nilerr` fires and the directive is load-bearing. The PR inserts an `errors.Is` branch ahead of it (`persistent.go:1470-1473`), `nilerr` stops firing, and `nolintlint` (enabled, `.golangci.yaml:53`, `allow-unused` left at default false) errors on the now-unused directive. Fix is deleting the comment on line 1474. `Entire Gates` also fails.

### 3. Is the cap value correct and safe? The stated reasoning does not hold

The justification — "the same limit the store already writes transcripts under, so anything this store wrote stays readable" — is wrong in both directions.

**It doesn't bound memory.** `readBlobContents` calls `file.Reader()`, but laziness was decided earlier, inside `tree.File(path)`. `Blob.Reader()` is just `b.obj.Reader()` (go-git `plumbing/object/blob.go:92`), and `b.obj` was already chosen. Measured against the pinned go-git with the literal `Reader()` + `LimitReader(50MB+1)` sequence:

- **packed, non-delta** → `FSObject.Reader()` streams a bounded zlib reader over a `SectionReader` (`packfile/fsobject.go:108-167`). 512MB blob → 13MB RSS. **Cap works.**
- **packed, delta** → falls to `getMemoryObject` → `ApplyDelta` (`packfile/packfile.go:407-465`), base + patched + target all resident. A **14KB delta entry in a 432KB pack** produced **1,897MB RSS**. Cap contributes nothing, and the pack is entirely attacker-controlled — that is a delta bomb, strictly worse than the DoS being fixed.
- **loose object** → `getFromUnpacked` copies into a `MemoryObject` because `LargeObjectThreshold` is 0 in this repo's storage construction (`gitrepo/repository.go:169-175`). 512MB blob → 1,953MB RSS, all inside `tree.File()`.
- **`git cat-file` fallback** → `fetching_tree.go:336-345` takes `cmd.Output()`, unbounded, then copies it into a `MemoryObject`. This is tier 2 of `FetchingTree.File`'s normal resolution order (`fetching_tree.go:156-199`), not an exotic fallback — and it is precisely the fetched-from-a-remote case the PR's threat model names, because blobs pulled by `FetchBlobsByHash` land in a pack the already-open storer can't see.

At 5GB the extrapolations are ~19GB (loose), ~30GB (delta), ~10-15GB (cat-file). The one shape the cap defends is the one that was already cheap.

**And write-side "everything we wrote is under 50MB" is not established for the fields it caps.** `transcript.jsonl` genuinely is (`persistent.go:1144`, `&gt;`, matching the reader's `&gt;`), and `ChunkJSONL` chunks are ≤ maxSize. But:

- `prompt.txt` has **no write-side cap at all**. `lifecycle.go:596-614` appends every turn's raw prompt with `"\n\n---\n\n"` separators, and `RedactedJoinedPrompts` (`checkpoint/prompts.go`) truncates nothing. It grows monotonically for the life of a session.
- Gemini's `ChunkTranscript` (`geminicli/gemini.go:350`) appends a single oversized message without splitting and without erroring, so it can emit a chunk larger than `maxSize`. `ChunkJSONL` at least errors loudly on that case (`agent/chunking.go:88`).

So a cap that is "too low silently breaks legitimate reads" is a live risk on the prompt path, and there is no override — `maxCheckpointBlobBytes` is a compile-time const with no flag or env escape hatch, so a user with one legitimately oversized blob has no recovery.

The right fix is a size check **before** materialization. `FetchingTree.blobsOnDisk` already shells out to `git cat-file --batch-check` (`fetching_tree.go:313`), whose output is `&lt;oid&gt; &lt;type&gt; &lt;size&gt;` — the size is already in hand, one subprocess per tree. Note go-git's own `EncodedObjectSize` is not a substitute: for a packed delta it routes through `GetByOffset` and materializes the object to answer.

### 4. Behaviour change on hitting the cap — mixed, and one is wrong

| Surface | Path | New behaviour | Judgement |
|---|---|---|---|
| `checkpoint explain &lt;id&gt;` | `explain.go:885-888` | hard fail | Right. Single-item drill-down; truncating a transcript silently is worse. |
| `checkpoint list` | `readCommittedInfoFromCheckpointTree`, only callers `persistent.go:1620` / `refs_store.go:531` | degrades to defaults | Right, and correctly reasoned in the PR. |
| `tokens profile` | `tokens_profile.go:154-157` | **hard aborts the entire scan** | **Wrong.** `store.Read` → `readSummaryFromCheckpointTree` now returns an error, and this enumerates up to `--limit` checkpoints. One bad legacy blob kills the whole report. This directly contradicts the PR's own comment at `persistent.go:1646-1652` reasoning that enumerations must not fail wholesale — the same principle, opposite outcome, because only one of the two enumerating readers was considered. |
| `session attach` | `attach.go:479-481` | hard error | Questionable — this is a "does this checkpoint contain session X" probe; failing it aborts attach over an unrelated session's blob. |
| `session resume` | `resume.go:381-388` | debug log + `continue` | Fine. |
| `checkpoint tokens` | `checkpoint_tokens.go:172-178` | warning counter + `continue` | Fine. |

There is also a routing hazard that makes "fail loudly" configuration-dependent. `firstResolved` (`routing_store.go:79-88`) returns a non-final store's result only if `err == nil`; an error falls through to the next store. Under a git-refs primary the read order is `{refs, branch}` (`routing_store.go:63`), so `ErrCheckpointBlobTooLarge` from the refs store is **discarded** and the branch store's `(nil, nil)` is normalized to not-found. The user gets "checkpoint not found" for a blob the PR intended to shout about. On a git-branch primary the same read order is `{branch}` alone and the error does surface — so the two backends behave differently.

### 5. Completeness — the admitted gap does undermine the fix

The PR lists ephemeral.go ×2, migrate.go ×1, persistent.go ×5 as follow-up. That count is accurate (`ephemeral.go:678,686`; `migrate.go:222`; `persistent.go:1696, 1971, 2798, 2820, 2827`). Two of those omissions are not separable:

**The transcript guard is dead code.** `readTranscriptFromTree` still uses `file.Contents()` at `persistent.go:2798` (chunk reads), `:2820` (base file) and `:2827` (legacy filename). It therefore can never return `ErrCheckpointBlobTooLarge`, so the new branch at `persistent.go:1515-1517` is unreachable today. The transcript is the single largest and most attacker-controlled blob — the thing the PR summary explicitly claims to cap — and it is entirely uncapped. Worse, even a per-blob cap wouldn't fix it: `readTranscriptFromTree` reads *N* chunks and `ReassembleTranscript` concatenates them into one buffer, so the aggregate stays unbounded regardless.

**The one function the PR reasons about most carefully is self-defeating.** In `readCommittedInfoFromCheckpointTree` the new comment (`persistent.go:1649-1652`) asserts "The read itself is still bounded, which is what stops the memory-exhaustion DoS." Five lines later the loop calls `readCommittedMetadataFromCheckpointTree`, which reads one **uncapped** `metadata.json` per session (`persistent.go:1696`). The claim in the comment is false as written.

Also missing from the follow-up list entirely: **13 uncapped `.Contents()` calls in `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/`**, several reading the same attacker-controlled `entire/checkpoints/v1` trees — e.g. `strategy/common.go:1279` reads `prompt.txt` out of a committed checkpoint tree, `common.go:1579` reads a task transcript.

### 6. Scope and conventions

- **No tests.** The PR defers the oversized-blob test as "exactly the kind of CPU-heavy operation worth avoiding." That does not survive contact with the repo: `checkpoint/subagent_transcript_test.go:38` already allocates `agent.MaxChunkSize+1` bytes **in this very package**, with `t.Parallel()`, and `checkpoint_api_reader_test.go:292` is the direct precedent — an oversized-payload test for the analogous HTTP cap, which the PR cites as its model. Both run in CI today without trouble. `t.Parallel()` / isolated-temp-repo conventions are moot only because nothing was written.
- Error wrapping is correct throughout — `%w` on every path, lowercase messages, `errors.Is` rather than `==`.
- One in-scope behaviour addition beyond the title: a new `logging.Warn` at `persistent.go:1518` on transcript read failure. It does not fire on the normal no-transcript path (`readTranscriptFromTree` returns `nil, nil` there, `persistent.go:2830`), only on reassembly failure, which was previously swallowed. That is an improvement; worth keeping.
- Minor: the comment block at `fetching_tree.go:19-50` is ~30 lines of justification for a const, a var and a 12-line function, and parts of it are the claims shown above to be false.

### What would make this mergeable

Delete the stale `//nolint` on `persistent.go:1474` (blocking); reclassify `tokens_profile.go:154` to skip-and-count like its sibling at `:206`; either route `readTranscriptFromTree` through `readBlobContents` with an aggregate chunk budget or drop the unreachable guard at `:1515-1517` and the transcript claim from the description; fix the false comment at `:1649-1652` or cap `:1696` alongside it; and add the oversized-blob test the package already has a template for. The memory claim needs a pre-materialization size check via `cat-file --batch-check` before it is true in the commit-message sense — until then this is a bounded-string-into-`json.Unmarshal` hardening with a typed error, which is worth having but is not the DoS fix it is described as.

<result>## VERDICT: NEEDS CHANGES — the resolver is good and unusually well tested; the guard that justifies landing it alone does not work.

Everything below marked **[proved]** I ran; **[reasoned]** I only read. Worktree clean, nothing pushed, no stash used. Scratch trees: `/private/tmp/claude-501/.../scratchpad/pr` (PR) and `.../scratchpad/mainrepo` (main).

## 1. Duplication: it adds a parallel implementation **[proved, git grep on both refs]**

| | main | PR |
|---|---|---|
| `gitdir:` pointer parsers | 3 | **4** |
| `commondir` file readers | 1 | **2** |
| `rev-parse` git-dir/common-dir sites (non-test) | 15 | 15 |
| common-dir memoization caches | 2 | 2 |
| internal callers of the legacy unexported pair | 4 | **0** |

`metadata.go:62` `resolveWorktreeGitDir` is a **fifth-by-your-count / fourth-by-actual-count** parser sitting *alongside* `repository.go:235` `resolveDotGitPath`, which survives untouched and is kept alive solely for three Codex call sites (`agent/codex/hook_root.go:80`, `:84`, `:140`).

Three corrections to your priors: `gitdir/gitdir.go` is **not** a parser (it only shells out; its `gitdir:` hits are error-message prefixes — the package name plus a colon). `gitrepo/status.go` is **not** a parser (`status.go:183` is a bare `Lstat` existence probe). You missed a real third one: **`cmd/entire/cli/status.go:631` `resolveWorktreeBranch`**, a full independent `gitdir: ` parser including the relative case. Your two caches are `checkpoint/git_common_dir.go:26-28` and `gitdir/gitdir.go:45-50` — `session/state.go:843` is a doc comment on a one-line delegation, not a cache.

**Two parsers now in the same package disagree on two contracts:**
- Strictness — `metadata.go:91` requires exactly `"gitdir: "`; `repository.go:251` strips `"gitdir:"` then `TrimSpace`s.
- Missing-`commondir` sentinel — `metadata.go:110` returns `gitDirPath`; `repository.go:265` returns `""`. Opposite sentinels for one condition.

**Standing-alone judgment: temporarily worse on the metric the PR is named after** (4 parsers, 2 commondir readers), neutral on rev-parse sites and caches, better only on `repository.go`'s internal cohesion (threading `WorktreeMetadata` instead of re-resolving twice). That is an acceptable strangler-fig step **only** if the guard actually prevents 4→5 — and it does not (§2). The strictness divergence is genuinely in the new parser's favour, see §3.

## 2. `metadata_guard_test.go` is inverted — it misses the real patterns and blocks legitimate code **[proved, 8 probes run]**

Control probe (inline `strings.TrimPrefix(x, "gitdir: ")` in `cmd/entire/cli/`) → **caught** at `metadata_guard_test.go:123`. Then five plausible bypasses, all **PASSED**:

| Bypass | Why it slips through |
|---|---|
| Hoist `"gitdir: "` / `"commondir"` to a package-level `const` | `:96` inspects `fn.Body` only; a package-scope `GenDecl` is never visited |
| `var args = []string{"rev-parse","--git-common-dir"}` at package scope | same defect, `:106` |
| `git rev-parse --absolute-git-dir` | `:106` literal-matches only `"--git-common-dir"`; `--absolute-git-dir`, `--git-dir`, `--show-toplevel`, `--git-path` unchecked |
| `.git` literal in caller, `os.Stat` in a 1-line callee | `hasDotGitReference` (`:95`) is computed per function |
| `os.OpenRoot(filepath.Join(root, ".git"))` | verb list `:236` omits `OpenRoot` — notable, this repo has an `osroot` package and its own `rootbase_guard_test.go` |

The const bypass is the damning one: the guard **already** solves exactly this problem for `.git` (`:81-82` collects string-const identifiers, then hardcodes `dotGitIdentifiers["gitDir"] = true`) — so the author knew the pattern and applied it to one token but not the other two. And `repository.go:21` is literally `const gitDir = ".git"`, i.e. **this codebase's own house style is the bypass.**

Scope: `cliRoot` (`:52`) is `filepath.Dir(filepath.Dir(thisFile))` = `cmd/entire/cli`. Dropping the *identical* control probe into `internal/probepkg/` → **PASSED**. Nothing in `internal/`, `e2e/`, `tools/`, `api/`, `perf/`, `redact/` is policed.

**False positives, also proved.** Both of these are blocked at `:146`:
- `func f(gitDir string) { os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(gitDir, "HEAD")) }` — reading HEAD is not metadata traversal; the *parameter name* alone trips the hardcoded `"gitDir"` at `:82`.
- A function that mentions `".git"` only to **skip** it while walking, and reads `.entire/settings.json` — any function whose body mentions `.git` anywhere and calls any of the 7 verbs on anything is flagged.

`strategy` and `checkpoint` read files under a `gitDir` parameter constantly. Parts 2-4 migrate exactly those packages, so this fires where it will be least welcome, while the const-hoisted duplicate walks past.

**What does earn its keep:** `assertGuardLedgerSeen` (`:331`) fails when a documented exception disappears, so the four ledgers are a self-maintaining migration worklist — and all 10 `allowedCommonDirQueries` entries map 1:1 onto real rev-parse sites. Keep that. Fix the detectors.

## 3. Correctness across real layouts — clean, with one deliberate narrowing **[proved, 13 layouts, real git 2.50.1, run on both refs]**

Byte-identical and correct on both refs, verified end-to-end (commit → tree → blob **contents**): plain, linked worktree, linked worktree with relative `commondir`, submodule worktree, `--separate-git-dir`, `--shared` clone (**invariant (a) holds** — blob read through the alternate), **reftable** (**invariant (b) holds** — CLI storer, HEAD resolves), reftable linked worktree, **sha256**.

**Pointer strictness is a real win.** Across 7 pointer spellings, the PR's `OpenPath` agrees with real `git rev-parse` **7/7**; main agreed **3/7** — main accepted `gitdir:X` (no space), leading whitespace, tab-after-colon and trailing spaces, all of which git rejects with `invalid gitfile format`. Worth calling out in the PR description; it is the strongest single argument for the new parser.

**Three main-OK → PR-error changes, one root cause: a git dir is no longer accepted as a worktree root.**
- `OpenPath("&lt;repo&gt;/.git")` — now `has no worktree metadata and is not a bare repository`
- `OpenPath("&lt;repo&gt;/.git/worktrees/&lt;name&gt;")` — same
- bare repo with `core.bare` unset — `repository.go:151` requires `config.Core.IsBare`

The first two are **intentional and pinned** by the new `TestOpenPath_SeparatesBareRepositoriesFromWorktreeMetadata/separate_Git_directory_is_not_a_bare_repository`, and defensible (opening a git dir as a worktree root yields a repo whose worktree *is* the git dir). I traced all 14 non-test `OpenPath`/`OpenCurrent` call sites: **none passes a git dir**, and the only one taking a semi-arbitrary directory (`checkpoint/remote/util.go:513`) swallows the error (`repo = nil`) and degrades. So no live breakage — but this is an `OpenPath` **contract** change in a PR whose stated scope is "no consumers migrated," and it belongs in the description.

The `core.bare`-unset case I would push back on: it is stricter than git itself (git accepts a directory with `HEAD`+`objects`+`refs` regardless of the config key), and no stated motive.

## 4. Consumer-free? Mostly yes — measured, not assumed

- **git subprocesses per open: 0 → 0, unchanged** **[proved]** — emptied `PATH`, opens still succeed across all 8 real layouts; only reftable HEAD reads need git, identically on both refs.
- **Cost** **[proved, benchmark]** — plain 94.5µs → 102.7µs (+9%), linked 119.6µs → 129.7µs (+8%). The resolver does ~5 stat-family calls for a plain repo (vs 2) and ~11 for a linked worktree (vs 1 stat + 2 reads), because `metadataDirectoriesIdentifySameFile` costs 2 `os.Stat`s per call and runs 2-3×. ~10µs absolute — irrelevant against this repo's ~3s hook p50 floor. Not a blocker; worth knowing before parts 2-4 multiply the call count.
- **Error types preserved** **[proved]** — the `fs.ErrNotExist` contract that `settings/opf_command_trust.go:284` branches on to return "not versioned" survives **5/5** cases identically (it works via the double-`%w` in the `ErrWorktreeMetadataNotFound` wrap — fragile-by-luck, worth a comment at `metadata.go:69`).
- **Error strings all changed**, and **no consumer matches on them** **[proved by grep]**.

## 5. Test quality — the strongest part of the PR, with 5 uncovered branches

Real repos, not fixtures. The oracle is the right call: `assertMetadataMatchesGit` (`metadata_test.go:510`) cross-checks `GitDir` against `git rev-parse --absolute-git-dir` and `CommonDir` against `git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir`, compared with `os.SameFile`. That rules out a whole class of "my parser agrees with itself" bugs. Layouts are the ones that actually occur: `.bare`-worktrees, bare-backed linked worktree, `--separate-git-dir`, ordinary + linked submodules, submodule inside a linked superproject, moved worktree, symlink-aliased common dir / registration / root, symlinked `.git`. Hygiene correct: `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` → temp file, `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM=/dev/null`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, repo-local identity, `commit.gpgsign=false`. (`gitrepo` cannot use `testutil.InitRepo` — `testutil` imports `gitrepo`, so it would cycle. The hand-rolled helper is correct, not a shortcut.)

Zero new `git.PlainOpen`/`git.Open` outside `gitrepo` (both new `PlainOpen` calls are inside `repository.go`). `t.Parallel()` everywhere except the three tests using `t.Chdir`/`t.Setenv` — exactly CLAUDE.md's rule. <result># Review: PR #2241 @ `572177c3e`

**Verdict: NEEDS CHANGES** — one proven guard false positive that lands squarely in `strategy` (the package part 2 migrates), and one proven behaviour regression in `OpenPath`. Both are cheap to fix. Nothing here is architecturally wrong.

Nothing I list below duplicates your already-verified set. **PROVED** = I ran it; **READ** = source only.

## 1. The guard (638 lines, previously unreviewed)

### Critical

**[`metadata_guard_test.go:585-589`] The identifier fallback aliases by NAME, not value — and `strategy` is the package it misfires in. PROVED.**

When the per-file isolated type check can't resolve an identifier, `containsSeen` falls back to `r.packageValues[value.Name]`. Package `strategy` declares `gitDir = ".git"` at `common.go:361`. So *any* unresolved identifier spelled `gitDir` in that package is treated as the literal `".git"`.

Controlled A/B, three probes, one variable each:

| Probe | Flagged? |
|---|---|
| `os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(s.gitDir, "shallow"))`, type in **another file**, package `strategy` | **YES — false positive** |
| identical shape, package `checkpoint` (no `gitDir` const) | no |
| identical shape, type in the **same file** (type check resolves the selector) | no |

Failure scenario: part 2 adds a struct field named `gitDir` — the obvious name — to any `strategy` type declared in a different file from its methods. Every read under it fails the `gitrepo` build. The contributor's only outs are renaming the field or adding a bogus allowlist entry, and the error message ("independently inspects a .git entry") points at neither cause.

The two packages that can trigger this today are `gitrepo` and `strategy`. Those are exactly the packages this split touches.

Fix: parse all files of a package and pass them to `config.Check` together, so cross-file resolution actually works and the name-based `packageValues` fallback can be deleted outright. That also removes the special-case machinery in `collectGuardPackageStringValues`.

### Important

**[`metadata_guard_test.go:372-383, 166, 575-579`] Large false-negative surface. 7/7 probes bypassed the guard. PROVED.**

Each of these reaches `.git` or git metadata and is **not** flagged:

| Bypass | Why it slips |
|---|---|
| `os.ReadFile(root + "/.git/HEAD")` | literal is `"/.git/HEAD"`, not `".git"` |
| `os.Lstat(filepath.Join(root, ".git/config"))` | literal is `".git/config"` |
| `os.Getenv("GIT_DIR")` / `GIT_COMMON_DIR` | env vars unguarded entirely |
| `git rev-parse --git-path objects/info/alternates` | `--git-path` not in the flag list |
| `git worktree list --porcelain` | no `rev-parse`, no flag match |
| `osfs.New(filepath.Join(root, ".git"))` | `New` not in the selector list |
| `os.Lstat(fmt.Sprintf("%s/.git", root))` | no bare `".git"` literal |

The `.git/config` case is the sharp one: `filepath.Join(root, ".git", "config")` **is** flagged while `filepath.Join(root, ".git/config")` is not. Same operation, opposite verdicts, decided by comma placement. A contributor who hits the false positive will discover the second spelling and use it as the workaround — which is the worst outcome for a guard.

`os.Getenv("GIT_DIR")` is the most consequential gap: `repository.go:36-39` documents that git exports `GIT_DIR` to hooks and that reading it wrongly opened the wrong repository. That's the exact bug class the guard exists to prevent, and it's the one shape it can't see.

**[`metadata_guard_test.go:614-620`] Allowlist keys are ambiguous in two ways. PROVED.**

`guardSourceKey` is `&lt;path-with-cmd/entire/cli/-stripped&gt;:&lt;funcName&gt;` — no receiver, no package.

- A **method** named `resolveWorktreeBranch` added to `cmd/entire/cli/status.go` silently inherits the `status.go:resolveWorktreeBranch` exemption. Confirmed: not flagged.
- A repo-root `status.go` with a function `resolveWorktreeBranch` inherits the same exemption, because the prefix strip makes `cmd/entire/cli/status.go` and `status.go` collide. Confirmed: not flagged.

With 24 ledger entries today and more arriving in parts 2–4, an accidental exemption is a live risk. Include the receiver, and don't strip the path prefix.

**[`metadata_guard_test.go:151`] Bare token match over the whole function body. PROVED.** A function containing the literal `"commondir"` for any reason — a log line, a JSON key, a help string — is flagged as "independently parses Git metadata token". My probe was `fmt.Sprintf("checked %s", "commondir")`. Constrain the match to a parsing context, or accept that it will need ledger entries for non-parsing uses.

### Runtime cost — clean, measured

**PROVED.** The scan is ~0.7–1.0s of CPU, but full-package wall time is **unchanged**: merge base 2.73/2.52/2.56s vs head 2.27/2.26/2.55s (`-skip TestGitStatusCallSitesPassNoOptionalLocks`, 3 runs each). It runs under `t.Parallel()` and overlaps existing tests. No action needed — worth stating so nobody "optimizes" it later.

### Minor

- **[`metadata_guard_test.go:133`] Any unparseable `.go` file anywhere in the repo fails this package's tests.** PROVED — a WIP file in `strategy` yields `scan Go sources: collect package string values: …/zzprobe_broken.go:3:21: expected ')', found '{'`. It does name the file, so it's diagnosable; it's a nuisance, not a trap. Same for any untracked scratch `.go` file with a `.git` read.
- Build-tag-excluded files **are** scanned (`//go:build plan9` probe was flagged). PROVED. That's correct for a guard — noting it so it isn't "fixed".

### Praise

- The guard has its own tests (`TestGitMetadataGuardRecognizesBypassForms`, `TestGitMetadataGuardScansCompleteRepository` with a synthetic repo and an exact violation count). Guards that test themselves are rare and this one is well done.
- The two-way ledger — `assertGuardLedgerSeen` failing when a documented exception disappears — is the right design; it prevents the allowlist from silently rotting.
- Using `runtime.Caller(0)` to find the repo root is strictly better than the pre-existing `gitstatus_guard_test.go:43`, which shells `git rev-parse` and therefore can't run outside a git checkout.

## 2. Mutation testing `metadata.go` — the four prior survivors

All four **still survive**. Verdicts differ:

| Branch | Status | Matters? |
|---|---|---|
| ENOENT re-check `metadata.go:66-68` | SURVIVED | **No.** Sole caller already ran `requireMetadataDirectory` on the identical path at :38. Reachable only if the root vanishes between :38 and the `Lstat` at :64. Affects error text, not outcome. |
| Non-regular `.git` entry `metadata.go:83-85` | SURVIVED | **Yes — the one real gap.** |
| WorktreeID sanity check `metadata.go:158-160` | SURVIVED | **No.** `filepath.Base` never returns `""`, and `gitDirPath` is always absolute here, so only the `== "/"` disjunct is live — requiring a `/commondir` at the filesystem root. Defence against a state the code above cannot produce. |
| reftable `continue` `reftable.go:56` | SURVIVED | **Yes — silent perf cliff.** |

**[`metadata.go:83-85`] Deleting this branch makes `OpenPath` hang forever. PROVED** with a watchdog probe:

```
PRISTINE:  fifo .git → ".git entry at … is neither a directory nor a regular file"
M2 MUTANT: fifo .git → *** HUNG (no return after 4s) ***
```

Same for a symlink to a FIFO and to `/dev/zero`. A `.git` that is a FIFO, socket, or device reaches `os.ReadFile` at :87 and blocks with no writer. `OpenPath` is on the git-hook critical path with no timeout around it. Note the coupling: the symlink-follow branch at :74-79 is what *creates* this state from a symlink, and M5 dies precisely because :83 catches its damage. Low likelihood, severe and silent consequence — this branch deserves a test.

**[`reftable.go:56`] The `continue` is the difference between 300µs and 25ms per ref op. PROVED**, 100× `repo.Branches()`:

| | pristine | mutant |
|---|---|---|
| no stray entry | 268µs | 268µs |
| stray regular file `.git/reftable` | 372µs | **25.5ms** |
| dangling symlink `.git/reftable` | 312µs | **22.4ms** |

A single stray non-directory named `reftable` would route every ref read through a `git` subprocess — ~50-80× slower, no error, no log line. The comment above it explicitly justifies `Lstat` over `Stat`; nothing exercises it. Given this repo's documented hook-latency sensitivity, worth a test.

**Uncovered blocks in `metadata.go` (count 0, `-covermode=set`):** 34-36, 66-68, 71-73, 83-85, 88-90, 112-114, 120-122, 137-139, 146-148, 150-152, 158-160, 190-192, 209-211, 212-214, 216-218, 219-221. Most are I/O-error arms and reasonably left alone; 83-85 is the one that isn't.

## 3. Real git layouts — 33 built, compared against `git rev-parse`

Rows 1–11 of the matrix are **byte-identical between merge base and head**. Plain, linked worktree (relative *and* rewritten-absolute `commondir`), submodule, `--separate-git-dir`, `--shared` clone, reftable, reftable + linked worktree, sha256, symlinked root, `..` and relative paths: all resolve, all match the git oracle under `os.SameFile`, WorktreeID correct on all seven linked-worktree variants (bare-hosted, separate-git-dir-hosted, submodule-hosted, reftable, moved, symlinked-parent) and correctly empty for main worktrees, submodules and separate-git-dir. **The alternates invariant holds** — a commit created in the source *after* a `clone --shared` resolved through the clone. **PROVED.**

### Critical

**[`repository.go:151`] `OpenPath` now rejects git directories that git itself accepts. Regression. PROVED both directions.**

Three inputs where **merge base succeeds and head fails**:

| Input | git's verdict | base | head |
|---|---|---|---|
| `&lt;repo&gt;/.git` passed as the root | `rev-parse --absolute-git-dir` OK | opens, `Head()` + `CommitObject` OK | **fails** |
| `clone --bare` with `core.bare` unset | `--git-dir`=`.`, `HEAD` OK | opens | **fails** |
| `clone --bare` with `core.bare=false` | `--git-dir`=`.` | opens | **fails** |

```
failed to open repository at &lt;repo&gt;/.git: worktree metadata: worktree Git metadata not found
at &lt;repo&gt;/.git/.git: …; bare repository validation: path &lt;repo&gt;/.git has no worktree
metadata and is not a bare repository
```

`ErrWorktreeMetadataNotFound` routes to `openBareRepository`, which requires `config.Core.IsBare`. Git's own `is_git_directory` test is HEAD + objects + refs; it never consults `core.bare`. This is stricter than git without being more correct.

**Blast radius is bounded** (verified): `paths.WorktreeRoot` runs `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`, which exits 128 inside a `.git` dir and in a bare repo, so `OpenCurrent` can never produce this argument. The only exposed path is explicit-path callers — `checkpoint/remote/util.go:561 openRepoAt(ctx, dir)`, reached from `:513` with a caller-supplied dir.

So: real, low severity, but it should be a deliberate decision, not a side effect. Either drop the `IsBare` requirement (letting go-git's `PlainOpen` verdict stand, as the base did) or match git's actual test. Right now the PR description says no consumer changes, and this is a consumer-visible contract change.

**This confirms your prior-round finding (a), and extends it**: it isn't only "a git directory is no longer accepted as a worktree root" — it's any git directory whose `core.bare` doesn't happen to be `true`.

### Important

**[`repository.go:141-156`] `openBareRepository` codifies a broken alternates path. PROVED.** `clone --shared --bare` opens on both trees, but a commit living only in the source object store is `object not found` — on **both**. So it's pre-existing, not introduced. But the base reached that state through an unnamed fallback, whereas head now takes `git.PlainOpen` deliberately, in a function named for the purpose. Given that the alternates invariant is the stated reason `gitrepo` exists (CLAUDE.md), a function that opts out of it by name should say so in a comment, or route through `openWorktreeRepository`-shaped storage with `GitDir == CommonDir == repoRoot`.

### Not a regression — head is *more* correct

**PROVED.** `.git` files spelled `gitdir:&lt;path&gt;` (no space) and `gitdir: &lt;path&gt; ` (trailing space) open on the base and fail on head. I checked git: **git rejects both too.** The base's `TrimSpace`-based parse accepted layouts the user's own git refuses; head's `CutPrefix("gitdir: ")` + `TrimRight("\r\n")` matches git exactly, while still tolerating CRLF, a missing trailing newline, and a trailing blank line — all of which git also tolerates. This is a fix. Worth calling out in the PR description so it isn't mistaken for the regression above.

### Adjacent, pre-existing, both trees

`git worktree add --relative-paths` (git ≥2.48, `worktree.useRelativePaths`) cannot be opened by either tree: `open repository storage: unknown extension: relativeworktrees`. Notably **`ResolveWorktreeMetadata` gets this layout entirely right** — GitDir, CommonDir and WorktreeID all match git. The failure is downstream, in go-git's extension approval, which `reftable.go:118-123` already widens for `refstorage` (and delegates for `objectformat`/`worktreeconfig`) but not this. Not this PR's job; worth an issue.

### Caller-facing hazard (unchanged by the PR, but newly exported)

`GitDir`/`CommonDir` are **lexical, not physical**. For a symlinked worktree root the resolver returns `&lt;tmp&gt;/sym-link/.git` where git returns `&lt;tmp&gt;/sym-target/.git` — a different directory *name*, not just `/var` vs `/private/var`. `os.SameFile` agrees in every case. The doc comment says "retain their lexical spelling", so it's intentional — but this is now an *exported* API about to acquire many consumers, and any of them comparing these paths as strings (against `git rev-parse` output, a stored path, or a map key) will mismatch. The doc comment should warn callers to compare with `os.SameFile`, not `==`.

---

## 4. Your three un-posted prior findings

- **(a) `OpenPath` no longer accepts a git directory — STILL STANDS, and is broader than stated.** See above; proven in both directions, and it's `core.bare`, not just the `.git`-as-root case.
- **(b) `core.bare` stricter than git — STILL STANDS, now with a motive gap and a proof.** Git accepts on HEAD+objects+refs; head requires the config key. No comment explains why. Two of my three failing inputs are ordinary `clone --bare` repos with one config key removed.
- **(c) CLAUDE.md not updated — STILL STANDS.** `CLAUDE.md:989-990` still reads "Key files: `gitrepo/repository.go` (open entry points) and `gitrepo/reftable.go` (`reftableStorer`)". The PR touches **no** docs at all (`git diff --stat ea1374202 572177c3e -- CLAUDE.md docs/` is empty). `metadata.go` is now the canonical owner, hosts a guard that can fail another contributor's build, and is invisible in the one document that section directs reviewers to. `AGENTS.md` is a symlink to it, so agents inherit the gap.

Related and worth folding into the same doc edit: the package now exports **two** metadata resolvers with **opposite sentinels** — `ResolveWorktreeMetadata` returns `CommonDir == GitDir` when there's no `commondir` file, while `ResolveCommonGitPath` returns `""`. `agent/codex/hook_root.go:80-84,140` still uses the legacy pair. Neither doc comment mentions the other. For a PR whose title is "centralize metadata resolution", that's the thing most worth writing down.

---

## 5. What `64f3ba8dc` itself introduced

**READ + PROVED (cost).** It moved `filepath.EvalSymlinks(gitDirPath)` in `resolveWorktreeID` from a fallback (only when the lexical check failed) to unconditional.

The motive is sound and now tested: a symlink planted inside `.git/worktrees/` pointing outside the common dir used to pass — `filepath.Dir(&lt;common&gt;/worktrees/escaped)` is `SameFile` with `registrationRoot` — and is now rejected. `TestResolveWorktreeMetadata_RejectsRegistrationSymlinkEscapingCommonDir` covers exactly that. Good change.

Two consequences to note, neither blocking:

- **Cost on the hook path.** `OpenPath` on a linked worktree: base ~117-131µs/op, head ~139-211µs/op (2000 iterations × 3). ~+20-40µs. Negligible against hook budgets — stating it so it's a measured fact rather than an open question.
- **WorktreeID spelling can now differ from git's registration name.** `id` is `Base(EvalSymlinks(gitDirPath))` rather than `Base(gitDirPath)`. If `.git/worktrees/foo` symlinks to `.git/worktrees/bar` (both inside, so the `!same` check passes), the ID becomes `bar` where git's registration name is `foo`. No consumer today — `ResolveWorktreeMetadata` has zero callers outside the package — but worth a test before part 2 wires `WorktreeID` into session state, where a mismatched ID would misroute checkpoints. `EvalSymlinks` failing now also fails resolution outright where the lexical path used to succeed.

---

## 6. Conventions — clean

**PROVED / READ.** No issues found:

- `t.Parallel()` on every test that can take it; the four that can't (`WhitespaceOnlyRoot` uses `t.Chdir`; `IgnoresGitEnvironment` and `StartsNoGitSubprocess` use `t.Setenv`) correctly omit it. Ordering in `StartsNoGitSubprocess` is right — the repo is initialised with the real git *before* `PATH` is narrowed to the fake.
- Isolation is **better** than the repo baseline: `runMetadataGit` (`metadata_test.go:628-640`) sets `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` to a temp file and `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM=/dev/null`, so it can't read the developer's real gitconfig. No `~/.config/entire` or real-`.git` access anywhere in the new tests.
- No new `git.PlainOpen`/`git.Open` outside `gitrepo`. The two added `PlainOpen` calls (`repository.go:123`, `:142`) are inside the package, and `:121-122` documents why the fallback is now safe.
- Error wrapping is consistent and `%w`-preserving. I verified specifically that the double-`%w` at `repository.go:94-99` keeps `errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist)` true for a non-repo directory — `settings/opf_command_trust.go:279-286` depends on that to mean "not versioned", and it still works. **PROVED**, both trees.

Two tests worth singling out: `IgnoresGitEnvironment` (sets `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE`/`GIT_COMMON_DIR` to a *different* repo and asserts they're ignored) and `StartsNoGitSubprocess` (a fake `git` on `PATH` that touches a marker file, asserting the marker never appears). Those pin the two properties that make this resolver worth having.

# Security review — PR #2241 (`refactor-git-metadata-owner`), head `572177c3e` vs merge base `ea1374202`

## Headline answer

**No CVE. No security advisory.** Nothing in shipped `main` or in this PR crosses a trust boundary that an unprivileged or remote attacker can reach. Every hostile shape I could find requires **local filesystem write access to the worktree root or the git directory** — and I proved that precondition is not removable by clone or fetch.

Specifically on your priority-1 hypothesis: **no, this PR does not incidentally fix a shipped vulnerability.** The symlink weakness that `64f3ba8dc` fixed lived only inside this PR's own history (`d3df6e287` → `64f3ba8dc`), never on `main`. Details in F3.

The PR *does* fix a real shipped defect class (F2, indefinite hang) — just not one that warrants an advisory. And it leaves one shipped defect unfixed that I'd hold merge for (F1).

**The reachability result that caps everything** (proven by execution): git refuses a `.git` path component in a tree at *any* depth, *any* case, *any* mode, including as a symlink, even through the lowest-level plumbing route:

```
update-index --cacheinfo 100644 &lt;oid&gt; sub/.git   -&gt; error: Invalid path 'sub/.git'
update-index --cacheinfo 100644 &lt;oid&gt; .git       -&gt; error: Invalid path '.git'
update-index --cacheinfo 100644 &lt;oid&gt; sub/.GIT   -&gt; error: Invalid path 'sub/.GIT'
update-index --cacheinfo 120000 &lt;oid&gt; sub/.git   -&gt; error: Invalid path 'sub/.git'
```

A FIFO mode (`010644`) normalized to a regular file on clone. A symlink to `/dev/zero` clones fine but cannot be named `.git`. So no hostile `.git` entry arrives over the network.

---

## F1 — Unbounded metadata read, with the payload echoed into the error. **SHIPPED + still present in the PR. Fix before merge.**

**Files.** Shipped: `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/gitrepo/repository.go:212` and `:234`. PR: `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/gitrepo/metadata.go:87` and `:119`, with the error formatting at `metadata.go:199`.

Both sides `os.ReadFile` the `.git` gitfile and `commondir` with **no size cap**. The PR's special-file branch (`metadata.go:84`) admits any *regular* file, so a sparse file sails straight through it.

**Proven by execution**, 64 MiB sparse file (≈0 bytes of actual disk for the attacker):

| | allocation | resulting error string |
|---|---|---|
| shipped `main` | **1501 MiB** | 64 MiB |
| PR head | **860 MiB** | **128 MiB** |

At 256 MiB the PR allocated **5489 MiB** and emitted a **1 GiB error string** — that run exhausted this machine's memory outright (`tail: failed to allocate memory`).

Two distinct problems: a ~13–23x memory amplification, and `metadata.go:199` formatting the entire attacker-controlled pointer into the error via `fmt.Errorf("inspect %s at %s: %w", label, path, err)`. That error reaches stderr and the logging path — which also makes it a newline-injection vector into logs, and it sits against the CLAUDE.md rule about not logging user content.

**This is not "matching git" — it exceeds git.** Real git has an explicit cap and refuses the same file:

```
fatal: too large to be a .git file: '/private/var/.../wt/.git'
```

**Attacker model.** Local write access to the worktree root or git dir. **Not** reachable by clone/fetch (see headline). On the git-hook critical path.

**Verdict: fix-before-merge, not a vulnerability.** Cap the read (git's own bound is the obvious precedent), and truncate the pointer before putting it in an error. Since the PR is establishing itself as the single owner of this parsing, this is exactly the right moment — and the PR already reduced the blast radius by half, so it is halfway there by accident.

---

## F2 — Indefinite hang on special-file `.git` / `commondir`. **SHIPPED defect that this PR fixes.** Not advisory-worthy.

**Proven by execution.** Shipped `main` `OpenPath` hangs forever (no timeout anywhere in the code) on all of:

| shape | shipped `main` | PR head | real git |
|---|---|---|---|
| FIFO `.git` | **hangs** | rejected instantly | rejects cleanly |
| symlink `.git` → FIFO | **hangs** | rejected instantly | — |
| symlink `.git` → `/dev/zero` | **hangs** | rejected instantly | — |
| socket `.git` | errors | rejected instantly | — |
| FIFO `.git/commondir` | **hangs** | rejected instantly | **also hangs** |
| symlink `commondir` → FIFO | **hangs** | rejected instantly | — |

The branch at `metadata.go:84` and `:116` is what closes these. It works, and for the `.git` case it brings the CLI in line with git, which main was *not*. Note the `commondir` FIFO case: **real git hangs too**, so main's behavior there was already git-equivalent.

**Verdict on main: not CVE-worthy, no advisory.** Local write access required, and the outcome is a wedged hook process, not code execution or data disclosure. Someone who can `mkfifo` your `.git` can also just delete it.

---

## F3 — The registration-symlink escape is **PR-only. It was never shipped.** ← your priority-1 question

**Proven by execution on both sides.** I checked out `d3df6e287` (pre-fix) and `572177c3e` (post-fix) and ran the same hostile shape — a symlink at `&lt;common&gt;/worktrees/escaped` pointing at an attacker directory outside the common dir:

- **pre-fix**: `ACCEPTED`, `WorktreeID="escaped"`, `GitDir` physically outside the common dir.
- **post-fix**: `REJECTED` — *"git directory …/worktrees/escaped is not an immediate child of …/worktrees"*.

Your reading of the mechanism is exactly right: the pre-fix code tried the lexical `filepath.Dir(gitDirPath)` first, and `filepath.Dir("&lt;common&gt;/worktrees/escaped")` is `SameFile` with the registration root regardless of where the symlink actually points. Making `EvalSymlinks` unconditional (`metadata.go:145`) closes it.

**Does the equivalent exist on shipped main? No — and this is the important part.** `resolveWorktreeID` is new code; there is no counterpart at the merge base. Main's worktree identity comes from a different function entirely, `paths.GetWorktreeID` (`/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/paths/worktree.go:14`), which is pure string surgery (`strings.Cut` on `".git/worktrees/"`) with no validation at all. I ran it against the same hostile shape: it returns `"escaped"`. So **main is strictly more permissive than the PR's pre-fix state** — it never validates the registration in the first place.

**So why is that still not a vulnerability on main?** Because worktree identity scopes **no isolation boundary today**. It feeds exactly one thing: the shadow-branch name, `entire/&lt;commit7&gt;-&lt;sha256(worktreeID)[:6]&gt;` via `checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit` (`cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/ephemeral.go:721`). Session state (`.git/entire-sessions/`) and checkpoint refs (`refs/entire/checkpoints/`) are **deliberately common-dir-wide and shared across every worktree** — `session/state.go:853-854` documents this explicitly. Worktree ID is a naming discriminator inside one shared object store that every worktree can already read and `git branch -D`. Forging it buys a different branch *name* in a store the attacker already fully controls.

**Verdict: not a vulnerability on either side. No advisory, no CVE, nothing to disclose.** The hardening is sound defense-in-depth for parts 2-4, and it is worth keeping precisely because parts 2-4 may promote this ID into something load-bearing.

---

## F4 — The special-file guard is genuine but **not complete**. Not a vulnerability; don't let the tests overclaim.

You asked whether the branch is complete. It is complete **for the two files the resolver itself opens**, and I verified that by inspection: every read in `metadata.go` is mode-gated, and `os.Stat`/`os.Lstat`/`EvalSymlinks` never block on a FIFO (only `open(2)` does).

It is **not** complete for the package. **Proven at PR head**, still hanging indefinitely:

- FIFO `.git/config` — **hangs**
- FIFO `.git/HEAD` — **hangs**

These block *inside* go-git / `osfs`, downstream of the resolver, so `metadata.go` structurally cannot guard them. (`packed-refs`, `index`, and `objects/info/alternates` as FIFOs all returned fine.) Plus the unbounded-size class in F1.

**Verdict: not a vulnerability** (same local-write precondition). But the guard should not be described, in comments or tests, as a DoS mitigation for repository opening — it closes the blocking-open class for two specific files. If DoS-hardening is actually a goal, the bound has to sit at the `osfs`/go-git boundary, not in the resolver.

---

## F5 — Lexical `GitDir`/`CommonDir`: **no current exploit, real prospective hazard.**

**Proven by execution.** For a worktree root reached through a symlink:

```
entire GitDir  (lexical) = /var/.../elsewhere/aliasrepo/.git
git --absolute-git-dir   = /private/var/.../realrepo/.git
os.SameFile(...)                                        = true
strings.HasPrefix(GitDir, realRepoRoot+sep)             = false   &lt;- containment on the REAL root FAILS
strings.HasPrefix(GitDir, unrelatedAliasParent+sep)     = true    &lt;- looks contained by an UNRELATED dir
```

That is both failure directions at once: a containment check against the true root says "outside", and a containment check against an unrelated directory says "inside".

**Is any existing caller fooled? No — this category is clean.** There is exactly one external consumer that string-compares a gitrepo-returned path: `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/codex/hook_root.go:149`

```go
return err == nil &amp;&amp; resolved == common
```

and both sides pass through `canonicalPath` (`hook_root.go:268-276`), which does `filepath.EvalSymlinks`. It is not fooled. The repo also has a good central primitive (`paths.IsSubpath`) and a kernel-confined `osroot` for the cases that matter.

**Verdict: not a vulnerability. Fix-before-merge as documentation only.** This is about to become an exported API with many new consumers in parts 2-4, and the struct comment currently says only *"retain their lexical spelling"* — which reads as a formatting note, not a warning. Say plainly on `WorktreeMetadata` that these fields are **identity-comparable (`os.SameFile`) but NOT containment-comparable**, or export a resolved variant so the safe thing is also the easy thing. The cheapest durable fix.

---

## F6 — `WorktreeID` spelling on case-insensitive filesystems. Low; no new divergence.

**Proven on macOS APFS**: a pointer spelled `worktrees/LW` yields `WorktreeID="LW"` while the same physical worktree via `worktrees/lw` yields `"lw"` — two IDs for one worktree.

But I A/B'd the PR's ID against the shipped `paths.GetWorktreeID` across the shapes that matter, and **they agree everywhere normal**:

| shape | shipped `paths.GetWorktreeID` | PR `metadata.WorktreeID` |
|---|---|---|
| main worktree | `""` | `""` |
| linked worktree | `"lw"` | `"lw"` |
| submodule | `""` | `""` |
| escaping registration symlink | `"escaped"` | rejected (PR stricter) |

So swapping the implementation in part 2 will not churn shadow-branch names for real repositories, and the case quirk is pre-existing (string surgery has it too). Worst case is state *fragmentation*, not a crossed boundary — and per F3 there is no boundary to cross. **Not a vulnerability.** Worth one line in the PR description so part 2 doesn't rediscover it.

---

## Clean categories — stated as clean

**Pointer traversal (your priority 2): clean, and the PR is a net improvement.**

I traced every shape you asked about. The PR accepts an absolute or `..`-relative `gitdir:` naming a directory anywhere on disk — **and so does real git** (proven: both `gitdir: /abs/other/.git` and `gitdir: ../victim/.git` open the unrelated repo under git 2.50). Matching git is the defensible answer and it is what the PR does. There is no confinement, and none is appropriate.

The PR is **stricter than main and matches git** on two malformed forms:

| input | shipped `main` | PR | real git |
|---|---|---|---|
| `gitdir:&lt;path&gt;` (no space) | accepts | rejects | `fatal: invalid gitfile format` |
| `gitdir: &lt;path&gt;\nextra junk` | accepts (takes line 1) | rejects | rejects |

It is *more permissive* than git in one cosmetic way — `gitdir: /etc` passes the resolver (which only requires a directory) where git says `fatal: not a git repository` — but go-git rejects it downstream, so `OpenPath` still fails. The `..`-through-symlink reconciliation in `resolveMetadataDirectory` (`metadata.go:173-192`) behaves identically to git (both reject). No finding.

**`osroot` (your priority 2, second half): not using it here is not a gap.**

`osroot` is the right tool for operating *inside* an established root; `metadata.go` is the code that *establishes* the root. Confining anchor-resolution to the anchor it is resolving is circular. The `osroot` guard test (`cmd/entire/cli/osroot/rootbase_guard_test.go:18-40`) enumerates the approved anchors — `entiredir`, `gitdir`, `worktreedir`, `userdirs`, `agent.SessionStore` — and `gitrepo` is deliberately not among them; the guard's own rationale is that rooting at `filepath.Dir(target)` "enforces nothing that the join did not already decide," which is precisely what a root inside `metadata.go` would be. The correct `osroot` boundary is downstream in `cmd/entire/cli/gitdir/gitdir.go`, and it exists. The narrower, defensible ask is F1's size bound plus `O_NOFOLLOW` on the two reads — not `os.Root`.

**`GIT_DIR` / `GIT_COMMON_DIR` (your priority 5): clean, and the test genuinely proves it.**

Verified by inspection: `metadata.go` contains **no** `os.Getenv`, `os.LookupEnv`, `os.Environ`, or `exec.` — zero matches. So the resolver structurally cannot read the git environment.

Both tests are real proofs, not decoration:
- `TestResolveWorktreeMetadata_IgnoresGitEnvironment` (`metadata_test.go:246`) sets all three of `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR` at a *different* repo and asserts the result is the passed root's own `.git`. Combined with the grep above, that is airtight.
- `TestResolveWorktreeMetadata_StartsNoGitSubprocess` (`metadata_test.go:261`) puts a fake `git` on `PATH` that touches a marker file and asserts the marker does not exist. That genuinely proves no subprocess — a good test.

What an attacker controlling those variables gains: **nothing against the resolver.** They still steer `OpenCurrent`, because `paths.WorktreeRoot(ctx)` honors `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE` (documented at `repository.go:37-41`) and decides *which* root is handed in. That is unchanged by this PR and is git's own contract for hooks — an attacker who controls a hook's environment controls the hook. Not a finding.

**Per your note, I did not treat the AST guard's gaps as vulnerabilities.**

### Q1 — Sentinel disagreement: NOT a problem here. PROVED.

I reimplemented the deleted `resolveGitCommonDir` verbatim and diffed it against `repositoryDirs` across 14 real repos. The part-1 concern doesn't reach these call sites, because the deleted resolver was subprocess-based, not `ResolveCommonGitPath`. For a plain repo `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` returns relative `.git`, which the old code joined to the root — landing on exactly the `CommonDir == GitDir` value `ResolveWorktreeMetadata` returns.

Identical old/new: plain repo · linked worktree · linked worktree of a submodule · bare repo (both fail at `repo.Worktree()`) · broken `commondir` (both fail) · symlinked worktree root · `--separate-git-dir` · opened from a nested subdirectory · true linked-worktree hook env.

Two genuine divergences, both improvements:

**1. Canceled context.** Old returned `resolve git common dir: context canceled`; new succeeds. This is the exact hazard `refs_store.go:199-210` was written around ("A ref that misses the queue is never pushed... it stays local-only forever"). The failure mode is now structurally impossible rather than mitigated by a cache warm-up.

**2. `GIT_DIR` contamination — a real latent bug, now fixed.** The deleted resolver ran `exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "-C", root, "rev-parse", ...)` with the **inherited** environment and no `cmd.Env`. `cmd/entire/cli/gitrepo/env.go:8-13` documents precisely why that is wrong: git exports `GIT_DIR` to its hooks and it *outranks* `-C`. The sibling resolver `strategy/manual_commit_session.go:483` already sets `cmd.Env = gitrepo.EnvWithoutRepoOverrides()`; checkpoint's was the one that didn't. Proved: with `GIT_DIR=repoA/.git` and the repo opened at `repoB`, old returned **`repoA/.git`**, new returns `repoB/.git`. On the hook path that meant repo A's `post-commit` could write repo B's push-discovery queue and redact cache into repo A's common dir. Not exploited in practice (hooks normally run in their own repo), but it was live.

---

### Q2 — Deleted cache is a large net win. PROVED with numbers.

`BenchmarkResolveVariants`, Apple M4 Max, 300 iters:

```
new_filesystem/main               34,061 ns/op   (0 subprocesses)
new_filesystem/linked             76,312 ns/op   (0 subprocesses)
old_subprocess_cachemiss/main 10,825,719 ns/op
old_subprocess_cachemiss/linked 10,877,935 ns/op
```

Break-even vs. the old *first* call is **~318 calls** (main) / **~142** (linked). Actual calls, measured by instrumenting `repositoryDirs` with an atomic counter and running real writes:

- shadow-branch `store.Write` (Stop-hook path): **2 calls** per checkpoint
- git-refs backend `store.Write`: **2 calls** per checkpoint

So per hook process: **~68 µs (new) vs ~10.8 ms (old)** — a ~99.4% reduction on this step, and one fewer process spawn. The PR's own `TestPushQueueForOpenRepositoryRunsNoGitMetadataSubprocess` pins the zero-subprocess property with a positive control (it re-runs git to prove the Trace2 detector actually works), which is the right way to write that test.

---

### Q3 — cwd vs explicit root: clean.

`repositoryDirs` derives the root from `worktree.Filesystem().Root()` — byte-identical to what the old code passed as `git -C &lt;root&gt;`, same source, same line of code, just relocated. No call site changed which repository it names. Confirmed empirically from a nested subdirectory (`SAME`) and in a linked worktree (`SAME`). No new `os.Getwd()` derivation anywhere in the diff.

---

### Q4 — Duplication: downward, but only by one, and part 1's regression is untouched. PROVED.

Counts I verified myself with `git grep` at both revisions:

| Axis | before `63a0232c3` | after `09dae63c0` |
|---|---|---|
| Files shelling out for `--git-common-dir` | 10 | **8** |
| `gitdir:` pointer parsers (non-test) | 4 | **4** |
| `commondir` file readers (non-test) | 2 | **2** |
| `ResolveWorktreeMetadata` non-test callers | 1 | **2** |

Plainly: **the trajectory is downward, by exactly one resolver.** The `checkpoint` package is now completely free of `--git-common-dir` (it was the only consumer that shelled out without the env scrub, so the one removed was the worst one). But part 1's parser-count regression — 3→4 `gitdir:` parsers, 1→2 `commondir` readers — is **not** addressed here and remains outstanding; those retire in the session / strategy / remaining-consumer splits per the guard ledger's own reasons.

**The removed guard-ledger entry is genuinely obsolete — PROVED, and removal was mandatory.** I re-added the line and ran the guard:

```
--- FAIL: TestGitMetadataTraversalHasCanonicalOwner
    metadata_guard_test.go:80: documented git metadata query
    checkpoint/git_common_dir.go:resolveGitCommonDir --git-common-dir
    (checkpoint migrates in the checkpoint split) no longer exists;
    remove or update the exception
```

The guard is bidirectional (`assertMetadataQueryLedgerSeen`), so a stale entry is a hard failure. This was not removed to silence anything — leaving it would have broken CI.

---

### Q5 — Error paths changed in the right direction.

- `writeCheckpoint` / `writeTask` (`ephemeral.go:108`, `:310`): still **fail closed** on metadata error, same as before, now pinned by `TestWriteCheckpointFailsClosedOnMetadataError`. Shadow-branch writes behave sanely.
- `repoRedactCache` (`redact_cache.go:148`): still **fails open** (nil → full redaction, no write failure). Same policy, and it now also survives a canceled context instead of silently disabling itself.
- `PushQueueForRepo` (`pushqueue.go:69`): still fails closed, and now **wraps** the error (`"resolve git common dir for push queue"`) where it previously returned it bare. Better.
- Nothing that previously degraded gracefully now hard-fails. Nothing that previously hard-failed now degrades silently.
- One asymmetry worth noting and dismissing: `ResolveWorktreeMetadata` validates more strictly than `git rev-parse` (it rejects a `commondir` naming a non-`worktrees` parent). In production this cannot bite, because every repo reaches checkpoint through `gitrepo.OpenPath`, which already ran the identical validation at open time. I confirmed the strict rejection fires in `OpenPath`, not in checkpoint.

---

### Q7 — Conventions: clean.

`t.Parallel()` on all three new tests that can take it; correctly omitted on `TestPushQueueForOpenRepositoryRunsNoGitMetadataSubprocess` with the reason stated (`t.Setenv`, process-global `GIT_TRACE2_EVENT`). Isolated temp repos via `testutil.InitRepo` throughout. No `worktree.Status()`. Errors wrapped with `%w`. Notably the new tests use `gitrepo.OpenPath` where the **deleted** test file used `git.PlainOpen` directly — the PR removes a CLAUDE.md violation as a side effect.

## Findings

### Important (should fix — one-line doc edit, non-blocking)

**`CLAUDE.md:1036`** — "Opening Repositories" still ends: *"Key files: `gitrepo/repository.go` (open entry points) and `gitrepo/reftable.go` (`reftableStorer`)."* `gitrepo/metadata.go` is missing, and after this PR it is the canonical owner used by **two** packages and backed by a test-failing guard ledger. Concrete failure: a contributor adds `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` in a new spot, follows CLAUDE.md's reviewer guidance (which only warns about `git.PlainOpen`), and gets an opaque red `TestGitMetadataTraversalHasCanonicalOwner` in `gitrepo` with no documented pointer to the ledger they must edit. Part 1's review already raised this; the PR touches `CLAUDE.md` anyway, so it was the cheapest possible moment to close it.

### Suggestions (consider)

**`cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/pushqueue.go:69` + `refs_store.go:212`** — `PushQueueForRepo(_ context.Context, ...)` now advertises cancellation-awareness it does not have, and `refs_store.go:212` still wraps a discarded value in `context.WithoutCancel(ctx)`. The comment at `refs_store.go:206-210` honestly admits the ceremony is now vestigial. Four callers (`checkpoint/migrate.go:74`, `strategy/manual_commit_push.go:455`, `strategy/unpushed_checkpoints.go:38`, `strategy/manual_commit_opf_refs.go:36`) would need touching to drop the param, which widens the split — so keeping it is defensible. Worth an explicit follow-up rather than leaving the signature lying.

**Residual duplication is now *work*, not parsing.** `gitrepo/repository.go:87` already computes the full `WorktreeMetadata` (GitDir, CommonDir, WorktreeID) inside `OpenPath`, uses it for alternates/reftable detection, and **discards it** — nothing is attached to the returned `*git.Repository`. So `checkpoint.repositoryDirs` re-derives per call what the open already knew. The natural end state for the split series is for `gitrepo` to hand back the metadata it resolved. Cheap now (34-76 µs), but it is the reason a per-call resolve is needed at all.

**Lost test property.** The deleted `TestResolveGitCommonDir_CachesPerWorktree` asserted "two distinct repos must never resolve to each other's common dir" — the cross-repo-confusion guard. There is no replacement. It is now structurally guaranteed (no cache to confuse), and `TestPushQueueForRepoUsesLinkedWorktreeCommonDir` covers the adjacent case, so this is a judgement call — but it was the cheapest test in the file. The other two deleted tests (`CachedValueServesCanceledContext`, `FailureIsNotCached`) are correctly retired: the first is moot with no `ctx` in the signature and is still covered end-to-end by the existing `TestGitRefsStore_EnqueuesAfterCancellationFollowingCAS`, and the second is superseded by the stronger `...ObservesRepair` test.

### Note (no action — verified inert)

**macOS `/var` vs `/private/var` spelling in the submodule case.** The only value divergence I found besides the two improvements: for a plain submodule the `.git` file holds a *relative* pointer, so the new resolver keeps the lexical `/var/...` spelling while `git rev-parse` canonicalized to `/private/var/...`. Same directory, different string. I checked whether it can matter and it cannot: the common dir is only used to build filesystem paths (redact cache dir, push-queue file, flock and ref-lock paths), where `open()` follows the symlink to the same inode. The only two common-dir *string equality* comparisons in the tree (`strategy/manual_commit_session.go:298` and `:454`) take both sides from `strategy`'s own resolver, which `EvalSymlinks`es deliberately ("Resolve both through the same probe so normalization matches"). No cross-resolver comparison exists. Linux is unaffected. Given this repo's history with `/var` vs `/private/var` in test assertions, it is worth knowing about, but it is not a defect in this PR.

### Praise

- Deleting a memoizing subprocess wrapper and coming out **~160x faster with fewer failure modes** is the rare refactor that is unambiguously better on every axis. The performance objection one would reflexively raise against dropping a cache is simply wrong here, and the PR's own no-subprocess test says so.
- `TestPushQueueForOpenRepositoryRunsNoGitMetadataSubprocess` includes a positive control that re-runs git and asserts the trace file *does* appear. That is what stops the test from silently becoming vacuous, and it is frequently omitted.
- `TestCheckpointMetadataResolutionPreservesCallerPoliciesAndObservesRepair` tests three properties in one flow — required-path fails closed, optional-path fails open, and a repair is observed without a cache to clear — and its comment names the direction the old cache got wrong. Well-aimed.
- The `refs_store.go` comment was updated rather than left to rot, and it is honest about the `WithoutCancel` now being belt-and-braces instead of pretending the old rationale still holds.

**Effort to address feedback:** ~5 minutes (one `CLAUDE.md` line). Everything else is a tracked follow-up for the later splits.</result>

## Bottom line

**No CVE, no advisory.** There is one real security-relevant defect in the area you pointed at, it is in **shipped** code (`main`, and equally at this PR's head), and it is **not attacker-reachable**: it needs a `GIT_DIR` that names a *different* repository than the one Entire opened, and nothing an untrusted remote can do sets an environment variable. Fix-forward, one line.

The PR itself introduces no vulnerability, weakens no privacy control, and rejects no legitimate repository layout. It also **narrows** the pre-existing defect on the git-refs backend. Points 2, 3 and 4 of your brief are clean, and I verified each by execution rather than reading.

## Finding 1 — `casUpdateRef` writes the checkpoint ref into the hook's repository, not the opened one

**Severity: Low. Shipped on main; untouched by this PR. Fix-forward, not an advisory.**

`/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/.claude/worktrees/agent-a99f9bb5e66d0c4a0/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/persistent_ref_update.go:48-50`

```go
cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "update-ref", refName.String(), newValue, oldValue)
cmd.Dir = repoRoot
cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "LC_ALL=C", "LANG=C")
```

`os.Environ()` carries `GIT_DIR`, which outranks `cmd.Dir` — the same rule `gitrepo/env.go` documents. This is the *writer*, and it is the sibling the PR did not touch: the PR deletes the contaminated **reader** (`checkpoint/git_common_dir.go:52`) and leaves the contaminated **writer**. The doc comment at `checkpoint/shadow_ref.go:47-49` ("repoRoot is used as cmd.Dir so the update targets the same repository as the rest of WriteTemporary (i.e. s.repo) regardless of the process cwd") is false in a hook environment.

**Proved by running,** at both `63a0232c3` and `09dae63c0`, with `GIT_DIR=repoA/.git` and the repo opened at `repoB`:

- *Without* shared objects: git refuses the ref write — `trying to write ref 'refs/heads/entire/50d7b1c-e3b0c4' with nonexistent object b84c5f90…` — `writeCheckpoint` returns an error and nothing lands in repo A. **Fail closed.**
- *With* repo A holding an `objects/info/alternates` link to repo B (a `clone --shared`/`--reference` shape): the write succeeds, the ref lands in **repo A** (`refs/heads/entire/8bce693-e3b0c4`, and with the v1 name `refs/heads/entire/checkpoints/v1`), and repo A's ordinary `git push origin entire/checkpoints/v1` — exactly what the git-branch pre-push path does at `strategy/manual_commit_push.go:114-119` — put repo B's transcript on **repo A's remote**. I read it back off the bare remote: `{"transcript":"SECRET-FROM-REPO-B-TRANSCRIPT"}`.

**Attacker model: there is no attacker.** Two preconditions, both requiring the user's own environment or filesystem:

1. `GIT_DIR` (or `GIT_WORK_TREE`) naming a different repository. **Measured on git 2.50.1:** main-worktree hooks get *no* `GIT_DIR` at all (only `GIT_INDEX_FILE=.git/index`, `GIT_PREFIX`, `GIT_AUTHOR_*`, `GIT_EDITOR`) — so the premise "git exports GIT_DIR to its hooks" holds only for **linked-worktree** hooks (`GIT_DIR=&lt;common&gt;/worktrees/&lt;id&gt;`, absolute) and for an explicit `git --git-dir=… --work-tree=…`. In the linked-worktree case `GIT_DIR` names *the same repository*, so the deleted resolver returned the correct answer anyway. I found exactly one legitimately-configured cross-repo export: a hook firing in a linked worktree while a git command targets a **submodule** — there `git -C sub rev-parse --git-common-dir` returned the *superproject's* `.git`, and `git -C sub update-ref refs/probe/x HEAD` wrote the ref into the superproject. But no Entire code path crosses that boundary: both the git-hook and agent-hook entry points resolve the repo from the invoking worktree (`paths.WorktreeRoot()` → `cmd/entire/cli/hook_registry.go:222`), and nothing on the checkpoint write path opens a submodule or a foreign repo. The multi-repo commands that *do* (`dispatch`, `session adopt`, `trail`) are user-invoked, with no hook env.
2. Repo A must be able to resolve repo B's objects, or git refuses the write outright.

Not reachable by cloning or fetching an untrusted repository: a remote cannot set env vars, cannot ship config, and git refuses to check out paths under `.git`. Reachable only with the user's own exported `GIT_DIR` or a user-authored hook that crosses repository boundaries — which, as you said, is not an attacker.

**Fix (one line, matching the already-scrubbed sibling at `checkpoint/ephemeral.go:1382`):**

```go
cmd.Env = append(gitrepo.EnvWithoutRepoOverrides(), "LC_ALL=C", "LANG=C")
```

Safe for linked worktrees: `refs/heads/*` lives in the common dir either way, and `cmd.Dir` rediscovers the same gitdir through the `.git` pointer file. `DeleteShadowBranch` (`checkpoint/ephemeral.go:710`) and the delete sites in `strategy/cleanup.go:312` / `strategy/common.go:1614` set no `cmd.Dir` at all, so they are cwd-intentional and consistent with `gitdir.CommonDir`'s documented "the hook's repo IS the target" policy — leave them.

**On 1(c), locks:** under the same contamination the shadow flock (`shadow_ref.go:84-90`), the persistent-ref flock (`persistent_ref_update.go:77-85`) and `tryDeleteLooseObject` (`shadow_ref.go:124-136`) land in the wrong `.git`. Consequences are availability and hygiene only: mutual exclusion degrades to the `casUpdateRef` CAS retry (16 attempts, the actual correctness guarantee), and the abandoned-commit cleanup misses, leaving an unreachable object until `git gc`. No cross-repo deletion in practice — the path is a content-addressed hash the other repo does not have — and every access is `os.Root`-scoped, so nothing escapes the directory it opened.

---

## Finding 2 (positive) — the PR strictly reduces exposure from Finding 1

Not a vulnerability; the security-relevant delta, and worth stating for the merge decision.

At the merge base, everything filesystem-side resolved to repo A while objects still went to repo B (go-git's storer is built from the *open-time* metadata at `gitrepo/repository.go:87`). I measured `commonDir`, the push queue and the redact cache all landing in `repoA/.git`, including `repoA/.git/entire-redact-cache` and `repoA/.git/entire-shadow-locks`. Ref *and* queue agreeing on repo A is precisely what the git-refs push needs: A holds the ref, A's queue names it, `partitionLocalRefs` keeps it, A pushes B's objects.

At the PR head I measured queue, cache and locks in `repoB/.git` (correct), with the ref still landing in A. A never learns of the ref; B has the queue entry but not the ref, so `partitionLocalRefs` prunes it as stale (`strategy/manual_commit_push.go:514-520`). **The git-refs exfiltration chain is broken** and degrades to fail-closed checkpoint loss. The git-branch v1 push is by branch name rather than queue-driven, so that variant is unchanged.

Two further improvements worth crediting:

- The checkpoint's `commonDir` now comes from the same pure function that built the repository's object and ref storage, so the filesystem bookkeeping **cannot** diverge from the storage. Under the deleted subprocess it could, and I demonstrated that it did.
- `PushQueueForRepo` is now context-free, so the post-CAS enqueue can no longer fail on a canceled context with a cold memo — the exact "a ref that misses the queue … stays local-only forever" window described at `checkpoint/refs_store.go:207-215`.
- The deleted allowlist row in `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/.claude/worktrees/agent-a99f9bb5e66d0c4a0/cmd/entire/cli/gitrepo/metadata_guard_test.go:35` is the guard doing its job: the exemption's stated reason was "checkpoint migrates in the checkpoint split", and the guard now forbids any checkpoint code from re-adding a `--git-common-dir` subprocess. That is a security control being tightened, not loosened.

---

## Finding 3 — Privacy controls: clean, verified by execution

**Not a vulnerability. No path yields less redaction after the change.**

`repoRedactCache` (`cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/redact_cache.go:148-154`) fails open to `nil`; `nil` short-circuits `reusePrefix`, so `redactIncrementally` runs the redactor over the **whole** content. I instrumented the redactor at the PR head and measured bytes actually scanned:

| case | redactor saw | secret in output |
|---|---|---|
| healthy, first pass | 2090 / 2090 | no |
| healthy, incremental | 33 / 2123 | no |
| broken `commondir` → cache nil | 2123 / 2123 | no |
| planted entry, stale `SourceHash` | 2123 / 2123 | no |

Fail-open means *more* redaction, confirmed. Cross-repo cache reuse (the merge-base contamination) also cannot under-redact: reuse requires `sha256(content[:SourceBytes]) == entry.SourceHash` **and** a matching `redactionFingerprint()`, so a foreign entry either describes the same bytes (identical output) or is discarded. Dropping `ctx` removes a cancellation-dependent fallback — a canceled context used to fail the subprocess and force a full redaction — but since the output is hash-gated it is a performance change, not a redaction change.

One pre-existing, unchanged property, for the record rather than as a finding: the cache directory is **integrity-trusted**. I planted an internally consistent entry whose `RedactedFile` held the raw text with a correct `SourceHash`, and the secret spliced straight into the output (33/2123 bytes scanned, secret present). That requires write access to the victim's `.git`, which already implies code execution via `hooks/` or `core.hooksPath` — strictly weaker than the capability it would grant. Not worth changing under this threat model; if you ever want defense in depth, record a digest of the *redacted* payload alongside the source hash.

Also merge-base-only and now fixed: repo B's redacted prefixes were physically written into `repoA/.git/entire-redact-cache`. Redacted, never pushed, local-only.

Non-finding consistency note: `entire clean` resolves the same directory through `session.GetGitCommonDir` / `gitdir.Open` (cwd plus inherited env, deliberately, per `cmd/entire/cli/gitdir/gitdir.go:76-81`), while the writer now resolves it from the worktree root ignoring env. Same inode in every normal configuration; they can only disagree in the contaminated case, and `clean` is user-invoked with no hook env.

---

## Finding 4 — New strictness as denial of service: clean

**No legitimate layout is newly rejected.** I built each layout for real and compared `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` (absolutized) against `gitrepo.ResolveWorktreeMetadata`:

plain repo, linked worktree, submodule, submodule inside a linked worktree, **linked worktree of a submodule**, `--separate-git-dir`, linked worktree of a `--separate-git-dir` repo, `git worktree move`, `worktree.useRelativePaths` via `worktree add --relative-paths`, worktree of a **relocated main repo after `git worktree repair`**, repo reached through a symlink, bare path.

Every one: identical result, or (for one hand-built shape) both refuse. The only rejection is a gitdir whose `commondir` names a non-`worktrees` parent — `git rev-parse` also refuses that with "not a git repository", so it is not a layout anyone has. My prime suspect, submodule-inside-a-linked-worktree, turns out to have no `commondir` file at all (`gitDir == commonDir` at `&lt;super&gt;/.git/worktrees/&lt;wt&gt;/modules/sub`), so the `worktrees`-parent check never runs. The bare-directory rejection (`ErrWorktreeMetadataNotFound`) cannot arise here because `repositoryDirs` calls `repo.Worktree()` first, which fails for a bare repo before the resolver is reached.

---

## Finding 5 — Lexical path spellings: clean for trust and containment

The divergence is real and I reproduced it: submodule (`/var/…` from the resolver vs git's `/private/var/…`), submodule inside a linked worktree, and `--relative-paths` worktrees. `os.SameFile` is true in every case. It appears exactly when the pointer file is *relative* and the caller's root spelling is non-canonical; where git wrote an absolute pointer, the resolver inherits git's canonical spelling.

I verified independently that no trust or containment decision consumes it:

- Every use of the returned commonDir goes through `gitdir.OpenAt` → `osroot.Shared(abs)` → an `os.Root`, and containment is evaluated relative to the *opened directory*, so both spellings yield the same root: push queue (`pushqueue.go:57-60`), shadow lock (`shadow_ref.go:84-90`), persistent-ref lock (`persistent_ref_update.go:77-85`), loose-object delete (`shadow_ref.go:124-136`), redact cache (`redact_cache.go:131`). The `filepath.Join(gitCommonDir, RedactCacheDirName)` at `redact_cache.go:139` is stored for error messages only.
- **Locks cannot split.** `flock` is taken on a file, i.e. inode identity, not string identity — two spellings of the same directory lock the same file.
- The only commonDir *string* comparisons in the tree are self-consistent and unreachable from the checkpoint resolver: `strategy/manual_commit_session.go:298` and `:454` take both sides from `gitCommonDirForWorktree` (env-scrubbed **and** `filepath.EvalSymlinks`-canonicalized, with a comment saying exactly why), and `cmd/entire/cli/trail_checkout_worktree.go:464` — the one genuine "path belongs to another repository" containment test — takes both sides from `git rev-parse`, normalized through `normalizeWorktreePath`. `gitrepo/repository.go:171,198` compares `metadata.CommonDir != metadata.GitDir`, both from a single call.
- Nothing persists a common-dir string anywhere, so there is no stored-versus-recomputed comparison to split.
- Structurally: `repositoryDirs` hands `ResolveWorktreeMetadata` the *same string* `gitrepo.OpenPath` handed it, so the checkpoint's spelling equals the storage's spelling by construction.

---

## Other categories, stated as clean

- **No new untrusted-input parsing.** The `.git`/`commondir` pointer parsing is part 1, already on main; the PR only adds a caller. It is bounded (16KiB cap matching git's own `4*PATH_MAX`), rejects a symlinked `commondir`, validates directories before use, and elides pointer-derived paths in errors. A malicious remote cannot plant either file: git refuses to write tree paths under `.git`.
- **Dropping the memo is not a TOCTOU issue.** Metadata is now re-read per call, so two calls in one hook could in principle observe different values — but that needs a mid-process rewrite of `.git/commondir`, i.e. write access to `.git`, and every consumer is `os.Root`-scoped.
- **Untouched:** auth, transport, tokens, the OPF re-redaction path, what gets committed or pushed. The PR adds no subprocess, no new write location, and no network call. Per your instruction I did not re-run lint or the suites, and I did not treat the `CLAUDE.md` wording as a finding — though for what it is worth the edited sentence is now accurate: the cache dir is resolved without caching from the explicit worktree root.</result>
