## Verdict: **NEEDS CHANGES**

The locking design *is* coherent as it now stands — the oscillation resolved correctly, and I proved the original defect is fixed. But the fix relocated the failure rather than eliminating it: the v1 pre-push path now aborts the user's `git push` on transient ref-lock contention, which is the exact thing commit `82b12d2e3` set out to prevent and which `e59d26b96` left unaddressed after reverting the wrong-layer mechanism.

---

## Q1 — Is the locking/waiting behaviour coherent? Yes. Here is what actually happens.

Two callers, cleanly separated (`grep` verified: no other callers).

**Checkpoint-ref path** — `updatePersistentRef` (`checkpoint/persistent_ref_update.go:145`), called only from `store.go:116`, `persistent.go:2126`, `refs_store.go:235`:
- Entire writer flock: **taken**, via `flock.AcquireContext`. Deadline-free ctx → blocks in the kernel indefinitely (`flock_unix.go:72-77`). With a deadline → polls at 25ms and returns `ctx.Err()`. This is the documented intent for turn-end condensation and is unchanged by this PR.
- Native git ref lock: `ErrRefLocked` and `ErrRefCASConflict` are both flattened to `ErrShadowRefBusy` (`persistent_ref_update.go:53-55`), then retried up to 16× **with a rebuild** each time.

**OPF pre-push path** — `CASPersistentRef` (`:102`), called only from `manual_commit_opf_rewrite.go:812` and `manual_commit_opf_refs.go:169`:
- Entire writer flock: **not taken at all**.
- Native git ref lock: `ErrRefLocked` retried 16× **without** rebuild; `ErrRefCASConflict` terminal on the first attempt.

**No contradiction between `462fd2d2c` and `e59d26b96`.** `462fd2d2c`/`82b12d2e3` made the OPF path wait on the writer flock; `e59d26b96` removed that. The removal is *right*, and no residue of the bounded wait survives: `expectedHash` is computed by the caller before `CASPersistentRef` is entered, so waiting for the flock cannot make a stale expected value fresh — after the wait you still get a CAS conflict. The flock was pure latency on that path. The final state is the correct one.

**But the asymmetry you named is inverted at the top.** The checkpoint-ref path correctly withholds a flush (`manual_commit_push.go:258-259`, `warnOPFCheckpointRefsWithheld` → `return false`, push proceeds). The v1 path does not — see Critical 1.

---

## Critical

**1. `strategy/manual_commit_push.go:170` — transient ref-lock contention aborts the user's `git push`.**

`RewriteUnpushedV1WithOPF` now surfaces `gitrepo.ErrRefLocked`, and line 170 returns it unconditionally → `hooks_git_cmd.go:324` → `:341` `return fmt.Errorf("pre-push: %w", hookErr)` → non-zero hook exit → git aborts the whole push batch.

This violates the contract written in that same file, `hooks_git_cmd.go:326-337`:

&gt; `PrePush` itself only returns errors for privacy-critical failures (OPF rewrite — e.g., `V1DivergedError`, `BootstrapTooLargeError`, `V1RefMovedError`, `OPFRuntimeFailedError`, `OPFNoCategoriesError`); **transient checkpoint-push failures are logged and swallowed before reaching this point.**

`ErrRefLocked` is transient and is not in that taxonomy. It is also **new to this PR** — at the merge base `atomicSetV1Ref` used go-git's `CheckAndSetReference`, which ignores native git's `.lock` protocol entirely and so could never produce a lock error.

**PROVED, two scenarios:**

- *Concurrent writer.* With a native `git update-ref` transaction prepared (lock held) on `refs/heads/entire/checkpoints/v1`, `RewriteUnpushedV1WithOPF` returns after ~2.6s:
  ```
  set v1 ref: update persistent ref refs/heads/entire/checkpoints/v1 after 16 lock attempts:
  ... cannot lock ref ...: File exists. ...: git reference lock is unavailable
  ```
- *Stale lock — permanent.* I planted an empty `.git/refs/heads/entire/checkpoints/v1.lock` with no owning process and got the identical error. Nothing in git or Entire removes such a file, so **every subsequent `git push` in that clone fails until a human deletes it.** At the merge base the same stale lock was invisible to go-git and the push succeeded.

There is a real second-order cause here worth stating plainly: this PR is what *creates* the contention. Before it, checkpoint writers used native git's lock while the v1 OPF path used go-git's flock-on-the-ref-file — two disjoint mechanisms that never blocked each other (which is precisely why the update was lost). Unifying them on native git's protocol is correct, but it means the v1 path must now handle contention gracefully, and it doesn't.

*Fix:* at `manual_commit_push.go:164-171`, branch on `errors.Is(rewriteErr, gitrepo.ErrRefLocked)` and fail soft — log, warn, skip the checkpoint push, `return nil` — matching `:256-260`. Keep `V1RefMovedError`, `V1DivergedError`, `BootstrapTooLargeError`, `OPFRuntimeFailedError` terminal. Aborting is fail-closed so no unredacted content escapes; this is a liveness/UX defect, not a privacy one.

**2. The merge into main needs edits in two files, and one of them git resolves silently into a broken build.**

Confirmed by trial merge from `origin/main` (`360124a07`): exactly one conflicted path, `checkpoint/persistent_ref_update.go`, and the conflict is confined to the import block. Cause is **PR #2220** (`8547d57fd`, "take the persistent-ref lock through the git common dir"), not #2236 — #2236 never touched this file. `8547d57fd` renamed `persistentRefLockPath` → `persistentRefLock` (returning `(*os.Root, string)` for `flock.AcquireContextIn`). It introduced **no** new locking, retry, or CAS behaviour, and `casUpdateRef`/`updatePersistentRef` on main are byte-identical to the merge base.

So the resolution is mechanical: drop both contested stdlib imports (`os/exec` from this PR, `path/filepath` from main), keep `strings`, `gitdir`, `gitrepo`, `flock`, `logging`, `osroot`. Every function body auto-merges correctly, and `CASPersistentRef` never touches the flock so main's hardening cannot interact with it.

**The trap:** this PR's new `TestCASPersistentRef_DoesNotWaitForPersistentRefFlock` sits in a region main didn't touch, so it auto-merges with **no conflict marker** and then fails to compile — `persistent_ref_update_test.go:67` still calls the function main deleted:
```
vet: cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/persistent_ref_update_test.go:67:19: undefined: persistentRefLockPath
```
Fixing the import block alone is not enough. Rewrite that call site the way main already did at `:196-198`, to `persistentRefLock` + `flock.AcquireIn`.

**3. The #2226 landmine still holds — re-verified against current heads.**

PR #2226 head is `fb689886` (2026-09-01; re-checked 2026-09-04). It adds a *second* `exec.` site to `checkpoint/persistent_ref_update.go` — a new `casDeleteRef` shelling out to `git update-ref -d` — while this PR removes the `os/exec` import. The two merge **clean in both directions** (`Automatic merge went well`, zero conflict markers) and then:
```
cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/persistent_ref_update.go:65:9: undefined: exec
```
Not order-dependent; identical merged tree either way. Restoring the `os/exec` import is the whole fix (verified: `go build ./...` and `go vet` clean afterwards). Worth flagging to whoever merges second that after the merge, ref *updates* go through `gitrepo.CompareAndSwapRef` while ref *deletes* string-match `"cannot lock ref"`/`"but expected"` on stderr — the exact fragility this PR's typed sentinels replaced.

---

## Important

**4. `gitrepo/ref_cas.go:18,107` — `WaitDelay` kills with SIGKILL, which leaves a permanent stale lock.**

`cmd.WaitDelay = refCASWaitDelay` (3s). Go's `WaitDelay` calls `Process.Kill()` → SIGKILL. **PROVED** on both backends:

| signal | leftover | next CAS |
|---|---|---|
| SIGTERM | none | exit 0 |
| SIGKILL (`files`) | `refs/entire/t.lock` | `fatal: ... File exists.` |
| SIGKILL (`reftable`) | `reftable/tables.list.lock` | `fatal: prepare: cannot lock references` |

Git's `lockfile.c` signal handler cleans up on SIGTERM but obviously not on SIGKILL. On reftable the orphan is `tables.list.lock`, which blocks **every ref write in the repo**, not just the target ref — and it feeds straight into Critical 1's permanent-push-failure path.

Reachability is narrow (git aborts promptly at EOF — see Clean below — so the 3s window needs something like a hanging `reference-transaction` hook), but the failure is permanent and repo-wide, and it is strictly worse than the unbounded hang `a1a6c2f46` was fixing. *Fix:* have `cmd.Cancel` close stdin **and** send `syscall.SIGTERM`, keeping `WaitDelay` only as a last resort. `TestPreparedRefCASCancellationReleasesGitLock` covers the clean-EOF path only; the kill path is untested.

**5. `checkpoint/persistent_ref_update_test.go:37-52` — stale precondition asserts nothing.**

`TestCASPersistentRef_StopsWaitingWhenContextDeadlineExpires` calls `holdPersistentRefLock` (`:41`), but `CASPersistentRef` no longer takes that flock. The test passes purely because the already-expired ctx makes `exec.CommandContext` fail immediately. It is leftover from the `82b12d2e3` design and now misleads a reader into thinking the OPF path waits on the writer flock — the opposite of what `:96-101` documents. Drop the `holdPersistentRefLock` call.

---

## Suggestions

- **`ref_cas.go:43-52`** — on a symbolic ref the returned error is `errors.Join(symbolicErr, tx.abort())`. If `abort` itself fails with a lock error, the joined error satisfies *both* `ErrRefSymbolic` and `ErrRefLocked`, so `retryPersistentRefLockContention:128` retries a permanent symref rejection 16×. Check `ErrRefSymbolic` before `ErrRefLocked`, or don't join.
- **`ref_cas.go:250-251`** — `A || B &amp;&amp; C` relies on precedence. Parenthesize.
- **`gitrepo/reftable.go:162`** — switching `gitPlumbingEnv()` to `EnvWithoutRepoOverrides()` also changes every `reftableStorer.execGit` call, beyond this PR's stated scope. It's the correct change, but it deserves a line in the PR description.

---

## Q2 — Does it still fix the original defect? Yes. **PROVED in both directions.**

I did not take the existing tests' word for it. The existing tests (`manual_commit_opf_cas_test.go:22,80`) assert *lock contention*, not the lost update, so they don't demonstrate the defect. I wrote a test that asserts the lost update directly and ran it at both revisions.

Mechanism, from go-git's source (`storage/filesystem/dotgit/dotgit_setref.go:21-54`): `setRefRwfs` opens the loose ref file `O_RDWR|O_CREATE`, takes a **billy advisory flock on that file**, reads/compares/truncates/writes. Native git never flocks the ref file — it writes `refs/….lock` and renames. **The two protocols do not interlock at all.**

At **merge base `ea1374202`** (native transaction prepared, lock held, then committed after the OPF write):
```
LOST UPDATE REPRODUCED: OPF reported success writing v1=3732dd85…,
but after the concurrent native update committed the ref is b5e27de7…
```
`opfErr` was `nil`. The OPF-redacted tip was discarded while the hook believed it stored it — so the pre-push proceeds and pushes the un-OPF'd content. That is #2204, and it is a privacy failure, not merely a bookkeeping one.

At **head `a1a6c2f46`**, 5/5 runs: no lost update. The rewrite fails with `ErrRefLocked`, the ref stays consistent, the push aborts. Fail-closed. The defect is genuinely fixed.

---

## Q3 — `gitrepo/ref_cas.go` as production code

**Error classification — clean, and stronger than I expected.** I probed git 2.50.1 directly on `files` and `reftable`, loose and packed:

| condition | `files` | `reftable` | classified |
|---|---|---|---|
| wrong non-zero old value | `is at X but expected Y` | *identical* | `ErrRefCASConflict` ✓ |
| same, packed-refs only | *identical* | *identical* | ✓ |
| missing ref, non-zero expected | `unable to resolve reference '&lt;name&gt;'` | *identical* | ✓ |
| create-only but exists | `reference already exists` | *identical* | ✓ |
| lock contention | `Unable to create '&lt;…&gt;.lock': File exists.` | `cannot lock references` | `ErrRefLocked` ✓ |
| namespace conflict | `cannot lock ref '&lt;a/b&gt;': '&lt;a&gt;' exists` | — | neither ✓ (own test) |

Both backends produce byte-identical text for the three conflict shapes, and the two distinct lock shapes are each matched by one arm of `isRefLockContention`. Conflict is checked before lock and the files-backend lock message doesn't match any conflict pattern, so the ordering is safe. Locale is pinned via `gitPlumbingEnv`'s `LC_ALL=C`/`LANG=C`. `3ea22da57`'s change from a substring to a ref-name-scoped `HasSuffix` correctly stops `reference broken` / `Permission denied` from being read as a stale value.

**Prepared-transaction protocol — clean and fail-safe.** I probed both abandonment states on git 2.50.1: EOF while `PREPARED` and EOF while `STARTED` both **abort**, exit 0, leave the ref untouched, leave no stale lock. Neither state commits. So the un-aborted `preparationError` path (`:150-156`) is safe.

**Cancellation — clean.** Because git exits 0 on that abort, `cmd.Wait()` returns nil; the `canceled atomic.Bool` (`:100-104`, `:188-190`) is what makes `wait()` still report `ctx.Err()`. That is load-bearing and correct. Cancelling does **not** leave a stale `.lock` on the normal path — see Important 4 for the SIGKILL path.

**Ref-name validation — clean.** `refName.Validate()` (go-git `plumbing/reference.go:219-266`) rejects `" \t\n"`, control sequences, `~^:?*[`, `\`, `..`, leading `.`, trailing `.lock`, per path component — so newline injection into the line-oriented stdin protocol is blocked. `TestCompareAndSwapRef_RejectsRefProtocolInjection` covers exactly that. The `symbolic-ref` probe additionally uses `--end-of-options` (`:213`), guarding the one place a ref name reaches argv. `HexSize()` returns 40 for a zero-value hash (measured), so the `strings.Repeat("0", …)` old-value construction cannot silently degrade CAS into an unconditional write.

---

## Q6 — Symbolic refs: the fix holds, and the race is genuinely closed. **PROVED.**

The earlier round's finding is real: I confirmed that `option no-deref` + `update &lt;symref&gt; &lt;new&gt; &lt;old&gt;` **succeeds and converts the symref to a hash ref** on both backends. So the guard is necessary.

`462fd2d2c`'s guard works, and the reason it is race-free is that the check sits *between* `prepare` and `commit`, and `prepare` holds the lock. Measured, with a transaction prepared on `refs/entire/t`:

- `files`: concurrent `git symbolic-ref` → exit 1, `cannot lock ref …: Unable to create '…t.lock': File exists.`
- `reftable`: concurrent `git symbolic-ref` → exit 1, `cannot lock references`

A concurrent writer therefore cannot change the ref type between the check and the write, in either direction (already-symbolic is caught by the probe; hash→symbolic is blocked by the lock). `TestPreparedRefCASPreventsConcurrentSymbolicConversion` asserts exactly this, parametrized over both backends, and the clobber is no longer asserted as intended — `TestCompareAndSwapRef_RejectsSymbolicRef` and `TestCASPersistentRef_RejectsSymbolicRef` assert the symref survives intact.

## Q8 — Conventions: clean

`t.Parallel()` is present everywhere it can be; the two omissions are correct — `TestCompareAndSwapRef_IgnoresInheritedRepoOverrides` uses `t.Setenv` (and says so), and the two OPF tests use `configureFakeOPF`, which mutates process-global redaction config. No `git.PlainOpen`/`git.Open` and no go-git `worktree.Status()` anywhere in the diff. Every new git subprocess targets via `cmd.Dir` and gets `gitPlumbingEnv()`, which now routes through `EnvWithoutRepoOverrides()` — and `TestCompareAndSwapRef_IgnoresInheritedRepoOverrides` proves a decoy `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE` cannot redirect the write. Errors are wrapped with `%w` throughout and the sentinels survive `errors.Is`.

## Regressions from earlier rounds

Only one thing an earlier round "fixed" is gone, and it was removed deliberately: `82b12d2e3`'s *"Let deadline-free OPF writers wait instead of failing pushes"* was reverted by `e59d26b96`. The revert of the **mechanism** is correct — waiting on the writer flock cannot rescue a stale `expectedHash`. But the **problem** that commit named is real, unaddressed, and is Critical 1. The fix simply belongs at `manual_commit_push.go:170`, not in the lock layer. Everything else from rounds 1–5 is intact: `ref_cas.go`'s prepared transaction and symref guard, the `ErrRefLocked` retry, clean cancellation via the `canceled` atomic, `EnvWithoutRepoOverrides`, the ref-name-scoped conflict matcher, and the unverified-conflict guard in `atomicSetV1Ref`.

One design detail I checked and found sound: `RewriteQueuedCheckpointRefsWithOPF` (`manual_commit_opf_refs.go:167-172`) can abort mid-loop leaving some refs OPF'd and some not, but `unappliedAncestry` skips commits already carrying the OPF trailer, so the next push is idempotent.

**Effort to address:** small. Critical 1 is a few lines at `manual_commit_push.go:164-171` plus a test; Important 4 is a two-line `cmd.Cancel`; Important 5 is a one-line deletion. The merge work (Critical 2 and 3) is three import/call-site edits but must not be improvised at merge time — and since **CI has never run on this PR**, my local runs above are the only signal.
